= Agent 365 Security PoC
:toc: left
:sectnums:

Deployment procedures, control tests, and evidence requirements

Revision: 1.0 - 18 September 2026

Use a dedicated test environment and synthetic data.

Approve costs, licences, policy scope, and data locations before execution.

Product availability and preview access depend on the selected tenant.

No live product result is included in this package.

== Agent 365 and security prerequisites

Agent 365 is an agent control plane. It integrates administration, identity, security, and compliance services. It does not replace hosting or orchestration. Commercial general availability began on 1 May 2026. Individual integrations can remain preview. [CL1, CL4]

These procedures describe proposed tests. They contain no tenant results. Use synthetic data and an authorized evaluation tenant. Do not treat inventory, telemetry, detection, and enforcement as equivalent.

=== Approve licenses, roles, geography, and cost

[options="header"]
|===
|Dependency |Decision before execution
|Agent 365 |Standalone or E7. Confirm per-user licensing and qualifying base subscriptions. [CL2, CL3]
|Enterprise prerequisite |FAQ lists E5, or E3/O365 E3 plus EMS E3 and both Defender and Purview suites. [CL3]
|Other customer segments |Check frontline, education, and Business Premium conditions separately. Do not reuse enterprise assumptions. [CL3]
|Copilot Studio and Foundry |Publishing capacity, Azure model usage, hosting, and Bot Service costs remain separate.
|Agent security transition |Agent-level Studio/Foundry security needs Agent 365 from 1 July 2026. [CD4]
|Entra and network |Agent CA requires Entra P1/P2 plus Agent 365. Network controls require Internet Access. [CE2, CN2]
|Work IQ MCP |Preview with Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing. BYO MCP has additional deployment limits. [CM4, CM4B]
|Endpoint |Verify MDE and Intune entitlements separately. Discovery and runtime prerequisites differ. [CP1, CP3]
|Sentinel and Copilot |Budget ingestion, lake storage, query compute, and SCUs. Included capacity does not include Sentinel lake costs. [CK1]
|Preview evaluation |The documented Agent 365 trial has 25 seats for 30 days. Frontier has separate conditions. [CD4, CX11]
|===

. Name the PoC sponsor, security owner, compliance owner, tenant administrator, and Azure subscription owner.
. Approve exact test users, agent identities, environments, subscriptions, endpoint groups, and external provider accounts.
. Check Billing > Licenses in Microsoft 365 admin center. Record assigned product licenses and expiration dates.
. License each person who manages, sponsors, or uses agents on their behalf. One licensed user enables the tenant only.
. Validate the commercial prerequisite combination with licensing staff. Do not infer entitlement from a visible portal page.
. Approve each preview separately. Confirm enrollment, user eligibility, and tenant rollout before scheduling demonstrations.
. Approve Agent 365, Defender, Purview, Sentinel, Azure, and provider processing geographies. Use synthetic-only content throughout.
. Set Azure budgets, model quotas, test duration, Sentinel ingestion limits, and Security Copilot capacity alerts.
. Record existing policy settings before changes. Approve rollback owners and a test-end date.
. Stop a test when required scope isolation, entitlement, telemetry, or approval is missing. Record the missing prerequisite.

The launch price was USD15 per user monthly. Use a current quote, not this historical price, for approval. [CL4]

Security Copilot inclusion rolls out by tenant. Verify allocated capacity before assuming E5/E7 inclusion. [CK1]

Do not promise sovereign-cloud parity. Local-agent discovery excludes sovereign and national clouds. [CL2, CP1]

Bedrock provider readiness and actual Agent 365 registry synchronization are both required. Registry synchronization remains preview and requires separate approval. [CI1, CX15]

A provider-readiness result must not be reported as registry-sync, telemetry, or Agent 365 enforcement evidence.

ONB-10 must demonstrate actual Bedrock registry synchronization. Missing approval or rollout leaves full requested PoC acceptance BLOCKED.

Preview detection scenarios are optional. Keep them BLOCKED when approval, rollout, or a matching safe fixture is missing.

=== Assign minimum roles

[options="header"]
|===
|Activity |Required operator or approval
|Agent governance and tool approval |AI Administrator; separately authorized consent approver when required. [CX01, CM4]
|Entra policy changes |Conditional Access Administrator; identity owner and security approval. [CX07]
|Defender cloud onboarding |Security Administrator and Power Platform Administrator for Studio. [CD1, CD5]
|Endpoint configuration |Approved elevated local administrator or Intune Policy and Profile Manager. [CP3]
|Compliance |Purview policy permissions; separate Audit, eDiscovery, and IRM roles for restricted evidence. [CU1]
|Foundry Purview |Foundry Account Owner and compliance approval. Older UI can show Azure AI Account Owner. [CU3]
|Azure security plans |Subscription role accepted by the selected plan. AI Services documentation specifies Owner or Contributor. [CC2]
|Sentinel |Workspace contributor/write access and tenant Security Administrator. Agent 365 connector requires Global Administrator. [CS3, CX08, CX14]
|===

Use temporary privileged activation where available. Reader access does not grant configuration or investigation authority.

Use Global Administrator only for a documented requirement. Record who approved tenant-wide changes.

=== Keep data handling and evidence separate

* Agent 365 observability uses the tenant default geography, with service-generated-data exceptions. ADR is not supported. [CI4]
* Defender Agent 365 stores EU/UK tenant data in the EU. Other tenants use US storage. [CR1]
* Receiving services apply their own data commitments. Approve each copied-data path independently. [CR2]
* Observability content retention is 30 days. Defender session data can last 30 days; inventory/shared security data, 180 days. [CI4, CR1]
* These periods do not constitute a compliance archive. Configure Purview and Sentinel retention separately.
* Capture agent ID, user ID, publication state, policy ID, mode, UTC time, and correlation identifiers.
* Remove credentials and tokens from screenshots and exports. Record evidence provenance and access restrictions.

Cleanup: restore only PoC changes. Preserve approved evidence and legal holds. Remove temporary access after the review.

== PoC scope and acceptance

This proof of concept (PoC) tests Agent 365 controls against named test agents.

The kit contains source code, infrastructure templates, procedures, test data, queries, and an evidence recorder.

No customer tenant result is included.

The customer must approve each deployment, policy change, external connection, and test scope.

=== Execution sequence

. Approve the scope, licences, roles, preview access, data locations, cost limit, and evidence location.
. Install the kit and complete its local checks.
. Prepare the dedicated identities, devices, provider accounts, synthetic files, and optional Azure resources.
. Deploy each agent through its platform chapter. Complete ONB-01 through ONB-10.
. Resolve any required onboarding blocker before claiming complete agent coverage.
. Apply the Microsoft 365 and Entra procedures. Run M365 and ENTRA cases.
. Apply the approved Defender and network procedures. Run DEF, MDE, MDCA, DFC, and GSA cases.
. Apply the Purview procedures and run the approved PUR cases.
. Connect the required investigation data. Run SENT and COP cases.
. Run RSP cases after the required identity, action, and incident evidence exists.
. Review acceptance and measured value with the customer.
. Complete final cleanup only after all dependent cases finish.

Restore temporary policy changes after each case.

Keep shared test agents and resources until every case that needs those objects is complete.

Destructive case cleanup belongs at that object's final use, not automatically after its first test.

Use separate evidence stores when the approved scope or catalogue changes.

Catalogue acceptance does not make the unsupported OpenAI connector available.

=== Required demonstrations

[options="header"]
|===
|Requested group |Test instance |Primary proof
|Microsoft AI Platform |CPS-01: Copilot Studio |Registration, identity, authenticated use, and supported controls.
|Microsoft AI Platform |FDR-01: Foundry hosted agent |Hosting, publication, identity, and explicit Agent 365 telemetry.
|Partner integrated |SAS-01: entitled partner agent |Deployment, provider authorization, and actual interaction evidence.
|SDK-enabled |SDK-01: custom runtime |Model execution, explicit instrumentation, and service-side telemetry.
|Connected platform |EXT-01: Amazon Bedrock |Provider inventory synchronization and supported management actions.
|Connected platform |EXT-02: Claude Managed Agents |Preview workspace connection and inventory synchronization.
|Connected platform |OpenAI connector gap |Record the unsupported connector request. Use a separate SDK demonstration.
|Shadow and local |SHD-01: approved test device |Local discovery and separately configured endpoint controls.
|===

The group names describe this PoC. They do not replace product classifications.

A missing feature remains a gap. Another integration does not prove the missing feature.

=== Approve the test plan

. Copy config\poc-plan.example.json to an approved location outside the repository.
. Replace each placeholder with the approved test value.
. Record the tenant, cloud, region, users, devices, resource groups, agent aliases, and owners.
. Record the approved cost limit and stop time.
. Review the required flag for each catalogue case.
. Approve optional preview tests separately.
. Set target times before execution. Do not use measured times as retrospective targets.
. Record the approval reference and the evidence reviewer.

Keep actual tenant identifiers and approval records outside the source repository.

If a required feature is absent, record BLOCKED. Do not change the result to PASS.

=== Apply acceptance rules

* Run both the positive phase and the negative phase for each case.
* Record the actual service decision and the actual action result.
* Keep policy mode, scope, identity, timestamps, and correlation evidence.
* Report required and optional cases separately.
* Report approved exclusions with their original and revised denominators.
* Keep local simulation results separate from live product results.
* Keep a blocked case in the original denominator.

A screenshot of a configured policy does not prove enforcement.

A model refusal does not prove that a security service blocked the request.

An empty query result does not prove that the agent performed no action.

== Prepare the test environment

=== Assign operators

[options="header"]
|===
|Operator |Responsibility
|PoC lead |Approve scope, sequence, cost, stop conditions, and acceptance.
|Microsoft 365 administrator |Check licences, agent inventory, deployment scope, and consent.
|Identity administrator |Check identity objects, permissions, Conditional Access, and access expiry.
|Power Platform administrator |Prepare the Copilot Studio environment and connector policies.
|Azure administrator |Approve resources, roles, networking, model quota, and cleanup.
|Security operator |Prepare Defender, endpoint, network, and Sentinel controls.
|Compliance operator |Prepare Purview labels, policies, search, retention, and evidence access.
|Evidence reviewer |Compare artifacts with criteria and approve the final result.
|===

A responsibility is not an Entra role name.

Use the product role required by the relevant procedure.

Activate elevated roles only for the approved operation.

=== Prepare isolation

. Use a dedicated evaluation tenant where possible.
. Create named test users, test groups, and test devices.
. Create a dedicated Power Platform environment and a dedicated Azure resource group.
. Use separate provider accounts or workspaces for external agents.
. Record all existing policy settings before changes.
. Check policy assignments for production users and devices.
. Stop if a change cannot remain within the approved scope.
. Use a disposable tenant for a documented tenant-wide blocking demonstration.

Do not connect production mailboxes, customer sites, or enterprise messaging to the test agents.

Do not disable a production protection to make a test succeed.

=== Prepare synthetic data

. Inspect each file in fixtures.
. Copy the public procedure and restricted record to the approved test location.
. Use the DOCX copies for Office and SharePoint sensitivity-label tests. Use the TXT copies for the custom runtime.
. Apply the test sensitivity label through the supported product procedure.
. Create separate allowed and denied test accounts.
. Share each test file only with the intended test identities.
. Record the access grants before agent execution.
. Use the untrusted instruction only in the isolated test.

The fixture text does not create a Microsoft Purview label. The supplied DOCX files are also unlabeled.

Keep labeled test copies outside the repository. Never overwrite the clean fixture with protected customer content.

The untrusted instruction is not a guaranteed detector trigger.

Use the official harmless demonstration when the endpoint procedure requires that demonstration.

=== Set stop conditions

* Stop if a test affects a production identity, device, resource, or agent.
* Stop if an agent reads real restricted data.
* Stop if the selected policy scope differs from the approved scope.
* Stop if a licence, consent, or required feature is absent.
* Stop at the approved cost limit or end time.
* Record the cause and preserve evidence before cleanup.

== Install and inspect the kit

=== Get the repository

. Install Git, Python 3.12, Node.js 24, and Azure CLI.
. Install Bicep 0.46.1 for the local infrastructure checks.
. Authenticate to GitHub with an account that can read the repository.
. Clone the repository.
. Open a PowerShell session in the repository root.
. Read the source files before execution.

[source]
----
git clone https://github.com/oguzhanf/agent365-security-poc.git
Set-Location .\agent365-security-poc
python --version
node --version
az bicep install --version v0.46.1
----

=== Run local checks

The evidence recorder uses the Python standard library.

The document build uses the pinned npm dependencies.

Runtime dependencies are separate from document dependencies.

[source]
----
npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-audit --no-fund
.\scripts\Test-Kit.ps1
----

These checks do not authenticate to a tenant.

These checks do not prove a Microsoft security control.

=== Use the package files

[options="header"]
|===
|Path |Use
|docs\Agent-365-Security-PoC.docx |Editable runbook.
|docs\Agent-365-Security-PoC.pdf |Fixed-layout runbook.
|docs\POC.adoc |Repository-readable runbook.
|catalogue\scenarios.json |Test procedures and acceptance criteria.
|catalogue\support-matrix.json |Control coverage and integration boundaries.
|infra |Optional Azure infrastructure templates.
|runtime |Outbound-only custom model agent and explicit SDK instrumentation.
|hosted_foundry |Foundry hosted model agent and explicit SDK instrumentation.
|providers |Bedrock infrastructure and Claude Managed Agents lifecycle code.
|scripts\azure |Scoped infrastructure deployment and cleanup.
|poc_evidence |Local evidence records and acceptance reports.
|queries |Defender and Sentinel investigation queries.
|fixtures |Synthetic test content.
|reference-samples.json |Immutable references to official sample code.
|tools |Document build and export tools.
|===

Keep evidence, credentials, and populated configuration files outside the repository.

The GitHub workflow checks source files. It does not deploy resources or set tenant policies.

== Azure lab scope and approval

Use this infrastructure only for an approved, isolated Azure lab. It is not an Agent 365 deployment.

Run the commands in Windows PowerShell 5.1 from the repository root. Do not run a cloud command until the owner approves the target tenant, subscription, resource group and costs.

=== Identify the deployed resources

* Create one dedicated Log Analytics workspace. Use 30-day workspace retention and a configurable daily quota. Local authentication is disabled. [A01]
* Create one workspace-based Application Insights component. Local authentication is disabled. [A02]
* Enable Sentinel onboarding only when enableSentinel is true. The default is false. No connector or analytic rule is deployed. [A03]
* Create an OpenAI account and model deployment only when enableAzureOpenAI is true. The default is false. [A04] [A05]
* Create an account-scoped Cognitive Services OpenAI User assignment only when an operator object ID is supplied. [A06]

No agent, Entra Agent ID, app registration, tenant security policy or runtime is deployed.

A local compiler result is not cloud evidence. Azure telemetry is not proof of Agent 365 observability. Runtime integration is separate.

The OpenAI public endpoint uses Entra authentication, default deny and explicit IPv4 rules. It is not a private endpoint.

The monitoring endpoints are public and require Entra authentication. The OpenAI IP rules do not protect these endpoints.

=== Approve permissions, costs and residency

. Obtain owner approval for the exact tenant, subscription, resource group, location, tags and enabled services.
. Approve the maximum expected test volume and the test end time. Assign a person to review Azure costs.
. Verify the selected model name, version, deployment SKU and capacity in the approved region. Verify subscription quota and model access.
. Review the processing location for the selected SKU. Global and Data Zone deployments can process outside the resource region.
. Arrange a period with no concurrent resource changes for deployment and cleanup.

* Use an existing operator-established Azure CLI sign-in. The scripts do not log in or change the global subscription.
* Allow group, resource, child-resource, role-assignment and provider-state reads for cloud inventory.
* Allow deployment and target-resource permissions for Azure what-if. Reader alone may not be sufficient.
* Allow writes only for the approved resource types in the dedicated group. Prefer a reviewed custom role. Group-scoped Contributor is a broader option.
* Allow roleAssignments/write only if the optional operator role assignment is required. Contributor alone cannot assign this role.
* Allow exact-resource delete permissions for cleanup. Include roleAssignments/delete if the lab assignment exists.
* Register the required providers separately through an authorized operator. Use Microsoft.OperationalInsights and Microsoft.Insights. Add Microsoft.SecurityInsights or Microsoft.CognitiveServices only for the corresponding optional service.

The daily workspace quota is not a spending cap. Ingestion can exceed the cap. Some ingestion is not covered by it.

Sentinel, monitoring data and model use can incur charges. No budget, cost alert or capacity reservation is created.

Capacity units depend on the model and SKU. A successful compile or what-if does not reserve capacity.

NoAutoUpgrade does not prevent model retirement. Table retention can differ from workspace retention.

The operator object ID is a human user object ID in the expected tenant. It is not a client ID, service principal or Entra Agent ID.

Runtime telemetry needs a separate approved Entra identity and data-plane role. This template does not grant telemetry publishing rights.

== Prepare an isolated lab

=== Keep customer parameters outside the repository

. Create an operator configuration directory outside the repository.
. Copy the parameter example. Stop if the destination file already exists. Do not overwrite approved settings.
. Edit the copied file. Replace every angle-bracket placeholder.
. Keep every parameter from the example. Use JSON values, not strings for booleans or numbers.
. Save the file. Keep the file and removal plans out of source control.

* location: Set the approved canonical Azure region name.
* owner: Set a nonempty owner value. It must exactly match the resource group owner tag.
* workspaceName: Use 4-63 letters, digits or hyphens. Start and end with a letter or digit.
* applicationInsightsName: Use 2-255 letters, digits, dots, underscores or hyphens. Start and end with a letter or digit.
* dailyQuotaGb: Use a positive 32-bit integer. The example uses 1 GB/day.
* enableSentinel and enableAzureOpenAI: Keep false unless the owner approves each service.
* openAIAccountName: When OpenAI is enabled, supply a globally unique account and custom subdomain name. Use 3-63 lowercase letters, digits or hyphens.
* approvedIPv4Ranges: When OpenAI is enabled, supply at least one approved public egress IPv4 address or CIDR. Use a single address or /24 through /32. Do not use 0.0.0.0/0, private addresses, broad ranges or example IPs.
* modelDeploymentName, modelName, modelVersion and modelSku: When OpenAI is enabled, supply the approved exact values. No model or SKU is assumed.
* modelCapacity: Supply positive explicit capacity when OpenAI is enabled. Otherwise keep 0.
* operatorPrincipalObjectId: Leave empty unless the owner approves account-scoped access for the human operator.

[source]
----
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
$configDir = Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE 'Agent365-Config'
$null = New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $configDir -Force
$p = Join-Path $configDir 'parameters.json'
if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $p) { throw 'Parameter file already exists. Review it instead of overwriting it.' }
Copy-Item -LiteralPath .\infra\parameters.example.json -Destination $p
notepad $p
----

The scripts reject unknown parameters, missing parameters, unresolved placeholders and invalid network inputs.

The scripts cannot determine whether the customer approved an IP address. Record that approval separately.

Use scripts\azure\.work only for generated compilation and validation scratch. The lifecycle scripts remove their generated snapshots.

Use the lifecycle scripts rather than direct Bicep deployment. They enforce conditional input and network approval checks.

=== Run local checks

. Install or provide Windows PowerShell 5.1, Azure CLI and the Bicep compiler through the approved operator process.
. Run the local prerequisite check.
. Validate the edited parameters and compile the template.
. Run the offline infrastructure tests if Python is available.
. Stop on any failure. These checks do not verify Azure access, quota, region availability or provider registration.

[source]
----
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
$p = Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE 'Agent365-Config\parameters.json'
.\scripts\azure\Test-Prerequisites.ps1
.\scripts\azure\Test-Prerequisites.ps1 -ParametersFile $p
.\scripts\azure\Invoke-Deployment.ps1 -ParametersFile $p
python -B -m unittest discover -s tests\infra -p "test_*.py" -v
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Offline infrastructure tests failed.' }
----

These commands do not read subscriptions or run cloud what-if.

The tests mock Azure lifecycle commands. Only local Bicep compilation uses the real CLI.

Local validation used Bicep 0.46.1. No pwsh or external testing package is required.

=== Create the dedicated resource group after explicit approval

. Set the independently approved subscription and tenant GUIDs. Do not derive the expected tenant from the current CLI account.
. Set a new dedicated resource group name. Do not reuse or retag a production group.
. Read the approved location and owner from the validated parameter file.
. Verify the subscription, tenant, enabled state and public Azure cloud.
. Verify that the group does not exist.
. Review the displayed target. Type the exact approval text only after the resource owner approves creation.
. Create the group with both required tags. Check the CLI exit code.

[source]
----
$sub = '<approved-subscription-guid>'
$tenant = '<independently-approved-tenant-guid>'
$rg = '<new-dedicated-resource-group>'
$p = Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE 'Agent365-Config\parameters.json'
$null = [guid]::ParseExact($sub, 'D')
$null = [guid]::ParseExact($tenant, 'D')
if ($sub -eq [guid]::Empty.ToString() -or $tenant -eq [guid]::Empty.ToString()) { throw 'Use nonzero approved GUIDs.' }
$values = (Get-Content -LiteralPath $p -Raw | ConvertFrom-Json).parameters
$region = [string]$values.location.value
$owner = [string]$values.owner.value
$accountJson = az account show --subscription $sub --query '{id:id,tenantId:tenantId,state:state,environmentName:environmentName}' --output json --only-show-errors
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Account verification failed.' }
$account = $accountJson | ConvertFrom-Json
if ($account.id -ne $sub -or $account.tenantId -ne $tenant -or $account.state -ne 'Enabled' -or $account.environmentName -ne 'AzureCloud') { throw 'Approved Azure context did not match.' }
$exists = az group exists --name $rg --subscription $sub --output tsv --only-show-errors
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Resource group lookup failed.' }
if ($exists -ne 'false') { throw 'Use a new dedicated group. Do not reuse this group.' }
Write-Host "Tenant=$tenant Subscription=$sub Group=$rg Region=$region Owner=$owner"
$approval = Read-Host "Type CREATE $rg to approve this Azure write"
if ($approval -cne "CREATE $rg") { throw 'Creation was not approved.' }
az group create --name $rg --location $region --subscription $sub --tags 'poc=agent365-security-poc' "owner=$owner" --output json --only-show-errors
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Resource group creation failed. Inspect Azure before a retry.' }
----

This procedure writes to Azure. No lifecycle script creates the group for you.

Required tags are poc=agent365-security-poc and owner=<approved owner>. All expected taggable resources must have matching values.

Tags are an operational check, not an authorization boundary. Never change unrelated tags to bypass a rejection.

Keep the group exclusive to this lab. Do not add shared resources or shared child resources.

== Plan and apply the Azure lab

=== Run the read-only cloud plan

. Use the same approved $sub, $tenant, $rg and $p values from preparation.
. Run the script with -Plan.
. Review all what-if changes. Check every resource name, location, tag, IP rule, model value and role assignment.
. Stop if the plan contains an unexpected resource or if any read fails.
. Obtain approval for the displayed changes before apply.

[source]
----
.\scripts\azure\Invoke-Deployment.ps1 -ParametersFile $p -SubscriptionId $sub -ExpectedTenantId $tenant -ResourceGroupName $rg -Plan
----

This step reads Azure and runs Azure Resource Manager what-if. It does not submit a deployment.

The script validates the expected tenant with az account show --subscription. Each cloud command carries --subscription.

The script rejects untagged resources, owner mismatches and resources outside its expected ID list. It checks child model tags.

Sentinel onboarding and role assignments do not support tags. The script accepts only the specific expected scope and properties.

What-if does not guarantee quota, model availability, billing or successful deployment.

=== Apply only with explicit confirmation

. Run the command with both -Apply and -Confirm.
. Review the new what-if output. The script repeats the plan.
. Answer Yes to the PowerShell confirmation only for the approved exact group and changes. Answer No to stop.
. Wait for completion. Check the final result and any ownership validation error.
. Keep the approved parameter file unchanged for cleanup.

[source]
----
.\scripts\azure\Invoke-Deployment.ps1 -ParametersFile $p -SubscriptionId $sub -ExpectedTenantId $tenant -ResourceGroupName $rg -Apply -Confirm
----

-Apply without -Confirm is rejected. -Confirm:$false is rejected.

Add -WhatIf to suppress the Azure write even when -Apply and -Confirm are supplied.

The script checks resource state again around confirmation. It verifies the frozen template and parameter snapshot before the write.

A failed read, CLI error or detected change stops the operation. There is no automatic rollback.

This is an incremental deployment. Changing an optional flag to false does not remove its existing resources.

== Copilot Studio built-in integration

These procedures describe planned tests. They do not report a completed tenant deployment. Use only synthetic data and authorized test accounts.

A proof of concept, or PoC, tests a defined capability. Record deployment, registry visibility, runtime evidence, and enforcement separately.

Copilot Studio has built-in Agent 365 integration. Identity creation and registration do not require an added SDK. [P02]

Publication enables the selected user channel. Registry visibility alone does not prove successful publication or runtime telemetry. [P04, P09]

=== Preflight and ownership

Use a dedicated evaluation tenant. Identify the owner, operator, approver, test users, and evidence reviewer before changes.

Agent 365 is licensed per user. One qualifying license enables the service; it does not license every participant. [P01]

Prefer AI Administrator for supported governance actions. A reader role does not authorize installation or consent. [P03]

* Confirm Agent 365 or eligible Microsoft 365 E7 entitlement for the planned users and features.
* Confirm a standalone Copilot Studio subscription, maker access, publishing capacity, and the chosen environment.
* Do not use a free trial for publication. Its test-panel capability does not establish publishing entitlement. [P05]
* Do not substitute the Teams-included plan without checking its reduced capabilities. It does not provide generative orchestration. [P05]
* Use two licensed test identities. Assign the agent only to the approved test audience.
* Define an evidence window and a cost limit. Record local time, UTC, tenant ID, environment ID, and agent ID.
* Exclude customer documents, production connectors, real personal data, and production secrets.

=== Create, register, and publish

Use the standard-harness quickstart. Check the current portal labels before the customer demonstration. [P06]

Use https://copilotstudio.microsoft.com and https://admin.microsoft.com. The current registry path starts with Agents, not Agent 365. [P04]

. Open Copilot Studio. Select the approved Power Platform environment.
. On Home, turn off New experience if present. Start the standard agent creation flow.
. Name the agent A365 Studio Guide. Set instructions to explain Copilot Studio using public documentation.
. Open Overview > Knowledge. Add the official public Copilot Studio documentation used by the quickstart.
. Add fixtures\public-procedure.txt as a synthetic knowledge file. Wait until the source is ready.
. Open Test your agent. Ask: How can I add a public knowledge source?
. Open Microsoft 365 admin center > Agents > All Agents > Registry. Filter Platform to Copilot Studio.
. Find the draft agent. Record its ID, owner, description, and deployment state before publication.
. Open Settings > Security > Authentication. Select Authenticate with Microsoft, then Save.
. Select Publish > Publish. Record the publication result and time.
. Open the Teams and Microsoft 365 Copilot channel. Select Make agent available in Microsoft 365 Copilot if required.
. Select Add channel. Select See agent in Teams > Add for the maker test.
. Start a new conversation. Ask: Use the public procedure. State the test approval code.
. Confirm the answer contains POC-APPROVAL-6142. Use Start over when testing an updated publication.
. For distribution, select Availability options > Show to everyone in my org > Submit for admin approval.
. Have the administrator review the request and install for the approved test group only. Verify the effective audience.
. Change the agent description to include A365-STUDIO-01. Check the registry metadata after synchronization.

The organization submission label does not replace the administrator's audience decision. Verify the final deployment scope. [P08, P18]

Teams and Microsoft 365 channels require Authenticate with Microsoft in the documented configuration. [P07]

Identity and registry integration start before publication. Do not wait for Teams installation to assess draft registration. [P02, P04]

=== Positive and negative demonstrations

[options="header"]
|===
|Phase |Action |Expected result |Required evidence
|Positive |Use the assigned account in Teams. Ask: Use the public procedure. State the test approval code. |The authenticated agent returns POC-APPROVAL-6142. The registry identifies the same agent and approved channel. |Conversation time, synthetic response, publication state, agent ID, and assigned audience.
|Negative authentication |Open the Teams access link in a signed-out browser. Do not complete sign-in. |Authentication is required. No authenticated agent conversation occurs. |Sign-in requirement and test time. Do not record credentials or session tokens.
|Negative audience |Use the excluded test account. Verify no direct share, maker access, or other assignment grants access first. |The excluded account cannot use the scoped deployment. Investigate any alternate grant before judging the test. |Effective assignments and the actual denial. A missing catalogue tile alone does not prove authorization enforcement.
|Telemetry |Find the matching authenticated invocation in an available Agent 365 telemetry surface. |A matching record proves collection only for that invocation. Missing telemetry remains an open test result. |Agent identity, timestamp, supported telemetry fields, and any applicable redaction.
|===

=== Telemetry limits and stop conditions

Studio automatically emits telemetry, but only authenticated sessions qualify. Multitenant agents are excluded. [P09]

Agent names longer than 42 characters are not logged. Inputs and outputs can be truncated or redacted. [P09]

The generic Activity support list differs from the Studio observability guide. Confirm the actual tenant surface before execution. [P09, P10]

* Stop publication if licensing, capacity, authentication, approval, or audience scope is unresolved.
* Stop if a knowledge source includes customer content or requires an unapproved permission.
* Do not use an anonymous demo website as the Agent 365 telemetry acceptance path.
* Record an unavailable Activity view as a capability gap. Do not replace it with a fabricated telemetry result.
* Record every test as passed, failed, blocked, or not run. Separate documentation expectations from observed results.

=== Rollback and verification

. Save redacted evidence before changing the agent.
. Remove the test audience deployment through the supported administrative action.
. Remove the Teams test installation and direct shares created for this test.
. Remove test knowledge sources and connections. Delete the disposable Studio agent only after owner approval.
. Restore any changed external-publisher or audience settings to their recorded values.
. Verify the former test user cannot start a new agent session.
. Record remaining registry or audit history. Deletion does not promise immediate removal of retained evidence.

== Foundry hosted agents and Agent 365 telemetry

A hosted agent runs custom code in Foundry. A prompt agent uses a different configuration and telemetry path. [P12]

Normal publication, Agent 365 registry visibility, and Agent 365 runtime telemetry are separate results.

Published Foundry agents enter the registry. Do not apply Studio draft-visibility expectations to an unpublished hosted agent. [P12]

Use hosted_foundry for the required ONB-02 and ONB-09 cases. Its detailed deployment chapters follow this platform chapter.

Complete its export-off deployment, then use the normal publication steps below. Complete its export-on phase for ONB-09.

The official basic sample below is an optional hosting reference. Do not deploy both implementations for the same test agent.

This chapter uses normal publication. Autopilot blueprint publication is separately gated in the Frontier chapter.

=== Preflight roles, tools, and cost

[options="header"]
|===
|Operation |Required check
|New project provisioning |Confirm Owner at the dedicated resource-group scope and approved Azure subscription.
|Existing project deployment |Confirm Foundry Project Manager at the project scope.
|Normal publication |Confirm Foundry User at project scope and Azure Bot Service Contributor Role for Bot Service operations.
|Tenant distribution |Use an authorized agent administrator. Approve the selected audience and required permissions.
|Agent 365 telemetry |Confirm licensing, consent, SDK configuration, and the observability app-role assignment.
|Capacity |Confirm a supported region, model availability, quota, and a fixed cost limit.
|Workstation |Use Azure Developer CLI, called azd, version 1.32.0 or later for the selected extension versions.
|===

Foundry roles were renamed from Azure AI roles. Verify the role identifier and scope, not only its display name. [P11]

Foundry roles do not supply Azure Bot Service permissions. Confirm Microsoft.BotService registration before publication. [P13]

The code-deployment path builds remotely. It does not require local Docker. Other image-based paths can have different requirements. [P11]

The microsoft.foundry bundle includes azure.ai.agents. Do not install a duplicate extension. Follow the hosted-code version checks. [H15, H16]

Portal publication requires public network access on the project. Do not weaken a private-network policy to complete this demonstration. [P13]

=== Optional basic sample and normal publication

Run commands only against the approved evaluation subscription and project. Read each selected resource before confirming it.

Use official repository https://github.com/microsoft-foundry/foundry-samples.git at commit e899cd1ac6b372532954d0d4c22c4abc461621db.

The commit timestamp is 2026-09-17T15:39:41Z. Source existence was checked; deployment was not verified.

The reference-samples.json entry is foundry-hosted. Its manifest is samples\python\hosted-agents\agent-framework\responses\01-basic\azure.yaml.

Record the pinned revision and generated deployment files before provisioning. The deployment procedure follows P11.

. Check azd version. Confirm the approved tenant, subscription, resource group, project, region, model, and budget.
. Create an empty working directory outside the repository. Install the extension and authenticate with the commands below.
. Run azd ai agent init with the pinned sample manifest below and --deploy-mode code.
. Select the approved project and model during initialization. Enter the generated agent directory.
. Inspect azure.yaml and generated infrastructure. Stop if the selected resources or permissions exceed the approved scope.
. Run azd provision. Record created resource IDs without exporting secrets.
. Run azd ai agent run for the local development check. Stop it after the synthetic test.
. Run azd deploy. Wait for a successful hosted deployment before continuing.
. Run azd ai agent invoke with the synthetic prompt below. Record the response, version, endpoint, and time.
. In Foundry, open Details > Agent configuration > Active version > Edit. Select the tested version.
. Verify Foundry User, Azure Bot Service Contributor Role, and Microsoft.BotService registration for publication.
. Select Publish > Teams and Microsoft 365 Copilot. Complete metadata, then Next: Publish options > Direct publish.
. Select Just you for the first test. Select Publish and test with the authorized project user.
. For organizational distribution, choose People in your organization. Have the administrator approve the request and verify its effective audience.
. Open Microsoft 365 admin center > Agents > All Agents > Registry. Match the published entry to the hosted agent.

[source]
----
azd version
azd ext install microsoft.foundry
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Extension installation failed.' }
azd auth login
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Authentication failed.' }
azd ai agent init -m 'https://github.com/microsoft-foundry/foundry-samples/blob/e899cd1ac6b372532954d0d4c22c4abc461621db/samples/python/hosted-agents/agent-framework/responses/01-basic/azure.yaml' --deploy-mode code
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Initialization failed.' }

# Enter the generated agent directory before the next commands.
azd provision
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Provisioning failed.' }
azd ai agent run
# Stop the local run after its test. Then deploy the hosted agent.
azd deploy
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Hosted deployment failed.' }
azd ai agent invoke 'Return A365-FOUNDRY-01. Explain what a synthetic test is in one sentence.'
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Hosted invocation failed.' }
----

The basic sample uses Agent Framework, FoundryChatClient, DefaultAzureCredential, and ResponsesHostServer. It is not automatic hosted Agent 365 instrumentation. [P11, P12]

Just you uses project role-based access control, or RBAC. Sharing its link does not grant project invocation rights. [P13]

Stop the portal path if public network access is disabled. Use a separately approved private-network publication procedure instead. [P13]

=== Enable actual Agent 365 hosted telemetry

Application Insights is an Azure telemetry service. Its traces do not establish Agent 365 telemetry collection. [P12, P14]

Use the hosted_foundry sample and the hosted-code chapter for explicit SDK instrumentation and platform authentication.

Do not mark this phase complete until an actual Agent 365 record matches the test invocation.

. Record the hosted agent version and its current Application Insights tracing configuration.
. Read hosted_foundry and its pinned dependency file. Use the hosted-code chapter for deployment.
. Configure the hosted exporter and platform credential as specified in that chapter.
. Confirm Agent 365 licensing and approved tenant consent for this test.
. Open Azure portal > agent application resource > Overview > JSON View. Record agentIdentityId.
. Locate the tenant's Agent365Observability service principal. Verify its identity before assigning permissions.
. Use the permission procedure to grant Agent365.Observability.OtelWrite to the hosted agent identity.
. Read back the assignment. Record principal, resource, app role, scope, approver, and change time.
. Allow two to five minutes for propagation. Redeploy the instrumented hosted version when required.
. Invoke synthetic:add through the included sample's stateless request procedure. Record the returned trace and conversation IDs.
. Find the matching Agent 365 telemetry record. Preserve the successful runtime response separately.
. If collection fails, record the exact failure stage. Do not treat a registry entry as substitute evidence.

The role name is Agent365.Observability.OtelWrite. Follow the documented assignment procedure rather than inventing an API or role ID. [P15]

The included hosted sample does not export to Application Insights. Platform diagnostics can exist independently.

a365LoggingEnabled controls prompt-agent collection across the resource. It has no per-prompt-agent override. [P14]

Hosted SDK configuration can override resource-level logging disablement. That setting is not a reliable hosted-agent off switch. [P14]

=== Acceptance evidence and negative checks

[options="header"]
|===
|Test |Expected result |Evidence
|Positive hosting |The deployed version answers the synthetic prompt. |Deployment result, active version, endpoint, invocation time, and response.
|Positive publication |The intended user can access the approved channel. The published agent appears in Registry. |Channel result, tenant approval, effective audience, and matching agent identity.
|Positive telemetry |Agent 365 receives the instrumented hosted invocation. |Agent 365 record, identity, timestamp, and verified observability app-role assignment.
|Negative RBAC |A test identity without project rights cannot invoke the Just you publication. |Direct invocation denial and verified absence of inherited or alternative access.
|Negative inference |Application Insights traces alone do not satisfy the Agent 365 evidence requirement. |Separate result fields for Azure tracing and Agent 365 collection.
|===

* Stop on missing quota, unsupported region, role mismatch, consent failure, or unapproved public-network changes.
* Stop if the generated template introduces unapproved services or external connections.
* Do not grant a broad directory role merely to bypass a permission error.
* Treat missing Agent 365 records as blocked or failed after the agreed observation window.

=== Rollback and verification

. Save redacted hosting, publication, identity, and telemetry evidence.
. Remove the test publication or audience through the supported channel and administrative controls.
. Disable the hosted SDK exporter in the test deployment or stop the test workload.
. Remove only the observability assignment added for this disposable identity, after approval.
. Verify a new invocation does not produce new Agent 365 exports. Retained historical records can remain.
. Remove test Bot Service and Azure resources only when the deployment inventory proves exclusive PoC ownership.
. Restore any changed role assignments and resource settings. Confirm no shared resource was removed.
. Check vendor and Azure billing separately. Publication removal does not stop every hosted resource.

== Partner SaaS agents and a licensed Zendesk test

Software as a service, or SaaS, runs in a provider's environment. Its account permissions remain a separate boundary.

Choose a partner already licensed for the evaluation. A catalogue listing does not provide a free vendor subscription. [P16]

Microsoft distinguishes agents, AI teammates, and agent factories. Coming-soon and Frontier restrictions still apply. [P16]

Zendesk is listed as a Frontier AI teammate. Its provider guide describes Teams installation and login instead. Resolve this difference first. [P16, P19]

=== Preflight and catalogue checks

* Assign AI Administrator for supported installation and governance actions. Arrange an authorized consent approver. [P03]
* Confirm Microsoft 365 and Agent 365 entitlements for the selected offering and test users.
* Confirm vendor licensing, regional availability, provider account roles, support terms, and a synthetic test workspace.
* For Zendesk, confirm Suite Team, Growth, Professional, Enterprise, or Enterprise Plus eligibility. [P19]
* Alternatively, confirm Zendesk Support Team, Professional, or Enterprise eligibility. Verify the actual purchased plan. [P19]
* Create a licensed support-agent test account and a distinct end-user test account. Do not use real tickets.
* Confirm the exact publisher, catalogue entry, application identity, and vendor onboarding procedure during rehearsal.
* Do not infer readiness from Registered or Observable checkmarks when a listing says Coming soon. [P16]
* Apply the Frontier chapter gates if the selected offering requires AI-teammate activation or instance creation.

=== Common administrator deployment

Use this path for a catalogue offering that exposes the documented Install flow. Do not invent an instance-creation step. [P18]

Record the original external-publisher setting. A tenant-wide setting change can affect more than the test offering. [P17]

. Open Microsoft 365 admin center > Agents > Settings > Allowed agent types.
. Verify Allow apps and agents built by external publishers. Obtain approval before changing this setting.
. Open Agents > All agents > Registry. Filter Status to Available.
. Locate the entitled offering. Confirm publisher, product name, identity, and availability notes.
. Review Details, Permissions, Data & tools, and Certification where present.
. Compare every requested permission with the approved test purpose. Stop on an unexplained permission.
. Select Install. In Deploy agent to selected users, select only the approved test group.
. Select Review permissions > Grant admin consent. Have the authorized approver review the request.
. Select Accept > Next after approval. Record consent without capturing tokens.
. Select Review & finish > Finish deployment. Record the effective audience and result.
. Complete only the matching provider onboarding procedure. Use the provider's synthetic test account.
. Verify the registry entry and user access separately. Record any additional template or instance requirements.

Registry installation is not proof that the vendor account is connected.

Vendor OAuth authorization is not proof of Agent 365 runtime telemetry. Verify each claimed evidence surface separately.

=== Zendesk Support Assistant licensed path

Use a dedicated Zendesk test subdomain. Replace every placeholder with an operator-recorded test value.

Before execution, reconcile the Microsoft AI-teammate listing with the provider's Add and login flow. [P16, P19]

If they identify different offerings, stop. Do not combine them into one assumed deployment.

. Confirm the chosen Zendesk plan and the exact Support Assistant entry during rehearsal.
. Create synthetic tickets for two separate end users. Use subjects A365-ZD-OWN and A365-ZD-OTHER.
. Record ticket IDs and requester ownership. Keep all descriptions free of real personal data.
. Confirm the support-agent account can access both tickets in Zendesk. Confirm each end user sees only its permitted tickets.
. In Teams, open Apps. Search Zendesk Support Assistant and select Add for the approved test account.
. Open the app. Enter /login <test-subdomain> using the approved test subdomain.
. Select Connect to Zendesk. Authorize on Zendesk's page with the intended test account.
. Enter /login without a subdomain. Confirm the connected account and workspace.
. As the support-agent account, ask: Show me ticket #<synthetic-ticket-id>. Record the synthetic answer.
. In a separate end-user session, connect that user's account. Ask for its own synthetic ticket.
. Ask the same end-user session for the other user's synthetic ticket. Record the actual denial or non-disclosure.
. Capture the registry identity, approved audience, connected-account role, and both test outcomes. Exclude OAuth secrets.
. Enter /logout <test-subdomain> in each connected test session. Verify the vendor connection is no longer usable.

The provider applies the signed-in Zendesk account's permissions. End users and support agents have different ticket access. [P19]

Do not test delete or merge operations. Their additional restrictions are not needed for this read-only demonstration.

A refusal caused only by agent wording does not prove authorization enforcement. Compare the account's actual provider permissions.

=== Acceptance, stop conditions, and rollback

[options="header"]
|===
|Result |Required evidence
|Positive |The support-agent test account retrieves a permitted synthetic ticket through the installed assistant.
|Negative |The end-user test account does not receive another user's protected ticket content.
|Scope |The installed identity and effective audience match the approved catalogue entry.
|Connection removal |After logout, a new request requires connection or cannot access the previously connected account.
|Telemetry claim |Any claimed Agent 365 runtime record identifies the actual test operation. Registry visibility alone is insufficient.
|===

* Stop if the offering is unavailable, unlicensed, coming soon, or subject to unmet Frontier requirements.
* Stop if the vendor account can see real support data.
* Stop on unexplained consent, catalogue mismatch, or unexpected access by the excluded test account.
* After evidence capture, revoke only test OAuth grants and remove the scoped installation as supported.
* Restore changed tenant settings. Retire synthetic tickets and test accounts under the provider's approved procedure.
* Check vendor-side sessions, subscriptions, and scheduled work separately. Microsoft removal is not a global vendor rollback.

== Amazon Bedrock readiness and required Connected platforms sync

This procedure uses Amazon Bedrock Agents, now called Agents Classic. New-customer access closed on July 30, 2026. [P35]

Existing Agents Classic customers can continue. An existing AWS account alone does not establish eligibility. [P35]

Verify the specific account's Agents Classic eligibility and supported region before provisioning.

Do not substitute Bedrock AgentCore silently. Its APIs, permissions, deployment procedure, and acceptance evidence require separate approval.

Connected platforms imports external agent inventory and exposes management actions supported by the provider. [P20]

Registry sync remains a preview integration mechanism. It is not runtime telemetry, Teams hosting, or SDK instrumentation. [P02]

Required Bedrock coverage includes provider readiness, the provider authorization boundary, and actual Agent 365 registry synchronization.

ONB-05 tests provider readiness and authorization. ONB-10 requires actual registry synchronization, despite its preview status.

Approve the preview path separately. Missing rollout, eligibility, or approval keeps full requested PoC acceptance BLOCKED.

A provider-readiness pass does not prove registry synchronization or satisfy the connected-platform category.

Connected platforms differs from Researcher's Connected Agents feature. Do not use that feature for this procedure.

Use manual Sync agents. Scheduled synchronization is documented as a future release, not a current PoC dependency. [P20]

=== Preflight and credential warning

Use an authorized AWS test account that qualifies for Agents Classic. Isolate the test agent from customer workloads.

Stop if existing Agents Classic customer eligibility is unconfirmed. A new test account does not automatically inherit access. [P35]

For provider readiness, confirm AWS billing, model access, a test Bedrock service role, and approved positive and negative test identities.

For required sync, confirm Agent 365 eligibility, separate preview approval, and an authorized Microsoft agent administrator. [P03, P20]

Confirm Connected platforms access before preparing connector credentials. Provider readiness does not require creating a connector key.

The documented connector credential is not read-only. It includes invocation permissions and bedrock:DeleteAgent. [P20]

Obtain explicit approval for that authority. If approval is refused, stop the connector path and record the gap.

Scope IAM permissions using AWS-supported resource and condition constraints. Do not add a blanket administrator policy.

Keep access keys in the approved secret store. Do not place keys in JSON, screenshots, shell history, or evidence.

[options="header"]
|===
|Scope |Exact documented permission names
|Bedrock inventory |bedrock:ListAgents; bedrock:GetAgent; bedrock:ListAgentAliases; bedrock:GetAgentAlias; bedrock:ListAgentVersions; bedrock:GetAgentVersion
|Bedrock invocation and deletion |bedrock:InvokeModel; bedrock:InvokeAgent; bedrock:InvokeInlineAgent; bedrock:DeleteAgent
|Optional AgentCore inventory |bedrock-agentcore:ListHarnesses; bedrock-agentcore:ListAgentRuntimes; bedrock-agentcore:ListGateways; bedrock-agentcore:ListGatewayTargets
|Optional AgentCore reads |bedrock-agentcore:GetHarness; bedrock-agentcore:GetAgentRuntime; bedrock-agentcore:GetGateway; bedrock-agentcore:GetGatewayTarget; bedrock-agentcore:GetMemory
|Optional AgentCore deletion |bedrock-agentcore:DeleteHarness; bedrock-agentcore:DeleteAgentRuntime; bedrock-agentcore:DeleteGateway; bedrock-agentcore:DeleteGatewayTarget; bedrock-agentcore:DeleteMemory; bedrock-agentcore:DeleteWorkloadIdentity; bedrock-agentcore:DeleteAgentRuntimeEndpoint
|===

These are permission names, not a ready-to-apply IAM policy. Resource scoping requires separate AWS review.

The primary path tests a Bedrock agent. Do not add AgentCore permissions unless that additional scope is expressly approved.

Do not claim a reduced read-only policy meets the documented connection requirements without a separately verified result.

=== Required provider readiness and authorization boundary

. Verify existing Agents Classic customer eligibility and supported regional availability. Record the approval before creating resources.
. Record the approved AWS account, region, administrator, test identities, and cost limit.
. Open AWS console > Amazon Bedrock > Agents > Create Agent. Name it A365 Bedrock Inventory.
. Select Create. In Agent builder, select the approved service role and an accessible model.
. Set instructions: Explain synthetic test procedures. Do not request private data or perform external actions.
. Leave action groups and knowledge bases absent for this minimal test. Select Save > Prepare.
. Test in AWS: Return A365-BEDROCK-01 and explain a test inventory in one sentence. Record the agent ID.
. Record the provider configuration, authorized invoker, invocation time, and synthetic response.
. Use a separate approved AWS test identity without the required invocation authorization for this disposable agent.
. Repeat the same provider invocation. Capture the actual authorization error and effective permissions, without recording credentials.
. Record provider readiness and authorization separately. Proceed to required synchronization only after preview approval; otherwise, mark full PoC acceptance BLOCKED.

AWS permits a minimal agent without action groups or knowledge bases. This test needs no corporate connector. [P21]

A missing console item is not sufficient authorization evidence. Capture denial of the attempted provider operation.

Do not label these results as Agent 365 registry-sync, runtime-telemetry, or threat-detection proof.

=== Required Agent 365 preview synchronization (ONB-10)

Run this path only after provider readiness and separate preview approval. It does not replace the required provider authorization test.

If rollout, preview approval, or documented credential authority is unavailable, mark this path and full requested PoC acceptance BLOCKED.

Preserve the separate provider result. Do not count it as evidence that the required connected-platform demonstration passed.

. Approve the required registry-sync preview scope separately. Record the Microsoft connection owner and expected AWS agent.
. Review the exact connector permissions above. Obtain explicit approval for invocation and deletion authority.
. Confirm the Microsoft administrator can open Connected platforms. Verify the AWS account and region again.
. Create the approved dedicated IAM access key. Store its ID and secret securely without copying them into evidence.
. Open Microsoft 365 admin center > Agents > All Agents. In Connected platforms, select Manage.
. Select + Connect a platform. Enter A365 Bedrock Test and a description identifying the evaluation scope.
. Select Amazon Bedrock and the approved region. Record the automatic-import choice without assuming scheduled synchronization.
. Enter the access key ID and secret through the credential form. Select Validate credentials.
. Save only after successful validation. Select Sync agents.
. Review provider, region, last run, last sync status, total synced agents, and synchronization results.
. Match the synchronized entry to the AWS agent ID, name, and region.
. Change the AWS description to A365-BEDROCK-SYNC-02. Run Sync agents again and compare supported metadata.
. Run a synthetic provider conversation without Agent 365 runtime instrumentation. Record its exact UTC window and provider session ID.
. Search the agreed Agent 365 telemetry surface for that identity and window.
. Compare imported metadata with actual runtime records. Record absent transcripts as unobserved, not inspected.
. Complete this boundary phase before deactivating the connector key.

A successful provider response and a successful inventory sync are separate acceptance results.

=== Positive and negative demonstrations

[options="header"]
|===
|Phase |Procedure |Expected evidence
|Positive provider |Invoke the prepared agent in AWS with A365-BEDROCK-01. |Provider agent ID, region, time, and synthetic response.
|Required negative authorization |Repeat the provider invocation using the approved identity without the required invocation permission. |Actual provider authorization denial, test identity, and effective permission boundary.
|Required positive synchronization |Run manual sync, then repeat after the description change. |Matching registry identity, status, run time, count, and supported metadata update.
|Required negative runtime boundary |Run an uninstrumented provider conversation and search the matching Agent 365 telemetry window. |Provider session evidence and bounded search results. Registry metadata does not prove transcript inspection.
|Negative connector credentials |After sync evidence, deactivate only the dedicated connector access key in AWS. Attempt another manual sync. |Validation or synchronization fails. Capture the actual error without the key.
|Boundary |Compare the registry record with runtime evidence requirements. |Do not count inventory metadata as prompt, response, tool-call, or Teams-hosting evidence.
|===

A negative sync does not require deleting the provider agent. Do not invoke a delete action for this test.

If cached credentials delay the negative result, record it. Do not invent a revocation propagation time.

=== Stop conditions and rollback

* Stop provider testing on unconfirmed Agents Classic eligibility, wrong account, unsupported region, unsupported role scope, or missing model access.
* Record an eligibility block without claiming a failed Agent 365 connector. Do not provision AgentCore as an automatic replacement.
* Stop the required connector path if deletion authority or preview participation is unapproved. Mark full requested PoC acceptance BLOCKED.
* Stop if the connection can synchronize customer agents outside the approved boundary.
* Stop if validation fails. Review the specific error before changing IAM permissions.
* If created, remove the Microsoft connection and revoke the dedicated connector key after testing.
* Delete the disposable AWS agent and associated test-only resources through AWS after owner approval.
* Remove the test IAM user or policy only when no other approved test uses it.
* Verify the provider workload, access key, connection, and billing separately.
* Connection deletion does not guarantee provider deletion, stopped workloads, or immediate removal of retained registry history.

== Claude Managed Agents Connected platforms preview

This connector applies to Anthropic Claude Managed Agents. It does not register local Claude Code or every Claude API application. [P20]

Managed Agents APIs are beta. The Microsoft connection is preview. Approve both conditions before execution. [P20, P23]

Create a new non-default workspace. The default workspace does not expose the usable workspace ID required here. [P20]

Managed Agents are stateful. They are currently ineligible for Zero Data Retention and HIPAA BAA coverage. [P23]

=== Preflight and beta boundaries

* Confirm Claude Console account access, funded API usage, beta eligibility, and an available Managed Agents model.
* Create a dedicated non-default workspace. Record its workspace ID and authorized owner.
* Create a workspace-scoped API key for that workspace. Store it in the approved secret store.
* Confirm Agent 365 eligibility and an administrator who can open Connected platforms.
* Use no customer data, enterprise Model Context Protocol servers, production secrets, or connected business systems.
* Model Context Protocol, or MCP, connects an agent to tools or data. This minimal test adds no MCP server.
* Approve the provider-managed cloud environment and its networking policy before running the sample.
* Set a cost limit and a session time limit. Do not leave an unattended beta session running.

The quickstart enables built-in agent tools and unrestricted networking. Those settings require explicit approval. [P22]

If unrestricted networking is prohibited, stop this sample path. Review a documented restricted configuration separately.

Raw REST calls need anthropic-beta: managed-agents-2026-04-01. The documented SDK supplies this header. [P22]

=== Create the provider agent and synchronize it

. Create the dedicated non-default workspace and approved workspace-scoped key. Record only the workspace ID in evidence.
. Load the key into the operator process through the approved secret-handling method.
. Select a Managed Agents model available to that workspace. Set ANTHROPIC_MODEL to its verified identifier.
. In an isolated working directory, create the Python environment below. Install and record the anthropic package version.
. Run the provider code below. It creates an agent, cloud environment, and session.
. Wait for the synthetic response and idle status. Record agent, environment, and session IDs.
. Stop on an error, timeout, or unexpected tool action. Do not continue with an unverified provider agent.
. Open Microsoft 365 admin center > Agents > All Agents. In Connected platforms, select Manage.
. Select + Connect a platform. Enter A365 Claude Managed Test and the evaluation description.
. Select Anthropic Claude Managed Agents. Record any required region choice and the automatic-import choice.
. Enter the new workspace ID and its workspace-scoped API key. Select Validate credentials.
. Save after successful validation. Select Sync agents.
. Record provider, last run, last sync status, total synced agents, and synchronization results.
. Match the provider agent ID and name to the registry entry. Do not substitute the session ID.
. Perform the negative credential test below. Then remove credentials from the operator process.

Use the provider API or SDK quickstart. No verified equivalent Console creation wizard is assumed here. [P22]

An environment defines execution configuration. A session is one running instance of an agent in that environment.

Synchronization does not prove that session transcripts or runtime tool events reached Agent 365.

=== PowerShell launch and documented SDK calls

This is a proposed test script, not an executed deployment. Review beta API compatibility before use.

Inject ANTHROPIC_API_KEY securely. Do not enter a literal key in this document or in a recorded command.

The script uses provider-documented SDK calls with original synthetic instructions. Stop manually if the agreed time limit expires. [P22]

[source]
----
if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($env:ANTHROPIC_API_KEY)) { throw 'Load the approved workspace key securely.' }
if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($env:ANTHROPIC_MODEL)) { throw 'Set an approved Managed Agents model identifier.' }
python -m venv .venv
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Python environment creation failed.' }
& .\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install anthropic
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'SDK installation failed.' }
& .\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip show anthropic
try {
@'
import os
from anthropic import Anthropic

client = Anthropic(timeout=60.0)
agent = client.beta.agents.create(
    name="A365 Claude Managed Test",
    model=os.environ["ANTHROPIC_MODEL"],
    system="Use synthetic content only. Do not contact external services or change files.",
    tools=[{"type": "agent_toolset_20260401"}],
)
print("agent_id:", agent.id, "version:", agent.version, flush=True)
environment = client.beta.environments.create(
    name="A365 disposable environment",
    config={"type": "cloud", "networking": {"type": "unrestricted"}},
)
print("environment_id:", environment.id, flush=True)
session = client.beta.sessions.create(
    agent=agent.id,
    environment_id=environment.id,
    title="A365 synthetic inventory test",
)
print("session_id:", session.id, flush=True)
with client.beta.sessions.events.stream(session.id) as events:
    client.beta.sessions.events.send(
        session.id,
        events=[{
            "type": "user.message",
            "content": [{"type": "text", "text": "Return A365-CLAUDE-01. Explain synthetic data in one sentence. Use no tools."}],
        }],
    )
    for event in events:
        if event.type == "agent.message":
            for part in event.content:
                if part.type == "text":
                    print(part.text, flush=True)
        elif event.type == "agent.tool_use":
            raise RuntimeError("Unexpected tool use. Review and stop the provider session.")
        elif event.type == "session.status_idle":
            print("Provider session is idle.", flush=True)
            break
'@ | & .\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Provider test failed. Review retained provider resources.' }
}
finally {
    Remove-Item Env:\ANTHROPIC_API_KEY -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
}
----

The instructions are not an enforcement boundary. Built-in tools remain enabled in this documented quickstart configuration.

An SDK error or stopped local stream does not prove that the remote session stopped.

The script prints resource IDs before later operations. Use them to locate partial deployments for cleanup.

The script removes the local key after provider testing. Retrieve it securely again for the Microsoft connection form.

=== Positive and negative demonstrations

[options="header"]
|===
|Phase |Action |Required evidence
|Positive provider |Run the synthetic task with the approved workspace key. |Agent ID, workspace ID, environment ID, session ID, synthetic response, and idle status.
|Positive registry |Run Sync agents and locate the matching provider agent. |Connection status, run time, count, and exact provider-to-registry identity match.
|Negative credentials |After positive evidence, revoke the dedicated test key in the provider. Attempt another manual sync. |The actual credential or synchronization error. Do not capture the revoked key.
|Coverage boundary |Compare registry metadata with the runtime evidence checklist. |Mark transcript or tool-event collection unproven unless a separate instrumented path supplies it.
|===

=== Stop conditions and rollback

* Stop if only the default workspace is available or no workspace-scoped key can be issued.
* Stop if beta access, model availability, networking approval, or required data-retention terms are incompatible.
* Stop if the response uses unexpected tools or accesses data outside the synthetic scope.
* Remove the Microsoft connection. Use an approved workspace cleanup credential for the remaining provider operations.
* If the negative test revoked the original key, obtain a separately approved cleanup credential. Do not assume revoked credentials still work.
* No agent DELETE endpoint was verified. Agent retirement uses POST /v1/agents/{agent_id}/archive. [V20]
* Agent archival is irreversible. Obtain explicit owner approval and preserve evidence before archiving the disposable agent. [V19]
* Archival blocks new sessions, but existing sessions continue. The archived agent definition remains. [V19]
* Delete each recorded test session through DELETE /v1/sessions/{session_id}. Verify session_deleted confirmation. [V17]
* Delete the disposable environment through DELETE /v1/environments/{environment_id}. Verify environment_deleted confirmation. [V18]
* Revoke the original test key and any replacement cleanup key after provider cleanup.
* Verify remote session state and billing. Closing a terminal does not establish provider cleanup.
* Remove local process credentials and the disposable Python environment after evidence capture.
* Retained provider state and registry history can remain. Do not promise global or immediate deletion.

== OpenAI Connected platforms coverage gap

No verified native OpenAI Connected platforms connector is documented as of 2026-09-18. Record this as a coverage gap. [P20]

Do not invent an OpenAI connection wizard, authentication fields, API, or committed availability date.

An OpenAI Agents SDK integration, an OpenAI model in Foundry, and Codex discovery are different paths. [P02, P27]

A successful alternative does not close the native external-platform registry-sync gap.

=== Preflight and decision

* Define whether the requirement concerns provider inventory, hosted runtime telemetry, model use, or endpoint discovery.
* Use an authorized registry reader or administrator to inspect available options. Do not change platform connections.
* Require the selected alternative's own licensing, provider account, data handling, and role checks.
* Do not create an OpenAI API key solely for a nonexistent documented connector.
* Keep the native-connector acceptance result separate from alternative integration results.

=== Record the gap and select an approved alternative

. Write the requirement as native OpenAI external-platform inventory synchronization through Connected platforms.
. Record the source date and current supported-platform list from P20.
. If authorized, open Microsoft 365 admin center > Agents > All Agents > Connected platforms > Manage.
. Inspect + Connect a platform without saving a connection. Record the actual available platform names.
. Do not submit OpenAI credentials under Amazon Bedrock, Anthropic Claude Managed Agents, or another provider.
. Record the native path as unsupported by the verified documentation, not as a failed deployment.
. If runtime integration is required, select the separately documented OpenAI Agents SDK integration chapter.
. If workstation discovery is required, select the Codex procedure in the local-agent chapter.
. Apply the selected alternative's preflight gates. Use the marker A365-OPENAI-ALTERNATIVE-01 in its synthetic test.
. Record the alternative's own positive and negative results. Keep the native-connector gap open.
. If the tenant exposes an undocumented new option, stop. Obtain matching official authentication and support guidance before use.

=== Acceptance, evidence, and rollback

[options="header"]
|===
|Check |Outcome to record
|Native connector |No verified onboarding procedure. No deployment success claim.
|Positive alternative |Only the chosen SDK or endpoint capability passes, using that chapter's evidence.
|Negative boundary |A model response or a Codex inventory record does not count as native Connected platforms synchronization.
|Evidence |Requirement, source date, supported list, observed tenant options when authorized, gap owner, and alternative decision.
|Rollback |The inspection path creates no connector. Clean up only resources created by the separately approved alternative.
|===

This is not a forecast. Reassess only when current official documentation supplies a supported OpenAI procedure.

== Local Codex, Claude Code, and OpenClaw

Endpoint discovery identifies installed local agents. It does not turn them into hosted Agent 365 SaaS integrations. [P26]

Separate three tests: discovery, runtime threat protection, and whole-application blocking.

Defender for Endpoint runtime protection preview is separate from Frontier and Intune application blocking. [P34]

Native Claude Code and Codex CLI support runtime prompt and tool inspection with audit or block modes. [P34]

Their Microsoft 365 Local Agents application-blocking matrix still says unavailable. That does not mean all threat blocking is unavailable. [P24]

=== Runtime protection is not whole-application blocking

[options="header"]
|===
|Tool or mechanism |Supported claim |Limit
|Claude Code and Codex CLI runtime protection |Preview prompt and tool inspection can audit or block supported threats. |Apply the control chapter's exact prerequisites and safe positive/negative test. Do not infer coverage for every invocation.
|OpenClaw runtime protection |Network inspection is a distinct protection path. |Certificate-pinned traffic and HTTP/3 are excluded. Network visibility is not unrestricted prompt or tool inspection.

|Supported VSCode extensions |The Local Agents matrix lists application blocking for Claude Code and Codex extensions. |Extension coverage does not establish the same action for native CLI clients.
|Microsoft 365 Shadow AI |The documented OpenClaw application-blocking action uses Intune policy. |Propagation and shared Node.js effects can extend across all Intune-managed Windows devices.
