Use P34 and the control chapters' configuration sources for runtime protection. Use P24 and P25 for administrative application blocking.

For runtime protection, establish a permitted synthetic baseline before the control chapter's approved negative test.

Record inspection mode, policy scope, actual alert or block, and exclusions. A benign prompt alone does not test threat prevention.

Do not use a harmless synthetic file test as proof that malicious prompts or tool actions are blocked.

=== Optional whole-application blocking: disposable tenant only

Do not run this optional test in a customer production tenant or a shared tenant.

Use only a dedicated disposable tenant. The documented action can affect all Intune-managed Windows devices. [P24, P25]

A small device or user group does not establish isolation for this action.

Node.js-based blocking can stop other agents and processes using the shared runtime. Unblocking one can remove the shared block. [P24]

Documented propagation is 15 minutes to eight hours. Do not present it as an immediate emergency-response guarantee. [P25]

. Confirm the tenant is dedicated and disposable. Inventory every Intune-managed Windows device in it.
. Obtain explicit approval for tenant-wide impact and shared Node.js disruption. Otherwise skip this demonstration.
. Record current policy state and installed Node.js-based test processes. Use no production process.
. Start the approved synthetic OpenClaw test and capture successful baseline execution.
. Open Microsoft 365 admin center > Agents > Shadow AI. Select the test OpenClaw entry.
. Open Security policies > Block > Apply Policies. Record the operation and time.
. Allow documented Intune propagation. Inspect policy delivery on every disposable test device.
. Attempt a new synthetic OpenClaw operation. Record the actual blocking result and shared-runtime effects.
. Restore the unblocked state through the same documented policy workflow. Record its time and affected runtime.
. Wait for policy removal and retest every disposable device. Record any remaining block before ending the test.

For a supported extension, the Local Agents action is Security policies > Block agent > Apply Policies. [P24]

Do not substitute native Codex CLI or Claude Code in this whole-application blocking procedure.

Do not promise independent rollback for one Node.js agent. Verify all affected processes after restoring the shared policy.

=== Stop conditions and cleanup

* Stop on missing endpoint entitlement, inactive Antivirus, unsupported execution target, or unapproved provider authentication.
* Stop if local tools access a real repository, personal directory, enterprise service, or production secret.
* Stop the application-blocking path unless every tenant-wide impact gate passes.
* For runtime protection, restore only the test policy configuration defined in the control chapter. Verify its effective state.
* Sign out of test tools. Revoke test-only provider credentials when no other approved test needs them.
* Uninstall the selected tool through its provider's supported method. Stop and remove test gateway services where applicable.
* Remove synthetic project files after evidence capture. Restore or discard the disposable device baseline.
* Retained discovery or alert records can remain. Uninstallation does not guarantee immediate inventory deletion.

== Optional Frontier and Foundry autopilot pathway

This chapter is optional. It is not required for normal hosted-agent publication or the core registry demonstrations.

An autopilot blueprint is a publishable template. Users create instances with separate agent identities and agent user accounts. [P32]

Only hosted agents support this autopilot publication path. Do not apply it to a Foundry prompt agent. [P12]

Frontier has no specific service-level agreement. The Agent 365 guidance recommends trials rather than Frontier for product evaluations. [P30]

=== Hard preflight gates

Resolve the licensing discrepancy before provisioning. Do not select the cheapest interpretation and assume it works.

The Agent 365 Frontier page states at least one E7 license. [P30]

The Foundry autopilot guide states a Copilot or Agent 365 license, including E7. [P32]

General Frontier guidance requires Copilot licenses for participating users. Verify the actual tenant and user eligibility. [P31]

* Use an authorized evaluation tenant. Confirm the selected users' Copilot, Agent 365, or E7 entitlements with the licensing owner.
* Have Global Administrator accept Agent 365 preview terms where required. Do not use this role for routine execution.
* Confirm the 25-seat preview subscription in Billing > Licenses. Check free seats before each instance creation.
* Plan one preview seat per instance. Do not count an unassigned commercial license as a verified preview seat.
* Confirm Azure provisioning and image-push roles specified by the current sample. Record their actual resource scopes.
* Assign AI Administrator or Global Administrator for the documented blueprint approval, as authorized.
* Confirm that the test user belongs to the approved instance-creation audience.
* Approve identity creation, agent user accounts, template permissions, cost, and asynchronous provisioning.
* Rehearse the complete sample and cleanup in the evaluation tenant before a customer demonstration.

=== Sample drift gate

The Learn procedure identifies publishAsAutopilot, publishScope, useAgenticUserTemplate, and agenticUserTemplate as relevant publication fields. [P32]

The researched Python script contains publishAsAutopilot and publishScope. It omits the two template fields. [P33]

This is a reproducibility gap, not proof that the service fails.

The reference-samples.json entry is foundry-autopilot. Its manifest is samples\python\foundry-autopilot-agent\azure.yaml.

The source snapshot is e899cd1ac6b372532954d0d4c22c4abc461621db, dated 2026-09-17T15:39:41Z. Source existence was checked; deployment was not verified.

Compare the current sample with the current documented request. Pin and record a rehearsed compatible revision before use.

Do not invent missing request fields, template identifiers, permissions, or API endpoints.

* Review samples\python\foundry-autopilot-agent in the official microsoft-foundry\foundry-samples repository.
* Inspect scripts\publish-digital-worker.ps1 and scripts\post-provision.ps1 before azd provision.
* Confirm what the post-provision hooks build, create, and publish. Provisioning can trigger those additional operations.

=== Enroll, publish, approve, and create one instance

. Complete the licensing, seat, role, networking, sample revision, and cleanup gates above.
. Open Microsoft 365 admin center > Copilot > Settings > View all. Search Frontier.
. Open Copilot Frontier. Select Specific users and the approved test users.
. Where required, have Global Administrator open Agents > Overview > Try now > I agree.
. Open Billing > Licenses. Verify the preview subscription and available seats.

. Authenticate az and azd to the approved tenant and subscription. Confirm the displayed identities before provisioning.
. Inspect sample parameters and hooks. Run azd provision only after the sample drift gate passes.
. Record created resource IDs, blueprint identity, publication result, and deployment values without secrets.

. In Publish to users, select Activate > Specific users or groups. Select only the approved instance-creation audience.

. Select Review and finish > Publish. Confirm Registry shows Available for the blueprint.

[source]
----
$TenantId = Read-Host 'Approved evaluation tenant ID'
$ApprovedAutopilotSamplePath = Read-Host 'Reviewed Python autopilot sample directory'
$ExpectedCommit = 'e899cd1ac6b372532954d0d4c22c4abc461621db'
if (-not (Test-Path (Join-Path $ApprovedAutopilotSamplePath 'azure.yaml'))) { throw 'Sample directory is invalid.' }
Set-Location $ApprovedAutopilotSamplePath
$ActualCommit = git rev-parse HEAD
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0 -or $ActualCommit -ne $ExpectedCommit) { throw 'Sample revision does not match the approved source snapshot.' }
az login --tenant $TenantId
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Azure authentication failed.' }
az account show --query '{tenantId:tenantId,subscriptionId:id,name:name}' -o table
azd auth login
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Azure Developer CLI authentication failed.' }
# Verify both tool contexts and resolve sample drift before this command.
azd provision
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Provisioning or a post-provision hook failed. Inspect partial resources.' }
----

Do not apply the normal hosted code-build Docker statement to this image-based sample. Check its own build prerequisites. [P32]

An available blueprint is not a created instance. A created instance is not proof that every telemetry or security control works.

Do not retry Create blindly while provisioning is pending. Inspect the existing request and seat usage first.

== Hosted Foundry: Agent 365 sample

Use hosted_foundry for the hosted-code category. This is separate from prompt-agent resource logging and from the local runtime sample.

The handler runs an actual local synthetic_add tool. It then awaits Agent.run through FoundryChatClient. It returns the model response, the actual invoke trace ID, and the SDK conversation-chain ID. A model error is not replaced with a synthetic success. [H01, H03, H11, H13]

The tool is a deterministic application call, not a model-selected function call. This proves a local tool execution path, not model tool-selection behavior.

Only the literal synthetic:add is accepted. No customer content, files, input references, caller instructions, external tools, or prior response history are sent to the model.

No hosted deployment, Azure authentication, model request, server start, or telemetry transmission was performed while creating these artifacts. Live eligibility and ingestion remain unvalidated.

=== Code and bounded behavior

main.py uses the current three-argument Responses response handler. The service uses Responses protocol 2.0.0 and the python_3_13 source runtime. [H01, H02]

application.py limits active handlers to two per process. Requests fail fast when busy. Model work has a 45-second deadline. Client cancellation and host shutdown cancel the active task.

limits.py limits POST bodies to 4096 bytes before JSON parsing. It limits body reception to ten seconds. The accepted input has a 64-byte upper bound. The model token limit is 256. Responses over 2048 characters fail.

The tool only adds two bounded integers. It has no file, shell, network, or external-tool capability. The model client is created per invocation and closed afterward.

The opaque Foundry call ID is forwarded with the SDK header constant. It is never treated as an email, user ID, agent appID, or verified human identity.

Use stateless requests with store=false. Both model storage and durable response storage are disabled in this sample. The host uses an in-memory provider; this is not a durable conversation service.

* hosted_foundry\src\main.py
* hosted_foundry\src\poc_hosted\application.py
* hosted_foundry\src\poc_hosted\limits.py
* hosted_foundry\src\poc_hosted\identity.py
* hosted_foundry\src\poc_hosted\telemetry.py
* hosted_foundry\src\poc_hosted\settings.py
* hosted_foundry\src\requirements.txt
* hosted_foundry\azure.yaml
* hosted_foundry\Grant-ObservabilityRole.ps1
* hosted_foundry\invoke_sample.py
* tests\foundry

The application admission limit is per process, not a platform-wide rate limit. Use the platform's approved ingress, authorization, scaling and spending controls.

The handler does not implement inbound bearer authentication. The Foundry gateway is the access boundary. Do not expose this host as a public standalone server.

A caller response contains model text and correlation IDs. Telemetry does not record the model text.

=== Local validation and package checks

Run the standard-library tests from the project root. Tests use fake credentials, model calls, SDK providers and exporters. PowerShell tests replace az with a local function. Tests neither open a server nor call Azure.

Direct runtime pins were checked against published package metadata. The official basic sample has requirements.txt, not pyproject.toml. The separately pinned official uv Responses pyproject was also inspected. [H03, H04, H08, H09, H10]

These are exact direct dependency pins, not a fully resolved transitive lock file. Resolve and review the complete environment in the approved build network before deployment.

Do not run main.py on a workstation to prove agent identity. Its hosted configuration and platform call context are required.

[source]
----
python -B -m unittest discover -s tests\foundry -p "test_*.py" -v

# Install packages only in the isolated environment.
py -3.13 -m venv hosted_foundry\.venv
hosted_foundry\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install --only-binary=:all: -r hosted_foundry\src\requirements.txt
hosted_foundry\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip check
hosted_foundry\.venv\Scripts\python.exe tests\foundry\sdk_import_check.py
----

The import check must not call create_app, main, get_token, Agent.run, or the exporter constructor.

The hosted sample has no dependency on runtime.

The complete dependency set resolved on Windows ARM64 with CPython 3.12. The network-blocked SDK import check passed.

The remote Python 3.13 build and live protocol require validation in the approved Foundry environment.

== Hosted Foundry: explicit telemetry and identity boundary

POC_A365_EXPORT defaults to false. Export on requires the literal true, an approved tenant GUID, and the deployed agent application's client ID.

Agent 365 resource logging settings are not a hosted-code kill switch. Explicit hosted SDK configuration can override resource disablement. Disable this application's exporter and stop all older export-on versions when testing opt-out. [H05]

The hosted platform creates the agent identity during deployment. The user who runs azd is not that identity. [H01, H02]

=== Metadata-only SDK scopes

telemetry.py installs an explicit OpenTelemetry TracerProvider. It returns a no-op tracer to all instrumentation sources except Agent365Sdk. A pre-existing global provider causes startup failure.

The sample deliberately does not call use_microsoft_opentelemetry. In 1.3.9, that convenience function can activate environment-selected OTLP export, resource detection, SDK self-telemetry, and automatic instrumentation. The explicit provider avoids that implicit behavior. [H07]

The isolated adapter constructs the pinned SDK's internal _Agent365Exporter only when export is on. It uses S2S mode, the production cluster, a bounded queue, and enable_durable_delivery=False. This is a version-pinned internal API, not a claim of a stable public exporter factory.

AgentServer receives configure_observability=None and access_log=None. The injected AppInsights connection string remains available but is not consumed by this sample. Agent Framework instrumentation is explicitly disabled. No logging, metrics, Azure Monitor, OTLP or console exporter is configured. [H12, H13]

Both MICROSOFT_OTEL_SDKSTATS_DISABLED and APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_STATSBEAT_DISABLED_ALL are set to true before SDK import. Inherited exporter, sampler, resource, domain and credential overrides are rejected.

InvokeAgentScope wraps the actual request work. ExecuteToolScope wraps the actual arithmetic. A ContextVar associates the tool with the correct request. The root starts with a clean OTel context, so incoming baggage cannot inject user or agent identity.

No prompt, result, tool argument, file content, raw token, caller identity or arbitrary attribute is supplied to SDK scope methods. Errors are reduced to a fixed message before the SDK can record them. Cancellation is recorded as cancellation.

Off mode creates no exporter and requests no observability token. The model can still run. It may request its own model-access token through the platform credential.

The sample does not switch off independently operated Foundry gateway, control-plane, network or platform diagnostics. Zero sample A365 export is not a claim of zero Azure telemetry.

Do not add automatic HTTP or Agent Framework instrumentation without a separate content-capture review. Keep the internal exporter pin until a reviewed upgrade replaces it.

The trace ID is the actual root invoke span ID's trace component, not a fabricated proof of ingestion. The conversation ID is the SDK-derived conversation-chain ID, not an invented human identity.

=== Platform credential and token consistency

The official hosted sample uses DefaultAzureCredential. This sample excludes environment-secret, Azure CLI, PowerShell, Developer CLI, shared-cache, VS Code, browser and broker credentials. Only managed-identity and workload-identity paths remain. It does not invent a metadata endpoint. [H03, H14]

On export-on startup, and again when the exporter requests a token, the credential requests api://9b975845-388f-4429-889e-eab1ef63949c/.default.

The token must have the approved tid, an appid or azp matching A365_AGENT_ID, the observability audience, the Agent365.Observability.OtelWrite role, and a future expiry. If both appid and azp exist, both must match. Delegated scp and user idtyp tokens are rejected.

Claims are read only from the trusted Azure SDK credential result to detect configuration mismatch. This is not cryptographic verification of an arbitrary JWT. The application never accepts an access token from an incoming request for telemetry.

If the platform does not make a supported workload or managed identity path available, stop. Mark the test BLOCKED. Obtain a documented platform credential adapter from the platform owner and review identity.py before continuing. Never enable desktop CLI or secret fallback to make the test appear successful.

* A365_TENANT_ID: approved tenant ID.
* agentIdentityId / AgentPrincipalId: deployed agent service-principal object ID; used for Graph app-role assignment.
* A365_AGENT_ID / ExpectedAgentAppId: that service principal's appId; used for telemetry attribution and SDK-token consistency checks.
* Agent365Observability resource appId: 9b975845-388f-4429-889e-eab1ef63949c.
* Observability role ID: 8f71190c-00c8-461d-a63b-f74abde9ba52.

The object ID and application/client ID are not interchangeable.

A source-verified credential path is not live proof that the tenant or region exposes it. Startup failure is preferable to an incorrectly attributed telemetry export.

No inbound caller email, object ID or agent-user identity is fabricated. Caller authorization and telemetry agent attribution are separate concerns.

== Hosted Foundry: phase one deploy with export off

Deploy first with export off. The platform can then create the agent identity. Grant the one observability role afterward. This avoids a circular prerequisite.

Use an existing approved Foundry project and an existing model deployment. The manifest has no model SKU, model version or model deployment resource. It does not deploy a new model. [H02, H03, H17]

Use a new, unique agent name. Reusing a name creates a new version of an existing agent and can change an unrelated application. [H15]

=== Operator gates and help checks

. Obtain authorization for the region, project, model, data handling and spending. Confirm Hosted Agent and source-code runtime availability for the selected region.
. Confirm the deployer has the documented Foundry Project Manager permissions. Do not grant tenant-wide permissions to the runtime application.
. Use azd 1.32.0 or later. Use azure.ai.agents 1.0.0-beta.16 or later. Check the extension's own compatibility requirement.
. Use approved azd and Azure CLI sessions in the selected tenant. Authentication is an operator action, not performed by the sample or its tests.
. Read the installed help. If required flags are absent, stop and update through the approved tooling process.
. Run from hosted_foundry. Do not run azd init at the project root, where it could affect other PoC artifacts.

[source]
----
Set-Location hosted_foundry
azd version
azd extension list
# Only if the extension is missing:
azd extension install azure.ai.agents
azd ai agent init --help
azd provision --help
azd deploy --help
azd ai agent show --help
----

Command flags were checked against pinned upstream help snapshots. The authoring environment has no azd executable. No binary help execution, provision preview or deployment was performed. [H15, H16]

The microsoft.foundry extension used in the platform preflight is a meta-package. It includes azure.ai.agents and other Foundry extensions. This sample can install azure.ai.agents directly with its declared dependencies. Do not reinstall it if the bundle already supplied a compatible version.

At the pinned CLI source revision, microsoft.foundry 1.0.0-beta.3 and azure.ai.agents 1.0.0-beta.16 both require azd >=1.32.0. Their extension.yaml files define these requirements. The sample manifest retains the older upstream minimum; the installed extension's stricter requirement takes precedence. [H03, H15]

Do not use broad Agent 365 setup-all flows. The only tenant grant in this chapter is the specific observability application role.

=== Attach and review the existing project

Replace every angle-bracket value. ProjectId is the complete ARM ID ending in /accounts/<account>/projects/<project>. ProjectEndpoint is its public-cloud data-plane URL ending in /api/projects/<project>.

The POC_EXISTING_PROJECT_ENDPOINT value is for azd's existing-project service. Do not redeclare FOUNDRY_PROJECT_ENDPOINT or other FOUNDRY_* variables under the agent's env map. The platform supplies those values. [H01]

The model name is the approved existing deployment alias, not a model catalogue name. Use --model-deployment, not --model. [H15]

[source]
----
$ProjectId = '<existing-project-ARM-ID>'
$ProjectEndpoint = 'https://<account>.services.ai.azure.com/api/projects/<project>'
$ModelDeployment = '<approved-existing-model-deployment>'
$AgentName = '<new-unique-synthetic-agent-name>'
azd env new '<new-isolated-environment-name>'
azd env set AZURE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID '<approved-subscription-ID>'
azd env set AZURE_LOCATION '<existing-project-region>'
azd env set POC_EXISTING_PROJECT_ENDPOINT $ProjectEndpoint
azd env set AZURE_AI_MODEL_DEPLOYMENT_NAME $ModelDeployment
azd env set POC_AGENT_NAME $AgentName
azd env set POC_A365_EXPORT false
azd ai agent init --manifest .\azure.yaml --project-id $ProjectId --model-deployment $ModelDeployment --agent-name $AgentName --deploy-mode code --runtime python_3_13 --entry-point main.py
Get-Content .\azure.yaml
azd provision --preview
----

Init can rewrite azure.yaml and local environment files. Review the result before provision. Preserve project: src, kind: hosted, Responses 2.0.0, python_3_13, entryPoint: main.py and dependencyResolution: remote_build.

Confirm the ai-project service still points to the existing endpoint and that no deployments block, new model SKU, external tool, broad permission, or export-on setting was introduced. Stop if the preview proposes changing the existing model or deleting resources.

The shipped .agentignore excludes local environment files, credentials, caches and built artifacts. The service project is only src; the grant script, probe, docs, tests and local virtual environment are not uploaded.

A remote build downloads dependencies. It does not require local Docker. Builds and hosted runtime/model usage can incur cost.

=== Provision, deploy and collect identity

. Approve the preview. Run provision and deploy as separate commands. Do not use an unattended azd up before review.
. Wait for active. Record the new agent name, version, endpoint, deployment timestamp and exact artifact revision.
. Confirm the deployed environment contains POC_A365_EXPORT=false. The app must not need an observability role to start in this phase.
. Open only the new agent application resource in the Azure portal. Select Overview, JSON View and the latest API version. Copy agentIdentityId. [H06]
. Keep agentIdentityId as the service-principal object ID. Resolve its appId with the preview script in phase two. Do not substitute the deployer's CLI client ID.

[source]
----
azd provision
azd deploy synthetic-agent
azd ai agent show synthetic-agent --output json
----

Do not claim success from a returned deployment operation alone. The version must become active and a separately authorized synthetic request must complete.

The platform injects FOUNDRY_PROJECT_ENDPOINT, FOUNDRY_AGENT_NAME, FOUNDRY_AGENT_VERSION, FOUNDRY_AGENT_SESSION_ID and an AppInsights connection string. This app uses the supported hosted values, not workstation copies. [H01]

If the identity is not yet visible, wait and inspect the deployed application again. Do not create an unrelated application registration as a substitute.

== Hosted Foundry: phase two narrow grant and opt-in

Use an approved Microsoft Entra administrator session for the grant. The application does not hold Graph permissions. The role must be assigned to the deployed agent object, not to its user, project or deployment CLI. [H06]

The script defaults to a read-only preview. It checks tenant, deployed principal, resource appId, fixed role, existing assignments and the reviewed agent appId. It does not sign in, grant unrelated permissions, or repeat an existing assignment.

=== Preview and apply exactly one app role

. Confirm Agent 365 licensing and tenant consent with the tenant owner. Confirm Agent365Observability exists in this tenant.
. Use the agentIdentityId collected from phase one. Run preview without -Apply.
. Review the returned tenantId, agentIdentityId, A365_AGENT_ID, resourceId and appRoleId against the new deployed agent.
. If alreadyAssigned is true, do not create a duplicate grant. Preserve the existing assignment during cleanup.
. Otherwise run -Apply -Confirm with the reviewed ExpectedAgentAppId. Approve the script's narrow change confirmation.
. Save the returned assignment ID and read-back result in the approved evidence location. After propagation, rerun preview to confirm alreadyAssigned=true.

[source]
----
$TenantId = '<approved-tenant-GUID>'
$AgentPrincipalId = '<deployed-agentIdentityId-object-GUID>'
.\Grant-ObservabilityRole.ps1 -TenantId $TenantId -AgentPrincipalId $AgentPrincipalId
$AgentAppId = '<A365_AGENT_ID-from-reviewed-preview>'
.\Grant-ObservabilityRole.ps1 -TenantId $TenantId -AgentPrincipalId $AgentPrincipalId -ExpectedAgentAppId $AgentAppId -Apply -Confirm
.\Grant-ObservabilityRole.ps1 -TenantId $TenantId -AgentPrincipalId $AgentPrincipalId -ExpectedAgentAppId $AgentAppId
----

Role: Agent365.Observability.OtelWrite. Fixed appRoleId: 8f71190c-00c8-461d-a63b-f74abde9ba52. Resource appId: 9b975845-388f-4429-889e-eab1ef63949c.

The resource is resolved by appId, never by display name alone. The resourceId in the POST body is its tenant-local service-principal object ID.

The script writes only a short-lived JSON request body containing identifiers under hosted_foundry\generated during Apply. It removes that file afterward. It never writes tokens or credentials.

Writes require both -Apply and an explicitly true -Confirm. Omitted confirmation and -Confirm:$false fail before any Azure CLI call, even when ConfirmPreference is None.

A failed or ambiguous POST is not blindly retried. Rerun preview and inspect the existing assignment. Allow 2-5 minutes for permission propagation. [H06]

After a successful POST, the script emits the assignment ID with verification=pending. A failed or mismatched read-back emits verification=incomplete and terminates with an error. It never reports that no grant was made after POST success.

For incomplete verification, save the receipt. Run the exact GET command from the error using readBackUri. Match assignmentId, principalId, resourceId and appRoleId. Then rerun preview without -Apply after propagation. Do not repeat POST. If POST returned no usable assignment ID, inspect the collection URI and match the three target identifiers first.

=== Enable export and redeploy

. Review the new version's environment. It must contain the approved tenant ID and agent application/client ID.
. Confirm the deployed agentIdentityId still maps to the reviewed appId. If the platform gives the new version a different identity, do not broaden access. Recheck the identity, preview its narrow grant, and update configuration deliberately.
. Wait for active. Export-on startup validates the platform observability token before any exporter is installed.
. On identity, audience, role, credential or expiry mismatch, leave the test BLOCKED. Do not bypass the consistency checks.
. Only after startup and tenant approval, run the one synthetic probe and collect ingestion evidence.

[source]
----
azd env set A365_TENANT_ID $TenantId
azd env set A365_AGENT_ID $AgentAppId
azd env set POC_A365_EXPORT true
azd deploy synthetic-agent
azd ai agent show synthetic-agent --output json
----

The explicit opt-in controls the application, not the tenant's global logging state.

A successful startup proves that a trusted SDK credential supplied a matching token at that time. It does not prove Agent 365 accepted a later telemetry batch.

== Hosted Foundry: evidence, negative test and cleanup

Run live checks only in the approved tenant and project. These steps were not executed during authoring.

invoke_sample.py is an operator-side caller. Its AzureCliCredential authenticates the user making the request, not the hosted agent. The hosted export path never uses AzureCliCredential.

A model response is not evidence of telemetry ingestion, Defender detection, Purview enforcement, registry synchronization, certification, or end-to-end security effectiveness.

=== One authorized synthetic invocation

. Record the start/end time, selected agent version and export setting.
. Confirm the model returns its actual short arithmetic explanation. Preserve the returned trace_id and conversation_id in the approved evidence record.
. In the authorized Agent 365 investigation view, find the same trace ID and conversation ID. Confirm one invoke_agent root and its synthetic_add execute_tool child.
. Confirm agent attribution matches the deployed appId and tenant. Do not infer human attribution from an absent caller field.
. Inspect exported attributes. Prompts, model results, tool arguments/results, authorization headers, emails and file contents must be absent.
. Record the ingestion timestamp and actual observed status. If spans are absent, mark ingestion UNVALIDATED or FAILED and investigate the role, identity, region, endpoint and pipeline. Do not invent a successful query result.

[source]
----
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe .\invoke_sample.py --endpoint $ProjectEndpoint --agent $AgentName --authorize-live-request
----

Tool execution is deterministic. The model explains the locally computed result. The model is not given customer content or a network-capable tool.

The provider flush result concerns local queue processing only. It is not an Agent 365 ingestion receipt.

Inspect only approved synthetic evidence. Do not print tokens to troubleshoot claims.

=== Negative export-disabled test

. Record the export-on version and stop its serving replicas through the approved Foundry version controls. Wait for old batches to drain.
. Deploy export off. Confirm the requested endpoint resolves to the export-off version. Do not assume that changing a local env file updates a running version.
. Send one synthetic request. Confirm actual model execution still succeeds and produces a new correlation ID.
. Verify no exporter construction or observability-token acquisition in the offline negative tests. For live evidence, use approved runtime/network instrumentation to confirm no outbound sample A365 export.
. Check the corresponding Agent 365 time window after the normal ingestion delay. Record no new matching trace, but do not use absence of rows alone as proof of zero export.
. Document any independent platform/AppInsights telemetry separately. It is not controlled by this application's SDK export switch.

[source]
----
azd env set POC_A365_EXPORT false
azd deploy synthetic-agent
azd ai agent show synthetic-agent --output json
# After confirming only the export-off version receives the test request:
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe .\invoke_sample.py --endpoint $ProjectEndpoint --agent $AgentName --authorize-live-request
----

Do not use a365LoggingEnabled=false as the negative test for this hosted SDK. Hosted SDK configuration has a separate scope. [H05]

Do not remove the role to simulate export off while leaving POC_A365_EXPORT=true. That tests authorization failure, not exporter disablement.

The unittest off case proves no fake exporter is constructed and no fake credential token is requested. It is not a live network observation.

=== Clean up only sample-owned resources

. Leave export off and stop every sample-owned export-on version. Remove only this unique sample agent and its versions through Foundry's approved management controls.
. Confirm the saved assignment's principalId, resourceId and appRoleId. Remove its appRoleAssignment ID only if this lab created it. Preserve pre-existing assignments.
. Delete only generated application or hosting resources proven by the deployment record to belong to this unique sample. Keep the existing Foundry account, project, model deployments, shared monitoring resources and unrelated identities.
. Do not run azd down against the shared existing project.
. Remove the local hosted_foundry\.azure environment, hosted_foundry\.venv and any operator-created build/evidence files only according to the approved retention policy. These are local artifacts, not proof of cloud cleanup.
. Record cleanup time and any remaining billable resource or retained evidence.

[source]
----
# Administrator-only example; use only the assignment ID created by this lab.
# Inspect the assignment with GET before authorizing DELETE.
$AssignmentId = '<saved-new-appRoleAssignment-ID>'
$AssignmentUri = "https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0/servicePrincipals/$AgentPrincipalId/appRoleAssignments/$AssignmentId"
az rest --method GET --uri $AssignmentUri
# Only after the exact target is reviewed:
az rest --method DELETE --uri $AssignmentUri
----

No broad resource-group deletion, tenant-wide setup, model deployment, resource opt-out, or cleanup action was performed by the authoring agent.

Passing local tests establishes mocked behavior and artifact consistency. Customer acceptance requires the separately captured deployment, identity, model, ingestion, negative-export and cleanup evidence.

== SDK runtime: scope, prerequisites, and local setup

This custom Python harness runs outbound-only. It has no server, incoming endpoint, deployment, publishing, or tenant provisioning.

The default mode is local_simulation. It reads bundled synthetic fixtures without loading SDK packages or using credentials.

The local fixture guard is application logic. It is not Microsoft Purview, Defender, Entra, or Agent 365 enforcement.

A model response is not proof of a security control. Record separate policy and downstream evidence.

=== Approve prerequisites

. Use a dedicated test tenant, blueprint, child agent identity, and approved Azure OpenAI deployment.
. Approve licence eligibility, model cost, regions, outbound access, operator roles, and telemetry retention.
. Use CPython 3.12 and a reviewed checkout. Keep credentials outside the checkout.
. Run each mode in a fresh process. Do not use an auto-instrumented Python launcher.
. Use public-cloud endpoints only. Sovereign clouds and custom gateways are not supported by this harness.
. Do not use production files, personal information, arbitrary prompts, or additional tools.

The runtime ignores .env files. It never searches external folders or downloads models.

The fixture allowlist rejects path traversal, symlinks, junctions, reparse points, altered content, and oversized files.

Copy the reviewed repository to a normal local directory if its path contains a reparse point.

Sources: T01, T02, T03.

=== Install the locked environment

. Open PowerShell in the repository root.
. Create an isolated environment. Do not activate it globally.
. Install the full version lock with binary wheels.
. Verify dependency consistency before running SDK modes.
. Stop if installation fails. Do not remove dependency checks or substitute unreviewed package versions.

[source]
----
py -3.12 -m venv .venv-runtime
& .\.venv-runtime\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install --only-binary=:all: -r requirements-runtime.lock.txt
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Runtime installation failed.' }
& .\.venv-runtime\Scripts\python.exe -m pip check
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Runtime dependency check failed.' }
----

Direct pins include microsoft-opentelemetry 1.3.9, OpenTelemetry 1.44.0, and azure-identity 1.25.3.

The transitive lock came from a successful Windows ARM64 CPython 3.12 installation on 18 September 2026.

The lock pins versions, not artifact hashes. Other operating systems and Python versions need separate validation.

Source: T01.

=== Run the no-network checks

. Run the default mode first. Confirm local_simulation, model_called=false, and ingestion_verified=false.
. Run the standard-library suite with site packages disabled.
. Run the installed SDK integration check in the isolated environment.
. Confirm eight metadata spans and zero attempted network operations.
. Run optional console instrumentation in a fresh process.

[source]
----
python -S -m runtime
python -S -m unittest discover -s tests\runtime -p test_runtime.py -q
& .\.venv-runtime\Scripts\python.exe tests\runtime\sdk_offline_check.py
& .\.venv-runtime\Scripts\python.exe -m runtime --instrument
----

The integration check blocks socket connections, DNS, datagrams, and subprocesses before SDK imports.

It checks real manual scope APIs, parent relationships, identity attributes, serialization, redaction, and the pinned transport adapter.

It constructs the Agent 365 exporter but never calls export or obtains a token.

The default mode remains offline with poisoned exporter variables. Instrumented modes reject inherited endpoint overrides.

The application does not initialize the unified distro, auto-instrumentation, resource detectors, Azure Monitor, or SDK self-telemetry.

== SDK runtime: run the bounded Azure OpenAI model

The --live-model flag authorizes actual Azure OpenAI requests. Model requests incur normal service costs.

Model authentication uses AzureCliCredential. Agent 365 authentication uses a separate blueprint-derived credential.

The model credential is not an Agent ID credential. Never reuse its token for Agent 365 ingestion.

=== Configure the model identity

. Choose an existing, approved Azure OpenAI account and a deployment that supports chat completions and function tools.
. Grant the operator Cognitive Services OpenAI User at that account, not subscription scope.
. Open a dedicated PowerShell session without inherited exporter, authority, or instrumentation overrides.
. Select AzureCloud in that dedicated Azure CLI profile. Sign in to the exact model tenant.
. Set the account endpoint, deployment name, and tenant GUID.

[source]
----
$env:AZURE_CONFIG_DIR = Join-Path (Get-Location) '.venv-runtime\azure-cli-profile'
az cloud set --name AzureCloud
$env:MODEL_TENANT_ID = Read-Host 'Model tenant GUID'
az login --tenant $env:MODEL_TENANT_ID
$env:AZURE_OPENAI_ENDPOINT = Read-Host 'Azure OpenAI endpoint, https://ACCOUNT.openai.azure.com'
$env:AZURE_OPENAI_DEPLOYMENT = Read-Host 'Existing tool-capable deployment name'
----

Azure CLI writes its own token cache in the dedicated profile. The runtime does not write a token cache.

The v1 model scope is https://ai.azure.com/.default. Dated API versions use https://cognitiveservices.azure.com/.default.

Tenant, public-cloud issuer, audience, and expiry are checked before the model request.

Decoded claims provide response consistency checks, not JWT signature validation or independent authorization.

TLS and the resource service remain the authentication boundaries.

Sources: T09, T10.

=== Run the model without tenant telemetry

. Run the approved-procedure scenario without --export-agent365.
. Confirm live_model and console_metadata_only. Record the trace and conversation identifiers.
. Confirm an allowed read_fixture decision and approved_reference_present=true.
. Run the restricted and injection scenarios separately.
. Treat missing tool decisions as inconclusive. The model can return a final answer without selecting a tool.
. Stop if the model does not support the request contract. Do not enable unsupported tools.

[source]
----
& .\.venv-runtime\Scripts\python.exe -m runtime --live-model --scenario approval --max-rounds 4
& .\.venv-runtime\Scripts\python.exe -m runtime --live-model --scenario restricted --max-rounds 4
& .\.venv-runtime\Scripts\python.exe -m runtime --live-model --scenario injection --max-rounds 4
----

Live-model mode enables metadata-only console instrumentation automatically. It does not enable Agent 365 export.

The default API version is v1. Use --api-version 2024-10-21 only with a compatible approved deployment.

Each run permits one to six model rounds, four tool calls per round, and 800 completion tokens per request.

The harness bounds request bodies, response bodies, arguments, fixture bytes, and conversation size.

Unknown tools receive unknown_tool. Restricted fixture reads receive application_policy.

The tool loop has no shell, write, delete, remote fetch, or arbitrary-file operation.

CLI results contain decisions and marker booleans, not model answers or fixture content.

A restricted denial proves only local application behavior. An injection result does not certify a Microsoft prompt shield.

Sources: T03, T09.

=== Understand the endpoint and error limits

* Only exact ACCOUNT.openai.azure.com hosts are accepted for model endpoints.
* User information, custom paths, query strings, fragments, and non-443 ports are rejected.
* Entra exchange uses login.microsoftonline.com and the configured tenant GUID.
* The application HTTP transport uses verified TLS, a 20-second socket timeout, and no inherited proxies.
* Redirects are rejected. Tokens and blueprint secrets are not forwarded to redirect destinations.
* There are no application HTTP retries or durable failed-payload storage.
* Azure CLI credential acquisition has a 20-second process timeout.
* HTTP and authentication errors use fixed messages. Response bodies and tokens are not printed.

The socket timeout is not a total run deadline. Stop a stalled test using normal process controls.

Model output remains nondeterministic. Marker booleans are observations, not policy verdicts.

== SDK runtime: provision a dedicated observability identity

Perform these steps only after tenant-owner approval. The runtime never performs provisioning or permission grants.

Create a real blueprint and child agent identity. A normal app registration does not demonstrate blueprint-derived Agent ID.

Entra objects alone do not establish Agent 365 registration. ONB-04 requires registry evidence and matching runtime telemetry.

Use only Agent365.Observability.OtelWrite application permission. Mail, files, chat, and Work IQ permissions are unnecessary.

The CLI setup-all workflow includes broad defaults. It is not the least-privilege procedure for this test.

=== Create the blueprint and child identity

. Open Microsoft Entra admin center. Select the approved tenant.
. Go to Entra ID > Agents > Agent blueprints > New agent blueprint (Preview).
. Create a dedicated blueprint. Assign an accountable owner and sponsor.
. Record the blueprint application ID and blueprint object ID separately.
. Open the blueprint credential settings. An Agent ID Administrator creates a short-lived development secret.
. Copy the secret value once into an approved secret manager. Do not copy its secret identifier.
. Go to Entra ID > Agents > Agent identities > New agent identity (Preview).
. Select the dedicated blueprint. Set an owner or sponsor as required.
. Record the child application ID and child service-principal object ID separately.
. Do not create an agent user, messaging endpoint, or published package for this outbound-only test.

A365_BLUEPRINT_ID means the blueprint appId. A365_AGENT_ID means the child appId.

Neither runtime variable accepts an object ID as a substitute.

Both values must be nonzero GUIDs and must differ.

The implementation fixes one tenant and one child identity. It is not a multitenant credential broker.

MODEL_TENANT_ID and A365_TENANT_ID must match during explicit export.

Production designs should use an approved federation or certificate strategy, not this development-secret adapter.

Sources: T04, T05, T06, T12.

=== ONB-04 gate: verify Agent 365 manager linkage

The blueprint's managerApplications must include the Agent 365 manager application.

Its application ID is e8be65d6-d430-4289-a665-51bf2a194bda. This is neither the child appId nor the observability resource.

The pinned CLI sets this property during blueprint creation. Registration-only does not create or repair the blueprint.

. Have the approved provisioning operator read the dedicated blueprint using its object ID.
. Check that the returned appId equals A365_BLUEPRINT_ID.
. Require managerApplications to contain e8be65d6-d430-4289-a665-51bf2a194bda.
. Save the property readback with tenant, blueprint object ID, blueprint appId, and UTC time.
. If absent or unreadable, record ONB-04 as BLOCKED. Do not infer linkage from the blueprint's display name.
. Have the platform owner approve a supported linkage repair or dedicated blueprint recreation before proceeding.
. For recreation, review the pinned creation manifest's managerApplications property. Reapprove the new identity and credential mappings.
. Do not use unrestricted setup to repair linkage or bypass the approved observability-only grants.

[source]
----
GET https://graph.microsoft.com/beta/applications/{BLUEPRINT_OBJECT_ID}?$select=id,appId,displayName,managerApplications

Expected managerApplications member:
"e8be65d6-d430-4289-a665-51bf2a194bda"
----

A portal-created blueprint can lack this linkage. Its successful creation is not a registry acceptance result.

The gate deliberately supplies no unverified PATCH recipe for this preview property.

Review Commands/SetupSubcommands/BlueprintSubcommand.cs and Constants/AuthenticationConstants.cs at the shipped source revision.

The relevant source revision is ea1579f37ff6ec549ecc78d0c57ef37f2fe2ccaf. The earlier 08e48a963dd879c6938d032e97e87ccbb03613de snapshot matches these contracts.

Sources: T16, T17.

=== ONB-04 gate: verify the registration-only CLI

. Install the pinned CLI into the isolated tool directory, not the global tool path.
. Run version and help commands only. These checks require no tenant sign-in.
. Require version 1.1.221 and the --agent-registration-only option in setup all help.
. Review the shipped orchestrator's registration-only branch before approving tenant execution.
. Confirm that blueprint creation, runtime permission grants, identity creation, endpoint registration, and project synchronization are skipped.
. Approve the separate provisioning client's registration permissions and operator access before execution.
. Use the Microsoft-managed CLI client only where tenant policy permits it.
. If using a custom client, review its consent requirements separately. Decline unexpected additional consent prompts.
. Stop if installed help or source behavior differs. Record ONB-04 as BLOCKED pending an approved registration path.

[source]
----
$env:DOTNET_CLI_TELEMETRY_OPTOUT = '1'
dotnet tool install Microsoft.Agents.A365.DevTools.Cli --version 1.1.221 --tool-path .\.venv-runtime\a365-cli
$cli = (Resolve-Path .\.venv-runtime\a365-cli\a365.exe).Path
& $cli --version
& $cli setup all --help
----

Local verification returned 1.1.221+ea1579f37f and confirmed --agent-registration-only. No tenant CLI command was executed.

The provisioning token requires AgentRegistration.ReadWrite.All. The registration implementation also reads the signed-in owner through /me.

These are provisioning-client permissions, not runtime permissions. Do not grant them to the child agent.

Registration-only can still check or request provisioning-client consent. It is not a promise of zero administrative side effects.

Do not run setup all without --agent-registration-only. Its default workflow is not observability-only.

Review Commands/SetupSubcommands/NonDwBlueprintSetupOrchestrator.cs, especially ExecuteAsync and ExecuteAgentIdentityAndRegistrationAsync.

The dry-run text can mention project settings. The reviewed execution branch skips synchronization for registration-only.

Sources: T13, T14, T15.

=== Read the existing child with an approved Graph client

Send this HTTP request through the approved provisioning client. It is not a PowerShell command.

Use the verified child object ID. Confirm appId and agentIdentityBlueprintId before creating the local registration configuration.

[source]
----
GET https://graph.microsoft.com/beta/servicePrincipals/{CHILD_SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_OBJECT_ID}?$select=id,appId,displayName,agentIdentityBlueprintId
----

=== Register only the approved existing identity

Proceed only after both ONB-04 gates pass and the tenant owner approves the registration operation.

Use a separate CLI working directory. Never point configuration synchronization at the runtime checkout.

The CLI's agenticAppId field contains the child service-principal object ID in this version.

A365_AGENT_ID remains the child application ID. Never copy agenticAppId directly into that runtime variable.

. Read the child service principal by object ID. Match its appId to A365_AGENT_ID.
. Match its agentIdentityBlueprintId to A365_BLUEPRINT_ID and confirm the approved tenant.
. Create the isolated CLI configuration below using verified identifiers, not display-name discovery.
. Set aiTeammate=false and useBlueprint=true. Omit --m365, messaging endpoints, and project paths.
. Preserve an existing agentRegistrationId if one is known. Do not discard it to force a duplicate registration.
. Run the registration-only dry-run. Require reuse of the exact existing child identity.
. If identity resolution fails, stop. Do not follow suggestions to run unrestricted setup.
. After approval, run only the registration-only command shown below.
. Decline unexpected provisioning, permission, repair, or consent prompts. Resolve them through the approved administrator.
. Save the resulting agentRegistrationId separately from blueprint and child identifiers.
. Read back that exact registration through the V2 endpoint. Compare identity, source, blueprint, owner, and manager fields.
. Require both registry readback and matching runtime trace evidence before recording ONB-04 as passed.

[source]
----
# Run from the repository root. These files initially contain identifiers only.
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
$cli = (Resolve-Path .\.venv-runtime\a365-cli\a365.exe).Path
$env:A365_TENANT_ID = Read-Host 'Verified tenant GUID'
$env:A365_BLUEPRINT_ID = Read-Host 'Verified blueprint appId, not object ID'
$env:A365_AGENT_ID = Read-Host 'Verified child appId, not object ID'
$registryDirectory = Join-Path (Get-Location) '.venv-runtime\a365-registration'
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $registryDirectory -Force | Out-Null
$configPath = Join-Path $registryDirectory 'a365.config.json'
$statePath = Join-Path $registryDirectory 'a365.generated.config.json'
if ((Test-Path $configPath) -or (Test-Path $statePath)) {
  throw 'Review existing registry state. Do not overwrite saved registration IDs.'
}
$childObjectId = Read-Host 'Verified child service-principal object ID, not appId'
$identityName = Read-Host 'Exact existing child display name'
$priorRegistration = Read-Host 'Existing registration ID, or leave empty when none exists'
$static = @{
  tenantId = $env:A365_TENANT_ID
  clientAppId = 'f54280f4-395e-4ea8-9e48-bf2d4952aa14'
  environment = 'prod'
  aiTeammate = $false
  useBlueprint = $true
  authMode = 's2s'
  agentIdentityDisplayName = $identityName
  agentDescription = 'Dedicated synthetic security PoC'
}
$state = @{
  agentBlueprintId = $env:A365_BLUEPRINT_ID
  agenticAppId = $childObjectId
}
if ($priorRegistration) { $state.agentRegistrationId = $priorRegistration }
$static | ConvertTo-Json | Set-Content $configPath -Encoding UTF8
$state | ConvertTo-Json | Set-Content $statePath -Encoding UTF8
Push-Location $registryDirectory
try {
  & $cli setup all --agent-registration-only --dry-run
  if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'The registration preview failed. Stop.' }
} finally {
  Pop-Location
}

# Review the preview and both identity gates before approving the write.
if ((Read-Host 'Type REGISTER to approve the reviewed tenant registration') -cne 'REGISTER') {
  throw 'Registration is not approved.'
}
Push-Location $registryDirectory
try {
  & $cli setup all --agent-registration-only
  if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Registration failed; ONB-04 remains blocked.' }
} finally {
  Pop-Location
}
----

Use the separate Graph readback below. Do not display provisioning tokens.

The V2 registration carries agentIdentityId and sourceAgentId equal to the verified child service-principal object ID.

agentIdentityBlueprintId must match the blueprint appId. Map the child object ID back to its runtime appId explicitly.

The CLI stamps managedByAppId=59eca866-2f46-40b8-96ff-63f663121ef9 for AgentX. Do not substitute the observability or managerApplications IDs.

managedByAppId and managerApplications represent different contracts. Neither is the runtime OAuth client_id.

The shipped GraphApiService.cs defines POST, GET, and DELETE under /beta/copilot/agentRegistrations.

This preview contract is source-verified. Tenant acceptance, registration readback, and Microsoft 365 inventory visibility remain customer validation gates.

An existing local ID or successful CLI exit is insufficient. An inconclusive readback must remain blocked.

For retries, review and reuse the existing isolated configuration. Do not rerun initial file creation or erase registration state.

Do not use the legacy agentRegistry instance endpoint as an interchangeable registration check.

Sources: T13, T14, T15, T17.

=== Read back the exact registration

Use the service-returned registration ID from the isolated CLI state.

Send this HTTP request through the approved provisioning client. It is not a PowerShell command.

Compare the returned identity, owner, source, blueprint, and manager fields before accepting registration.

[source]
----
GET https://graph.microsoft.com/beta/copilot/agentRegistrations/{AGENT_REGISTRATION_ID}
----

=== Grant the observability app role

Use one reviewed grant path. A required-permission declaration alone does not authorize the child identity.

The preferred single-child path grants the app role directly to the child service principal.

The resource application ID is 9b975845-388f-4429-889e-eab1ef63949c. It is not the resource service-principal object ID.

. Have an authorized Application Administrator perform the approved app-role assignment. Do not grant directory administration to the runtime.
. Use Microsoft Graph Explorer with the approved admin account and provisioning permissions.
. Retrieve the observability resource service principal using the GET request below.
. Require one matching resource. Select its enabled Agent365.Observability.OtelWrite role with Application in allowedMemberTypes.
. Copy that role's ID and the resource service-principal ID. Do not invent either GUID.
. Submit the POST using the child service-principal object ID, not the child appId.
. In Entra admin center, open Enterprise applications > All applications > the child identity > Permissions.
. Review the resulting application permission grant and administrator audit record.
. Remove elevated provisioning permissions from the operator when the approved setup is complete.

[source]
----
GET https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0/servicePrincipals?$filter=appId eq '9b975845-388f-4429-889e-eab1ef63949c'&$select=id,appId,appRoles

POST https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0/servicePrincipals/{CHILD_SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_OBJECT_ID}/appRoleAssignments
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "principalId": "{CHILD_SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_OBJECT_ID}",
  "resourceId": "{OBSERVABILITY_RESOURCE_SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_OBJECT_ID}",
  "appRoleId": "{OTELWRITE_APPLICATION_ROLE_ID}"
}
----

The admin assignment is the application-permission grant. Runtime token issuance must include the role.

Graph provisioning requires AppRoleAssignment.ReadWrite.All and Application.Read.All, subject to tenant policy and admin consent.

These permissions belong to the provisioning client. Never assign them to this agent runtime.

If the permission is unavailable, stop and contact the Agent 365 onboarding owner.

Sources: T02, T07, T11.

=== Alternative: blueprint admin consent and role inheritance

. Use this alternative only for a dedicated blueprint containing no unrelated grants.
. Open Entra ID > Agents > Agent blueprints > the dedicated blueprint > API permissions.
. Select Add a permission > APIs my organization uses. Find resource 9b975845-388f-4429-889e-eab1ef63949c.
. Select Application permissions > Agent365.Observability.OtelWrite > Add permissions.
. An authorized administrator selects Grant admin consent for the approved tenant.
. Verify Granted status. Review the blueprint principal's actual application-permission assignment.
. Configure role inheritance for only this resource. Use noScopes for delegated scopes.
. Use the blueprint object ID in the inheritance URL below. Do not use its appId.
. If an inheritance entry exists, review it and use PATCH on its resourceAppId path instead of duplicate POST.
. Confirm the child token receives OtelWrite before any telemetry test.

[source]
----
POST https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0/applications/microsoft.graph.agentIdentityBlueprint/{BLUEPRINT_OBJECT_ID}/inheritablePermissions
Content-Type: application/json
OData-Version: 4.0

{
  "resourceAppId": "9b975845-388f-4429-889e-eab1ef63949c",
  "inheritableScopes": {
    "@odata.type": "#microsoft.graph.noScopes",
    "kind": "none"
  },
  "inheritableRoles": {
    "@odata.type": "#microsoft.graph.allAllowedRoles",
    "kind": "allAllowed"
  }
}
----

Portal labels can vary during preview. Stop if these controls or permissions are unavailable.

allAllowedRoles inherits current and future granted roles for this resource. Review changes and keep this blueprint dedicated.

Do not configure all-resource inheritance or add Graph content permissions.

Sources: T02, T08.

== SDK runtime: explicit Agent 365 export and evidence

This phase sends metadata to the approved tenant. It requires both --live-model and --export-agent365.

At least one tenant user needs an assigned Microsoft 365 E7 or Microsoft Agent 365 licence.

A purchased SKU without user assignment can leave downstream ingestion unavailable.

SDK export status, HTTP success, and force_flush are not proof of downstream ingestion.

=== Run the approved export

. Complete the model-only test and the selected observability grant path first.
. Keep the model environment variables from the dedicated PowerShell session.
. Enter the observability tenant, blueprint appId, and child appId.
. Read the short-lived blueprint secret into memory. Do not use command-line literals or a .env file.
. Run one approved synthetic scenario. Remove the process environment secret in finally.
. Record UTC time, tenant, child appId, trace_id, conversation_id, scenario, and CLI exit status.

[source]
----
$env:A365_TENANT_ID = Read-Host 'Agent 365 tenant GUID'
$env:A365_BLUEPRINT_ID = Read-Host 'Blueprint application ID, not object ID'
$env:A365_AGENT_ID = Read-Host 'Child application ID, not object ID'
$secure = Read-Host 'Short-lived blueprint secret value' -AsSecureString
$pointer = [Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal]::SecureStringToBSTR($secure)
try {
  $env:A365_BLUEPRINT_CLIENT_SECRET = [Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal]::PtrToStringBSTR($pointer)
} finally {
  [Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal]::ZeroFreeBSTR($pointer)
  $secure.Dispose()
}
try {
  & .\.venv-runtime\Scripts\python.exe -m runtime --live-model --export-agent365 --scenario approval --max-rounds 4
  if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Runtime failed. Keep ingestion unverified.' }
} finally {
  Remove-Item Env:A365_BLUEPRINT_CLIENT_SECRET -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
}
----

Do not enable PowerShell transcription or SDK debug logging around credentials.

Environment variables contain plaintext in process memory. SecureString does not remove that limitation.

The runtime removes its inherited secret variable before model credential subprocesses and clears its single-entry token cache on exit.

Python cannot guarantee zeroization of immutable strings. Use short-lived credentials and terminate the process after testing.

Blueprint authentication requests api://AzureADTokenExchange/.default with fmi_path equal to the child appId.

The child exchanges that assertion for api://9b975845-388f-4429-889e-eab1ef63949c/.default.

The resource token must match tenant, child appId, public-cloud issuer, audience, expiry, and OtelWrite role. scp must be absent.

The cache holds one child token, refreshes before expiry, and rejects other agent or tenant identifiers.

The token exchange is protocol-based development code. Tenant issuance remains unverified until this approved live test.

Sources: T02, T06.

=== Check the metadata boundary

* Manual scopes cover invoke_agent, inference, and execute_tool.
* The pinned SDK uses InferenceOperationType.CHAT with value Chat. The metadata boundary normalizes it to chat.
* Identity fields include gen_ai.agent.id, microsoft.tenant.id, and microsoft.a365.agent.blueprint.id.
* Correlation fields include gen_ai.conversation.id, microsoft.session.id, trace ID, parent span ID, and timestamps.
* Optional usage fields contain token counts. Local decision fields contain a synthetic flag and an allowed boolean.
* The exporter removes input messages, output messages, tool arguments, tool results, user fields, events, links, and error descriptions.
* The resource and instrumentation scope are fixed. Console and Agent 365 export use the same redaction boundary.
* SDK self-telemetry, offline storage, automatic instrumentation, and Azure Monitor export remain disabled.

The SDK adapter uses the private 1.3.9 _Agent365Exporter transport seam. Any SDK upgrade requires review and integration tests.

Its only export destination is the exact tenant-and-child observabilityService traces URL.

No caller-provided telemetry endpoint is accepted.

Source: T01.

=== Perform the negative permission test

. Use the dedicated test identity. Do not revoke permissions from a shared blueprint.
. Record the selected grant and its assignment identifier before changing it.
. Remove the child OtelWrite assignment, or remove the corresponding blueprint grant and inherited authorization.
. Check that no alternative direct or inherited grant remains.
. Wait for permission propagation. Start a fresh runtime process without a cached agent token.
. Repeat the explicit export command with a newly entered blueprint secret.
. Expect safe token-claim rejection before model execution, or an authorization failure during export.
. Keep ingestion_verified=false. A local role check does not prove a backend 403.
. If a token still carries OtelWrite, record propagation as pending. Do not claim the negative test passed.
. Restore only the approved assignment. Rerun the positive test and collect separate evidence.

No permission-bypass flag is provided. Do not remove claim checks to force a backend test.

A model permission denial is a different test. It does not establish observability permission enforcement.

Sources: T02, T07.

=== Verify downstream trace evidence

. Confirm the runtime produced a nonzero trace_id and conversation_id.
. Expect export_attempted_ingestion_unverified. Preserve that status until downstream evidence exists.
. Open the approved Agent 365 or Microsoft Defender agent activity experience using a read-only analyst role.
. For ONB-04, first verify the registry readback, manager linkage, and child object-ID-to-appId mapping from the registration procedure.
. Select the exact tenant and child appId. Filter to the recorded UTC test window.
. Locate the root invoke_agent activity and matching conversation or trace identifiers where the surface exposes them.
. Confirm model and tool child spans where that surface supports them.
. For Sentinel evidence, use the configured connector and repository queries for the actual available schema.
. Capture the downstream record identifier, timestamp, identity match, and query or portal evidence.
. Record absence after the agreed observation window as blocked or inconclusive, not a product pass.
. Record policy enforcement separately. Trace visibility alone does not prove blocking, DLP, prompt shielding, or investigation coverage.

HTTP 200 can contain per-destination rejection despite partialSuccess.rejectedSpans=0.

The adapter rejects explicit rejected, unknown, or wholly unrouted responses without printing response bodies.

An empty successful response still leaves ingestion unverified.

There is no fabricated generic trace API, table name, or evidence record in this runtime.

Sources: T02, T03.

=== Clean up separate resources

. Stop the runtime and remove A365_BLUEPRINT_CLIENT_SECRET from the dedicated shell.
. Delete only the verified test registration using its saved V2 registration ID before removing the dedicated Entra identities.
. Confirm the registration is absent. Keep historical telemetry retention separate from registry deletion.
. Revoke the dedicated blueprint secret. Remove the approved test app-role assignment and inheritance entry.
. Delete only the dedicated child identity through Entra ID > Agents > Agent identities.
. Delete the dedicated blueprint only after confirming no other child identity depends on it.
. Remove temporary operator role activations and provisioning-client consents.
. Remove the dedicated model-user RBAC assignment if it was created for this test.
. Delete dedicated Azure resources only through the separate reviewed infrastructure cleanup procedure.
. Sign out of the dedicated Azure CLI profile. Delete that profile and the isolated venv after evidence approval.
. Apply approved retention to saved metadata and downstream telemetry. Identity deletion does not delete historical telemetry.
. Close the dedicated PowerShell session.

[source]
----
Remove-Item Env:A365_BLUEPRINT_CLIENT_SECRET -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
az logout
# Confirm AZURE_CONFIG_DIR identifies only the dedicated test profile.
Remove-Item Env:AZURE_CONFIG_DIR -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
Remove-Item Env:A365_TENANT_ID, Env:A365_BLUEPRINT_ID, Env:A365_AGENT_ID -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
Remove-Item Env:MODEL_TENANT_ID, Env:AZURE_OPENAI_ENDPOINT, Env:AZURE_OPENAI_DEPLOYMENT -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
----

No broad identity cleanup command is supplied. Do not delete shared blueprints or existing Azure accounts.

The reviewed V2 cleanup route is DELETE /beta/copilot/agentRegistrations/{AGENT_REGISTRATION_ID}, using the approved provisioning client.

Agent registrations, published packages, policies, and retained telemetry are separate cleanup surfaces if separately created.

Sources: T05, T15.

=== Verified status and remaining limits

Standard-library tests and installed SDK offline tests passed on Windows ARM64 CPython 3.12.

The tests use synthetic mocks or console spans. They make no model, tenant, ingestion, provisioning, or deployment calls.

Live model compatibility, Entra token issuance, tenant consent, backend routing, and downstream evidence still require approved customer validation.

CLI version and registration-only help were verified locally. No tenant registration, manager-link update, or registration deletion was executed.

ONB-04 remains blocked without actual registry evidence and matching runtime telemetry, even when Entra objects exist.

No product enforcement result, certification, or live tenant success is claimed.

== External provider lab: boundaries

These artifacts create actual provider resources only after operator authorization. No live provider deployment was performed.

Connected platforms synchronizes inventory. It does not deploy external agents into Teams or establish runtime telemetry. [V01]

ONB-10, actual Bedrock registry synchronization, is REQUIRED for the requested connected-platform category, despite preview status.

Provider readiness, ONB-05, does not satisfy ONB-10. Record the actual synchronized registry entry and matching provider AgentId.

ONB-07, actual Claude Managed Agents registry synchronization, is also REQUIRED. Missing beta or preview approval blocks agreed catalogue acceptance.

Use isolated test accounts, synthetic content, approved regions, and customer-approved spending limits.

=== Files and local validation

The AWS template creates one Bedrock Agent and one service role. It creates no access keys.

The Anthropic script uses Python standard-library HTTPS. No provider package installation is required.

Tests replace provider transports. They never authenticate or contact customer accounts.

. Run commands from the extracted project root.
. Run the standard unittest suite.
. Review the provider-specific eligibility gates before live testing.

* providers\aws\bedrock-agent.json
* providers\aws\Prepare-BedrockDemo.ps1
* providers\anthropic\managed_agent_demo.py
* tests\providers\test_bedrock_template.py
* tests\providers\test_managed_agent_demo.py
* tests\providers\test_provider_docs.py

[source]
----
python -B -m unittest discover -s tests\providers -p "test_*.py"
----

Local validation uses Python 3.12 and Windows PowerShell 5.1.

AWS CLI is unavailable in the build environment. CloudFormation service validation was not run.

JSON checks and mocked PowerShell tests do not prove AWS model access, service eligibility, or successful deployment.

Tests parse every provider command block with Windows PowerShell 5.1. They also check documented CLI arguments against the executable interfaces.

Mocked Anthropic tests do not prove beta access, workspace configuration, available credit, or successful inference.

== Amazon Bedrock: create an isolated inventory agent

This template targets AWS::Bedrock::Agent, now called Bedrock Agents Classic. It does not create an AgentCore runtime. [V02, V05]

Agents Classic closed to new customers on July 30, 2026. Existing Agents Classic customers can continue using it. [V05]

For new Agents Classic customers, mark this Classic path BLOCKED. An existing AWS account alone does not establish eligibility.

Do not silently substitute AgentCore. A separate AgentCore demonstration requires a separately scoped plan and explicit customer approval.

=== Model, role, and cost gates

Select an approved, available direct foundation model in the approved region. No model availability is assumed. [V04]

The script accepts a provider-qualified model ID or its same-region foundation-model ARN. It rejects profile ARNs and us., eu., apac., and global. routing prefixes.

Inference profiles require additional actions and resource scoping. This deliberately narrow template does not grant those permissions. [V03]

Bedrock inference can incur charges. The script prepares the agent but never invokes it.

. Confirm existing Agents Classic customer eligibility with the AWS account owner. Otherwise mark the Classic path BLOCKED.
. Confirm the chosen model, regional availability, quotas, model access, pricing, and purchasing terms.
. Use an already approved AWS CLI v2 session. This script does not sign in or switch accounts.
. Confirm the active principal can manage this stack, create its role, and pass that role to Bedrock.

* The service role permits only bedrock:InvokeModel on one foundation-model ARN.
* Trust permits bedrock.amazonaws.com, constrained by the current account and region.
* Bootstrap trust uses agent/*. A second reviewed update pins the returned AgentId.
* The role has no S3, Lambda, knowledge-base, credential, or connector permissions.
* The agent has no action groups, knowledge bases, external tools, or collaborators.

Do not confuse the agent service role with operator permissions or Microsoft connector credentials.

The customer must approve each permission set separately.

The model policy and trust conditions follow AWS documentation. [V03]

=== Preview, prepare, review, and execute

Replace every uppercase value before running the commands.

Use the same exact stack name throughout the lab. The example name is a365-bedrock-lab.

The normal command creates a change set, not deployed resources. This is still an authenticated AWS control-plane write. [V06]

WhatIf is fully local. It does not read credentials or call AWS.

. Run the WhatIf command first.
. Remove WhatIf to prepare a change set. Confirm the preparation prompt.
. Record its ChangeSetId. Inspect all changes, model parameters, IAM policy, tags, account, and region.
. Use -Apply -Confirm with that exact ChangeSetId. Confirm the execution prompt.
. Wait for a completed stack. Record AgentId, account, region, and stack name.
. Prepare a second change set using the same creation parameters.
. The second update automatically pins role trust to the existing AgentId. Review and execute that update separately.

* The script refuses account or approved-region mismatches.
* Execution requires explicit -Confirm, including execution previews. Omitted confirmation and -Confirm:$false are rejected before AWS calls, regardless of the caller's ConfirmPreference.
* It checks demo ownership tags before updating an existing deployed stack.
* It does not treat access-denied errors as a missing stack.
* Failed or incomplete change sets are never executed.
* An empty change set reports no changes, not a successful deployment.
* Failed stack waiters do not produce a successful AgentId result.

[source]
----
$common = @{
  StackName = 'a365-bedrock-lab'
  ExpectedAccountId = 'APPROVED_12_DIGIT_ACCOUNT'
  Region = 'APPROVED_REGION'
  ApprovedRegion = 'APPROVED_REGION'
  ConfirmClassicAccess = $true
}
$create = @{
  AgentName = 'a365-bedrock-lab'
  FoundationModel = 'APPROVED_DIRECT_MODEL_ID_OR_ARN'
  Owner = 'lab-team'
}
.\providers\aws\Prepare-BedrockDemo.ps1 @common @create -WhatIf
# After approval: prepare only.
.\providers\aws\Prepare-BedrockDemo.ps1 @common @create -Confirm
# After reviewing the returned change set:
.\providers\aws\Prepare-BedrockDemo.ps1 @common -Apply -Confirm -ChangeSetId 'RETURNED_CHANGE_SET_ARN'
# After CREATE_COMPLETE: prepare the exact-AgentId trust update.
.\providers\aws\Prepare-BedrockDemo.ps1 @common @create -Confirm
# Review this new change set before execution.
.\providers\aws\Prepare-BedrockDemo.ps1 @common -Apply -Confirm -ChangeSetId 'RETURNED_TRUST_UPDATE_CHANGE_SET_ARN'
----

An unexecuted CREATE change set can leave an empty REVIEW_IN_PROGRESS stack.

A failed change set remains available for investigation and exact-ID removal.

Do not retry after an uncertain timeout without inspecting the exact stack and change set.

AWS calls use 15-second connection and 30-second read timeouts. AWS CLI waiters have finite retry limits.

AWS error output is suppressed. Inspect the exact stack or change set in the approved AWS interface.

DescribeChangeSet does not return ChangeSetType. The script uses its preparation marker and verified stack state. [V07]

Changes to AWS or model access can still fail during execution. Local validation is not a deployment guarantee.

=== Hand off for manual Microsoft connection

Use the printed AgentId to verify the inventory entry after manual synchronization.

ONB-10 is REQUIRED. A created provider agent without actual registry synchronization does not pass the connected-platform demonstration.

The Microsoft wizard needs separate customer-approved AWS credentials and the deployment region. [V01]

This template does not create, retrieve, print, store, or rotate those credentials.

Microsoft's documented permission list includes invocation and deletion. Do not describe this connector as read-only. [V01]

. Obtain separate customer approval for the documented connector permission list.
. Have the authorized administrator enter credentials directly in the Microsoft wizard.
. Use the platform onboarding chapter for the portal connection and synchronization procedure.
. Perform the actual Bedrock registry synchronization. Compare the synchronized provider AgentId with the stack output.
. Record connection identity, account, region, synchronization status, timestamp, registry entry, and matching AgentId as ONB-10 evidence.
. Mark ONB-10 BLOCKED if eligibility, permissions, or preview availability prevents synchronization. Do not report provider readiness as PASS.

* bedrock:ListAgents
* bedrock:GetAgent
* bedrock:ListAgentAliases
* bedrock:GetAgentAlias
* bedrock:ListAgentVersions
* bedrock:GetAgentVersion
* bedrock:InvokeModel
* bedrock:InvokeAgent
* bedrock:InvokeInlineAgent
* bedrock:DeleteAgent

AgentCore synchronization has additional permissions. Do not grant them for this Agents Classic-only lab. [V01]

Coordinate any connector deletion test. It can remove the underlying agent and cause CloudFormation drift.

No Microsoft API or connector implementation is simulated by these artifacts.

=== Remove only the approved lab stack

Cleanup is optional and destructive. Obtain approval before deleting the exact lab stack.

Stop if the account, region, stack name, or ownership tags differ.

Do not delete stacks by prefix or enumerate stacks for deletion.

. Remove the Microsoft platform connection through the documented manual procedure.
. Have the credential owner revoke the dedicated connector credentials.
. Run the guarded cleanup below with the same approved account and region.
. Inspect DELETE_FAILED stack events instead of disabling resource-in-use protections.

[source]
----
$ExpectedAccountId = 'APPROVED_12_DIGIT_ACCOUNT'
$Region = 'APPROVED_REGION'
$ApprovedRegion = 'APPROVED_REGION'
$StackName = 'a365-bedrock-lab'
if ($ExpectedAccountId -notmatch '^[0-9]{12}$' -or $Region -cne $ApprovedRegion) { throw 'Invalid target.' }
$raw = aws sts get-caller-identity --region $Region --output json --no-cli-pager
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Identity check failed.' }
if (($raw | ConvertFrom-Json).Account -cne $ExpectedAccountId) { throw 'Wrong AWS account.' }
$raw = aws cloudformation describe-stacks --stack-name $StackName --region $Region --output json --no-cli-pager
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Stack inspection failed.' }
$stack = ($raw | ConvertFrom-Json).Stacks[0]
if ($stack.StackName -cne $StackName) { throw 'Wrong stack.' }
$project = @($stack.Tags | Where-Object { $_.Key -ceq 'Project' -and $_.Value -ceq 'Agent365SecurityPoC' })
$managed = @($stack.Tags | Where-Object { $_.Key -ceq 'ManagedBy' -and $_.Value -ceq 'provider-demo' })
if ($project.Count -ne 1 -or $managed.Count -ne 1) { throw 'Ownership check failed.' }
$stack | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 10
if ((Read-Host "Type DELETE $StackName to confirm") -cne "DELETE $StackName") { throw 'Cancelled.' }
aws cloudformation delete-stack --stack-name $stack.StackId --region $Region --no-cli-pager
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'Delete request failed.' }
aws cloudformation wait stack-delete-complete --stack-name $stack.StackId --region $Region --no-cli-pager
