=== ONB-01: Onboard an authenticated Copilot Studio agent

Agent group: copilot_studio. Security layer: onboarding.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Commercial core. Requires publishing entitlement. Keep NOT_RUN until both live phases have evidence..

.Prerequisites
* Use a disposable Power Platform environment and licensed maker.
* Provide AI Administrator, u-allow, and a separate signed-out browser session.

.Prepare the test
. Create Studio Guide with a name under 42 characters.
. Use Authenticate with Microsoft; publish only to the approved test group.
. Load public-procedure.txt as approved synthetic knowledge.

.Positive phase
. Locate the draft identity and registry entry before publication.
. Publish, install as u-allow, and request the public approval code in a new Teams conversation.

.Negative phase
. Open the published channel in the signed-out browser session.
. Attempt the public request without authentication; verify sign-in is required before agent content is delivered.

.Expected result
* Creation registers an identity; authenticated authorized use returns POC-APPROVAL-6142.
* The supported Microsoft channel requires authentication; the signed-out session receives no agent knowledge response.

.Evidence
* Capture draft and published IDs, deployment scope, authentication settings, response, and sign-in requirement.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, actor identity, session IDs, and artifact hashes for both phases.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require hashed live-product evidence and verified response side effects.
* Registry presence alone does not establish inspection or a specific Activity-page metric.

.Measured value
* Measure onboarding minutes and deployed-user count.
* Count unauthenticated access attempts prevented.

.Restore the test state
. Remove test deployment and knowledge; restore prior access settings.
. Delete only the disposable agent after evidence collection.

Sources: CM1, CM2, CE1.

=== ONB-02: Deploy and publish a Foundry hosted agent

Agent group: foundry_hosted. Security layer: onboarding.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires supported hosted-agent region, quota, publication permissions, and confirmed tenant availability. Missing prerequisites mean BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide a disposable Foundry project, supported model, and authorized deployment operator.
* Provide Foundry User and Bot Service permissions for publication; exclude u-deny from project access.

.Prepare the test
. Deploy hosted_foundry with export off through its documented azd workflow.
. Select the tested active version; publish using Just you for u-allow.
. Record resource identity separately from the published agent identity.

.Positive phase
. Invoke the deployed endpoint with the exact synthetic:add request.
. Open the published Teams agent as u-allow; match its registry entry and published identity.

.Negative phase
. Open the Just you publication link as u-deny without project authorization.
. Attempt invocation; inspect authorization failure and verify no completed inference for that request.

.Expected result
* Authorized invocation runs the selected hosted version.
* Sharing a Just you link does not grant invocation access.
* Publication does not automatically configure hosted Agent 365 SDK telemetry.

.Evidence
* Capture deployment version, project RBAC, publication identity, invocation response, and rejected request.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, caller identity, request/session IDs, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live-product artifacts and matching endpoint side effects.
* Do not substitute Application Insights traces for Agent 365 observability.

.Measured value
* Measure deployment and publication minutes.
* Count unauthorized invocations denied and identities mapped.

.Restore the test state
. Remove the test publication and restore project role assignments.
. Stop and delete only test resources after evidence collection.

Sources: CM1, CM3, CI3.

=== ONB-03: Onboard an entitled partner-integrated agent

Agent group: partner_integrated. Security layer: onboarding.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Use an available entitled offering. Approve Frontier when the selected offering requires it. Missing entitlement or approval means BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Select one licensed offering marked Registered and Observable in the current partner table.
* Exclude coming-soon offerings. Confirm the exact provider setup and any required Frontier approval.

.Prepare the test
. Record the exact offering, publisher, permissions, and supported integration capabilities.
. AI Administrator installs the agent for u-allow only.
. Create public and restricted synthetic provider records; deny u-deny access to the restricted record.

.Positive phase
. Complete the documented provider connection as u-allow.
. Read the authorized synthetic record; correlate the registry identity and available provider telemetry.

.Negative phase
. Authenticate through an approved test account without restricted-record permission.
. Request that record; inspect provider authorization and verify its contents remain undisclosed.

.Expected result
* The integrated offering serves authorized provider data.
* Provider permissions deny the restricted read.
* Observable status does not prove inspection of every provider-internal operation.

.Evidence
* Capture offering entitlement, consent, registry identity, provider ACLs, responses, and provider access decisions.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, account and session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live provider decisions and actual data-access outcomes.
* Record uncovered provider operations without claiming Microsoft runtime protection.

.Measured value
* Measure partner onboarding minutes and mapped permission count.
* Count tested provider operations with usable correlation.

.Restore the test state
. Disconnect the provider test account and remove test records.
. Remove deployment and test grants; restore previous publisher settings.

Sources: CI2, CM3, CI4.

=== ONB-04: Register and instrument an SDK-enabled agent

Agent group: sdk_enabled. Security layer: onboarding.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires the selected SDK, agent identity, and observability consent. Do not require Frontier mailboxes or Work IQ preview..

.Prerequisites
* Provide developer access, tenant consent approval, and a disposable SDK agent identity.
* Use the included custom runtime with pinned SDK dependencies and synthetic read-only tools.

.Prepare the test
. Configure registration and observability explicitly for agent-sdk.
. Grant only the documented telemetry permission; record effective grants.
. Use public-procedure.txt; omit secrets from exported traces.

.Positive phase
. Start an authenticated agent-sdk session and invoke the synthetic read-only tool.
. Locate its registry identity and exported Agent 365 trace using the recorded correlation ID.

.Negative phase
. Disable only the test export configuration; start a fresh synthetic session.
. Verify runtime completion separately; check that no new Agent 365 trace arrives within the documented collection window.

.Expected result
* Configured export produces correlated Agent 365 telemetry.
* Uninstrumented execution can still run; registry registration does not imply content collection.

.Evidence
* Capture sample revision, export settings, grants, runtime results, and bounded telemetry searches.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, identity, session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live runtime and service evidence.
* An empty search requires a bounded window and a working positive ingestion control.

.Measured value
* Measure instrumentation minutes and telemetry delivery delay.
* Count tool executions with end-to-end correlation.

.Restore the test state
. Restore export settings and original grants.
. Remove the disposable registration and runtime after preserving evidence.

Sources: CI3, CI4.

=== ONB-05: Prepare Bedrock onboarding and verify provider authorization

Agent group: connected_bedrock. Security layer: onboarding.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Required live Bedrock provider prerequisite. ONB-10 separately requires actual Agent 365 preview registry synchronization..

.Prerequisites
* Provide an isolated AWS account with existing Agents Classic eligibility, approved region, model access, and AWS operator.
* Agents Classic closed to new customers on 30 July 2026. Missing eligibility means BLOCKED.
* Approve an AgentCore alternative separately; do not substitute another runtime without changing the case.

.Prepare the test
. Create and prepare a synthetic Bedrock agent without corporate knowledge or action groups.
. Create a scoped test invoker and a denied control principal.
. Review documented connector permissions, including DeleteAgent; do not describe them as read-only.

.Positive phase
. Invoke the prepared agent through the authorized AWS test principal.
. Record its ID, region, version, and metadata for the supported Bedrock connection path.

.Negative phase
. Invoke the same agent through the principal without invocation permission.
. Inspect AWS denial and confirm that no model response reaches the denied principal.

.Expected result
* Provider onboarding produces a working authorized agent.
* AWS authorization denies the control principal.
* This baseline proves neither registry synchronization nor Microsoft runtime inspection.

.Evidence
* Capture Bedrock preparation, provider policy, successful response, and denied request.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, AWS principal, request/session IDs, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live AWS evidence; ONB-10 separately validates the Microsoft preview connection.
* Do not count this baseline as completed Agent 365 synchronization.

.Measured value
* Measure provider preparation minutes and reviewed connector permissions.
* Count authorized and denied provider invocations.

.Restore the test state
. Remove test principals and restore provider permissions.
. Delete the disposable Bedrock agent; revoke unused connector credentials.

Sources: CI1, CE2.

=== ONB-06: Discover Codex CLI, Claude Code, and OpenClaw on Windows

Agent group: shadow_local. Security layer: onboarding.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Commercial Windows discovery requires MDE P2, supported onboarding, active Defender Antivirus, and real-time protection..

.Prerequisites
* Use onboarded disposable Windows devices and a nonadministrator test user.
* Install supported Codex CLI, Claude Code, and OpenClaw versions through their official distributions.

.Prepare the test
. Record each tool, device, and user identifier. Use only empty synthetic projects.
. Record the three members under the fixed test-set alias SHD-SET.
. Leave runtime protection unchanged; discovery needs no added discovery script.

.Positive phase
. Run one harmless session in each approved tool.
. Find each Local agents inventory record and matching AgentsInfo record with Platform equal to LocalAgents.

.Negative phase
. For each tool, repeat under the same user and device in a second synthetic project.
. Compare inventory keys; reject any interpretation that creates one agent identity per project.

.Expected result
* Discovery associates each supported agent type with its executing user and device.
* A second project does not imply another runtime Entra identity or independent discovered agent.

.Evidence
* Capture each tool version, device onboarding, inventory record, and both project session times.
* Capture discovery mode/scope, UTC times, user/device correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live artifacts for all three tools. Missing tool evidence prevents this case from passing.
* Discovery does not prove prompt inspection, runtime blocking, or Entra registration.

.Measured value
* Measure discovery delay and discovered device/user coverage.
* Count distinct user-device-agent combinations without project duplication.

.Restore the test state
. Remove only test projects and provider sessions.
. Restore the device snapshot or uninstall the test tool without changing production onboarding.

Sources: CP1.

=== ONB-07: Synchronize a Claude Managed Agents workspace

Agent group: connected_anthropic. Security layer: onboarding.

Priority: P2. Required: Yes. Availability: Required connected-platform preview demonstration. Missing beta access, a non-default workspace, or preview approval means BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Use a disposable non-default Anthropic workspace and workspace-scoped API key.
* Approve provider beta data handling before sending synthetic content.

.Prepare the test
. Create an agent, environment, and session using the official Managed Agents quickstart.
. AI Administrator selects Anthropic Claude Managed Agents in Connected platforms.
. Store credentials securely; exclude keys from evidence.

.Positive phase
. Validate the approved workspace credentials and manually select Sync agents.
. Compare the provider agent ID and metadata with the synchronized registry entry.

.Negative phase
. Attempt validation using a revoked disposable workspace key.
. Confirm validation fails and no new successful synchronization is attributed to that attempt.

.Expected result
* Valid workspace access synchronizes supported inventory.
* Invalid credentials prevent the attempted connection.
* The connection does not import every transcript or register local Claude Code sessions.

.Evidence
* Capture workspace alias, provider ID, validation status, sync results, and failed validation.
* Capture UTC times, connection mode/scope, administrator identity, run IDs, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live connection evidence; unavailable beta access remains BLOCKED.
* Do not claim scheduled synchronization or runtime content inspection.

.Measured value
* Measure connection minutes, sync delay, and imported-agent count.
* Count rejected invalid-credential attempts.

.Restore the test state
. Remove the test connection and restore previous registry state where supported.
. Delete test sessions and environments through the supported provider procedures.
. Archive the test agent only after separate approval. Agent archival is irreversible.
. Revoke the disposable provider key after cleanup.

Sources: CI1, CI3.

=== ONB-08: Verify the unsupported OpenAI connector boundary

Agent group: openai_boundary. Security layer: onboarding.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Boundary check against the live provider selector and current documentation. This is not OpenAI platform onboarding..

.Prerequisites
* Provide read access to Connected platforms and the approved provider support register.
* Use a separate registered SDK agent or discovered Codex record as an integration control.

.Prepare the test
. Record the current provider list and its verification date.
. Do not enter OpenAI credentials into an unrelated provider connection.

.Positive phase
. Open the supported-provider selector and identify Amazon Bedrock.
. Locate the separate SDK or Codex control record and record its actual integration mechanism.

.Negative phase
. Check whether an OpenAI Connected platforms option exists.
. Compare the control record with connection results; reject claims that SDK or Codex registration proves an OpenAI connector.

.Expected result
* Documented providers appear through their own connection paths.
* The verified provider list omits OpenAI; no OpenAI platform connection is fabricated.
* If support changes, stop and rebaseline this boundary before recording acceptance.

.Evidence
* Capture the live provider selector, control-record origin, and dated support source.
* Capture UTC times, connection scope/mode, administrator identity, record correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live inventory evidence.
* Record unsupported platform coverage explicitly; do not infer it from model selection.

.Measured value
* Count unsupported onboarding assumptions removed.
* Measure assessment minutes and verified integration-path coverage.

.Restore the test state
. Close the unsaved connection wizard.
. Restore any temporary view filters; do not modify existing connections.

Sources: CI1, CI3, CP1.

=== ONB-09: Verify explicit hosted-agent SDK telemetry

Agent group: foundry_hosted. Security layer: observability.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires hosted SDK instrumentation and Agent365.Observability.OtelWrite. Missing permissions or service availability mean BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide the published disposable hosted agent and its agentIdentityId.
* Authorize an operator to inspect and restore observability app-role assignments.

.Prepare the test
. Package Agent 365 SDK export explicitly in the hosted agent.
. Grant Agent365.Observability.OtelWrite against Agent365Observability.
. Record resource logging settings separately from hosted SDK settings.

.Positive phase
. Invoke synthetic:add on the instrumented hosted version after permission propagation.
. Find the correlated Agent 365 trace and capture the successful runtime response separately.

.Negative phase
. Deploy the test version with its Agent 365 exporter disabled. Stop older export-on versions.
. Invoke synthetic:add and compare runtime success with bounded Agent 365 telemetry searches.

.Expected result
* Explicit SDK configuration produces Agent 365 telemetry.
* Hosting and Application Insights alone do not prove Agent 365 collection.
* Resource a365LoggingEnabled is not a reliable sole hosted-agent export switch.

.Evidence
* Capture deployed versions, exporter configuration, app-role assignment, runtime responses, and both telemetry searches.
* Capture UTC times, logging mode/scope, identity, invocation correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live runtime and Agent 365 evidence.
* Use a successful ingestion control before interpreting missing export.
* Application Insights export is not required by this sample. Do not claim that the sample sends those traces.

.Measured value
* Measure export delay and instrumented-hosted-version count.
* Count telemetry gaps detected before customer testing.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the original hosted version and exporter configuration.
. Restore previous role assignments and resource logging settings.

Sources: CI3, CI4.

=== ONB-10: Connect Bedrock inventory without claiming runtime inspection

Agent group: connected_bedrock. Security layer: onboarding.

Priority: P1. Required: Yes. Availability: Required registry-sync preview demonstration. Missing preview approval, access, or documented AWS permissions means BLOCKED; full requested acceptance remains blocked..

.Prerequisites
* Complete ONB-05 and approve the connector credential permissions.
* Provide AI Administrator and AWS operator access for the disposable region.

.Prepare the test
. Create a Bedrock connection using the approved region and secure credentials.
. Record import selection; isolate this agent from unrelated AWS inventory.

.Positive phase
. Validate credentials, save, and select Sync agents.
. Change the disposable provider description; manually resync and compare registry metadata.

.Negative phase
. Run a synthetic provider conversation without adding observability instrumentation.
. Search the agreed Agent 365 telemetry window; compare available metadata with absent transcript evidence.

.Expected result
* Manual synchronization imports supported provider metadata.
* Registry-only integration does not establish prompt or tool inspection.
* Missing runtime evidence is a documented coverage gap, not a detector pass.

.Evidence
* Capture provider metadata, connection scope, both sync results, and bounded telemetry searches.
* Capture UTC times, import mode/scope, identities, provider session and sync IDs, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live provider and Microsoft connection artifacts.
* ONB-05 provider readiness cannot replace this required synchronization case.
* Do not infer automated scheduling, Teams deployment, or runtime blocking.

.Measured value
* Measure sync minutes and metadata-field coverage.
* Count connected agents without demonstrated runtime telemetry.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the provider description and remove the disposable connection.
. Revoke test credentials; remove imported test entries only through supported actions.

Sources: CI1, CI3, CI4.

=== M365-01: Enforce scoped agent distribution

Agent group: copilot_studio. Security layer: m365_lifecycle.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Commercial administration with supported publication and licensed test users..

.Prerequisites
* Provide AI Administrator and two test groups with distinct membership.
* Use the published Studio test agent and supported Microsoft channel.

.Prepare the test
. Snapshot existing deployment settings.
. Install the agent only for group poc-agent-allowed; exclude u-deny.

.Positive phase
. Sign in as included u-allow after documented propagation.
. Open the deployed agent and complete a public-procedure request.

.Negative phase
. Sign in as excluded u-deny and open the same agent link.
. Attempt a conversation; verify access remains unavailable and no response is delivered.

.Expected result
* The included user can use the approved deployment.
* The excluded user cannot gain deployment access from a shared link.

.Evidence
* Capture group membership, deployment settings, allowed response, and denied access.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, user identity, session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live deployment decisions and user-visible outcomes.
* Allow recorded propagation before judging stale availability.

.Measured value
* Measure deployment propagation minutes and eligible-user count.
* Count excluded-user access attempts prevented.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the previous deployment audience and group membership.
. Remove temporary test installations.

Sources: CM2, CM3.

=== M365-02: Separate Foundry admin Block from compute Stop

Agent group: foundry_hosted. Security layer: m365_lifecycle.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires supported published Foundry deployment and authorized lifecycle controls..

.Prerequisites
* Provide AI Administrator and Foundry operator for a disposable published hosted agent.
* Prepare both Copilot Chat access and an authorized direct endpoint control.

.Prepare the test
. Snapshot agent availability and deployment running state.
. Confirm both access paths work before changing lifecycle settings.

.Positive phase
. Invoke the running agent through Copilot Chat and its authorized direct endpoint.
. Record the successful responses and current compute state.

.Negative phase
. Apply admin Block; retry both paths and inspect Copilot availability separately from compute.
. Apply Foundry Stop; retry the direct endpoint and verify the deployment no longer executes.

.Expected result
* Admin Block removes Copilot Chat availability but does not stop Foundry compute.
* Stop halts the underlying deployment; the direct request must not complete normally.

.Evidence
* Capture lifecycle actions, channel responses, direct endpoint responses, and compute state.
* Capture UTC times, action mode/scope, operator and caller identities, request IDs, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require actual channel and compute outcomes.
* Do not claim Block contains direct endpoint execution.

.Measured value
* Measure channel-block and compute-stop propagation minutes separately.
* Count invocation paths affected by each action.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the original running state and admin availability.
. Retest only the paths previously enabled.

Sources: CM3.

=== M365-03: Separate reader visibility from management permission

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: m365_lifecycle.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Commercial role-based administration. Use supported least-privilege roles..

.Prerequisites
* Provide a read-only test administrator and an AI Administrator.
* Use an unneeded disposable Studio agent.

.Prepare the test
. Export role assignments and agent state.
. Ensure the reader has no inherited management role.

.Positive phase
. View the agent registry entry as the reader.
. Change an approved test setting as AI Administrator and verify the saved state.

.Negative phase
. Attempt the same management operation as the reader through the supported UI.
. Inspect missing or denied action; confirm the agent setting remains unchanged.

.Expected result
* The reader can inspect permitted inventory.
* Only the authorized administrator changes the tested setting.

.Evidence
* Capture role assignments, visible inventory, authorized change, and denied management attempt.
* Capture UTC times, RBAC mode/scope, actor identity, action correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live authorization evidence and saved-state comparison.
* Viewing risk summaries does not imply access to underlying Purview cases.

.Measured value
* Count verified role boundaries.
* Measure privilege-review minutes and unnecessary grants identified.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the original agent setting.
. Remove only temporary role assignments.

Sources: CM1, CM2.

=== M365-04: Retire an owned disposable agent

Agent group: copilot_studio. Security layer: m365_lifecycle.

Priority: P1. Required: Yes. Availability: Use supported lifecycle actions and retained audit access..

.Prerequisites
* Provide AI Administrator, a named test owner, and a disposable published agent.
* Approve test-agent retirement; preserve baseline evidence first.

.Prepare the test
. Record agent ID, owner, audience, permissions, and connected test resources.
. Choose supported block or removal actions; do not delete shared resources.

.Positive phase
. Have the recorded owner identify the agent and its dependencies.
. Run a public request before retirement and preserve its response.

.Negative phase
. Retire the test deployment through the approved lifecycle action.
. Retry a fresh user session; inspect unavailable access and residual resource permissions.

.Expected result
* The active owned deployment serves its approved user.
* Retirement prevents the tested channel access.
* Residual credentials or runtime resources remain explicit cleanup work, not assumed deleted.

.Evidence
* Capture owner mapping, dependency list, lifecycle action, retry result, and residual-grant review.
* Capture UTC times, lifecycle mode/scope, actor identity, session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live channel evidence and a completed residual-resource review.
* Channel retirement alone cannot prove provider compute shutdown.

.Measured value
* Measure retirement minutes and residual permission count.
* Count inventoried agents with an accountable owner.

.Restore the test state
. Restore reversible baseline settings if the agent must remain.
. Otherwise remove only approved disposable grants and resources.

Sources: CM2, CM3, CE4.

=== M365-05: Approve and block a supported BYO MCP server

Agent group: sdk_enabled. Security layer: m365_lifecycle.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: BYO MCP preview. Requires approved supported client and reachable endpoint. Missing prerequisites mean BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Use Copilot Studio, VS Code, Claude Code, or GitHub Copilot CLI as documented.
* Do not use Foundry or Microsoft 365 declarative agents for this BYO path.

.Prepare the test
. Register one internet-reachable synthetic read-only MCP server through the approved workflow.
. Record server approval state and client policy scope.
. Accept that republishing versions and deleting BYO servers are currently unsupported.

.Positive phase
. Approve the test server for the supported client.
. Invoke its harmless read operation and verify the server receives the correlated request.

.Negative phase
. Apply the documented runtime server block.
. Repeat the invocation; inspect the client decision and absence of a completed server operation.

.Expected result
* Approved access permits the scoped tool call.
* Server blocking prevents the supported runtime operation.
* Tool metadata alone does not demonstrate execution.

.Evidence
* Capture approval/block settings, client results, and server access logs for both phases.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, identity, tool correlation IDs, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live client and server evidence.
* Unavailable preview controls remain BLOCKED; local tool refusal is not Microsoft policy evidence.

.Measured value
* Measure approval and block propagation minutes.
* Count controlled tool paths and unsupported clients.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the previous server policy and client configuration.
. Disable disposable endpoints and revoke credentials; document retained undeletable registry metadata.

Sources: CM4, CD3.

=== ENTRA-01: Apply Conditional Access to an on-behalf-of user

Agent group: sdk_enabled. Security layer: entra_identity.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires Entra P1/P2, Agent 365, supported OBO authentication, and test-only Conditional Access scope..

.Prerequisites
* Provide Conditional Access Administrator and a disposable OBO agent.
* Exclude emergency accounts; use u-allow and u-deny with synthetic resource access.

.Prepare the test
. Create a test policy targeting u-deny and the exact protected resource.
. Record that the evaluated subject is the user, not the autonomous agent identity.

.Positive phase
. Acquire a fresh OBO token as u-allow outside the blocking scope.
. Read the public test resource and inspect the user's sign-in decision.

.Negative phase
. Enable the scoped block and acquire a fresh OBO token as u-deny.
. Attempt the same read; inspect Conditional Access failure and resource access logs.

.Expected result
* The allowed user receives a token and reads the resource.
* The blocked user's token request fails; the denied request does not read resource content.

.Evidence
* Capture policy export, user sign-ins, CA results, and resource access outcomes.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, user and agent IDs, correlation IDs, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require Entra decisions and downstream evidence.
* Do not attribute OBO policy evaluation to an autonomous agent.

.Measured value
* Measure CA decision and enforcement propagation minutes.
* Count OBO resource paths covered by user policy.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the original policy state and test-user assignments.
. Remove temporary sessions and test resource grants.

Sources: CE2.

=== ENTRA-02: Apply Conditional Access to an autonomous agent identity

Agent group: sdk_enabled. Security layer: entra_identity.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires supported agent-identity application flow and licensed agent Conditional Access. Otherwise record BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide an actual Entra Agent ID, not only an ordinary app registration.
* Provide Conditional Access Administrator and a synthetic protected API.

.Prepare the test
. Target agent-sdk and the test API in a dedicated agent policy.
. Record application permissions and a separate excluded control identity.

.Positive phase
. Acquire a fresh application token while the test blocking policy is disabled.
. Call the synthetic API and record the evaluated agent identity.

.Negative phase
. Enable the agent-specific block and request a fresh application token.
. Attempt the API call; inspect token denial and absence of a successful protected operation.

.Expected result
* The autonomous flow evaluates the agent identity.
* The enabled scoped block denies fresh token issuance for the target resource.

.Evidence
* Capture identity type, policy export, sign-in evaluation, and API operation logs.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, agent ID, token-request correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live Entra and resource evidence.
* Do not substitute API-key calls or ordinary user sign-ins.

.Measured value
* Count autonomous identities and resource paths covered.
* Measure policy propagation and denied-operation count.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the original policy state and application grants.
. Remove the synthetic API and temporary identity when approved.

Sources: CE2, CE4, CI3.

=== ENTRA-03: Target an agent user account explicitly

Agent group: foundry_hosted. Security layer: entra_identity.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: Agent user accounts are Frontier preview. Require approved enrollment, available instance license, and supported account flow; otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide a disposable autopilot instance with its own agent user account.
* Provide Conditional Access Administrator and recorded preview approval.

.Prepare the test
. Record blueprint ID, agent identity ID, and agent user object ID separately.
. Create an explicit agent-user policy for the synthetic protected resource.

.Positive phase
. Run the supported agent-user operation with the test block disabled.
. Inspect the sign-in subject and verify the expected synthetic resource read.

.Negative phase
. Enable the policy explicitly including the agent user account.
. Start a fresh operation; verify CA denial and no completed protected read.

.Expected result
* This flow evaluates the agent user account.
* Explicit policy inclusion denies the tested operation.
* A policy targeting all users must not be assumed to include agent user accounts.

.Evidence
* Capture object relationships, explicit policy targeting, sign-ins, and resource results.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, account identity, session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require actual preview account flow and live decisions.
* Missing account support remains BLOCKED; ordinary-user tests cannot replace it.

.Measured value
* Count explicitly covered agent user accounts.
* Measure policy-review minutes and account-mapping completeness.

.Restore the test state
. Restore policy assignments and prior instance access.
. Remove the disposable instance and return its preview license when supported.

Sources: CE2, CE4, CI3.

=== ENTRA-04: Expose the API-key Conditional Access boundary

Agent group: sdk_enabled. Security layer: entra_identity.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires a disposable service supporting both approved Entra authentication and API keys. Otherwise record BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide a synthetic API endpoint and short-lived test key.
* Approve boundary testing without customer data or production credentials.

.Prepare the test
. Enable the test agent Conditional Access block for the Entra resource.
. Keep the separate test key valid; record its independent authorization scope.

.Positive phase
. Send a harmless request through the allowed API-key path.
. Verify its resource response and absence of an Entra token-issuance dependency.

.Negative phase
. Request a fresh Entra token through the blocked agent flow.
. Attempt the same resource operation; correlate CA denial separately from the successful key-based control.

.Expected result
* A valid API key can authorize the control request independently.
* CA denies the targeted Entra-token flow but cannot govern API-key token bypass.
* Key authorization is not a Conditional Access success.

.Evidence
* Capture sanitized authentication modes, CA decision, and both resource outcomes.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, identity or key alias, request correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live authentication and resource evidence.
* Never capture key values or raw tokens.

.Measured value
* Count API-key paths outside Conditional Access coverage.
* Measure key-removal planning minutes and covered Entra paths.

.Restore the test state
. Revoke the test key and restore authentication configuration.
. Restore the original Conditional Access policy state.

Sources: CE2.

=== ENTRA-05: Constrain consent to the intended resource operation

Agent group: sdk_enabled. Security layer: entra_identity.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires supported Entra consent and a disposable API with separable read/write permissions..

.Prerequisites
* Provide an authorized consent administrator and agent-sdk.
* Use a synthetic API exposing separate read and write app roles.

.Prepare the test
. Grant only the read role to agent-sdk.
. Disable write fallback and record the resource's authorization policy.

.Positive phase
. Acquire a fresh token using the granted read permission.
. Read the public record and verify the resource audit entry.

.Negative phase
. Invoke the write operation using the same least-privilege identity.
. Inspect the resource permission denial and verify the synthetic record remains unchanged.

.Expected result
* The granted read operation succeeds.
* An ungranted write operation is denied by resource authorization.
* Consent metadata alone does not prove a runtime deny.

.Evidence
* Capture granted roles, sanitized permission claims, API decisions, and before/after record state.
* Capture UTC times, authorization mode/scope, identity, request correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live identity and resource evidence.
* A local agent guardrail cannot substitute for resource permission enforcement.

.Measured value
* Count granted versus requested permissions.
* Measure least-privilege review minutes and denied write attempts.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the previous consent grants.
. Remove the disposable API record and temporary identity resources.

Sources: CE4, CI3.

=== ENTRA-06: Measure identity disablement and token persistence

Agent group: sdk_enabled. Security layer: entra_identity.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires an authorized disposable identity and protected resource. Do not promise immediate invalidation of issued tokens..

.Prerequisites
* Provide identity administrator and resource operator.
* Record supported disablement and revocation behavior for the selected authentication flow.

.Prepare the test
. Obtain one test access token securely; record only issuance and expiry metadata.
. Establish a fresh-token control and existing-token resource request.

.Positive phase
. Read the public resource using the active identity.
. Record successful fresh-token acquisition and existing-token access.

.Negative phase
. Disable the test identity; request a fresh token and verify denial after propagation.
. Retry the issued token until expiry or documented resource rejection; record each resource decision.

.Expected result
* Active identity access works before containment.
* Disabled identity cannot obtain fresh authorized access after propagation.
* Existing tokens may remain usable; record the actual residual-access interval.

.Evidence
* Capture disable action, token metadata, sign-in failures, and resource decisions without tokens.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, identity, request correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live identity and resource evidence through the agreed observation window.
* Do not declare immediate session revocation without resource-side proof.

.Measured value
* Measure disable propagation and residual-access minutes.
* Count resource paths needing additional containment.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the original identity enabled state if required.
. Revoke disposable credentials and restore previous resource permissions.

Sources: CE2, CE4.

=== ENTRA-07: Distinguish new Studio Agent IDs from legacy registrations

Agent group: copilot_studio. Security layer: entra_identity.

Priority: P1. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires new standard-harness creation and an authorized retained legacy test agent. Missing legacy comparison means BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide maker and Entra read permissions.
* Select an existing legacy agent without changing its production settings.

.Prepare the test
. Record the legacy agent's creation date and identity object type.
. Create a new disposable standard-harness Studio agent after the documented rollout.

.Positive phase
. Inspect the new agent's automatically created Agent ID.
. Match that identity to its Studio and registry records.

.Negative phase
. Inspect the legacy agent's identity rather than assuming automatic conversion.
. Compare object types and connector grants; flag any unsupported migration assumption.

.Expected result
* The new standard-harness agent receives the documented Agent ID.
* A retained legacy registration is classified from actual objects, not age-based assumptions.
* Identity creation does not prove existing connector permissions were transferred.

.Evidence
* Capture sanitized identity object types, creation times, registry mappings, and connector grants.
* Capture UTC times, configuration mode/scope, administrator identity, object correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live object evidence.
* Document any actual backfill without claiming all legacy agents were converted.

.Measured value
* Count verified Agent IDs and legacy registrations.
* Measure identity-reconciliation minutes and unmapped grants.

.Restore the test state
. Delete the new disposable agent when approved.
. Leave legacy settings unchanged and restore temporary read-role assignments.

Sources: CE1, CE4.

=== ENTRA-08: Validate offline risky-agent evidence

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: entra_identity.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: Restricted evidence-led test. Requires a real approved risky-agent event or a documented harmless fixture; otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide permitted risky-agent report access and licensed functionality.
* Do not manufacture risk through credential exposure or unapproved anomaly generation.

.Prepare the test
. Select an approved synthetic agent risk record and an unaffected control identity.
. Record event time, detection time, identity type, and risk policy.

.Positive phase
. Open the existing risky-agent record and correlate its underlying identity evidence.
. Measure the interval between observed activity and offline detection.

.Negative phase
. Inspect an OBO control session and its evaluated user identity.
. Reject claims that the agent risk record proves inline prevention or transfers OBO user risk to the agent.

* Available offline detections map to the correct agent identity.
* No deterministic inline prevention is inferred from later risk reporting.
* Missing matching evidence remains BLOCKED.

.Evidence
* Capture risk record, source event, OBO subject, and control sign-in.
* Capture UTC times, risk-policy mode/scope, identity/session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live risk and sign-in evidence.
* A generic suspicious prompt cannot substitute for a documented risk trigger.

.Measured value
* Measure detection delay and risk-to-identity correlation coverage.
* Count unsupported inline-prevention assumptions removed.

.Restore the test state
. Restore temporary risk policy settings and read permissions.
. Do not dismiss existing risk without the customer's approved workflow.

Sources: CE2, CE3.

=== DEF-01: Verify Defender Security for AI prerequisites

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: defender.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires Agent 365 entitlement, Security Administrator, and the documented Microsoft 365 connector event selections..

. Select one licensed test agent and its known synthetic interaction.

.Positive phase
. Verify connector health and required event selections in the live service.
. Find the test agent and correlate an available activity with its source event.

.Negative phase
. Inspect a separate uninstrumented test agent or deliberately disconnected disposable source.
. Search its bounded activity window; distinguish inventory metadata from unavailable security telemetry.

* Healthy prerequisites provide the documented connected evidence.
* Inventory alone does not establish runtime detection coverage.
* Absent telemetry remains a gap, not evidence that no threat occurred.

* Capture UTC times, connector mode/scope, identities, event correlation, and artifact hashes.

* Use a healthy ingestion control when interpreting missing activity.

.Measured value

* Count agents with demonstrated source connectivity.

.Restore the test state

. Remove disposable sources and temporary administrator access.

=== DEF-02: Distinguish assessed posture from missing assessment

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: defender.

Priority: P1. Required: No. Availability: Posture assessment public preview. Requires supported platform data and approved preview access; otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide a supported disposable agent and a platform with a documented unevaluated indicator.
* Select a harmless reversible configuration risk from the documented indicator list.

.Prepare the test
. Record the selected indicator's prerequisites and existing configuration.
. Keep the change limited to synthetic data and approved test users.

.Positive phase
. Apply the approved supported risk configuration and wait for assessment.
. Open the resulting indicator and trace its source configuration.

.Negative phase
. Inspect the same indicator for the unsupported or data-missing control.
. Verify Not evaluated remains distinct from No known risk; do not score it as secure.

.Expected result
* The supported indicator reflects actual assessed configuration.
* The unevaluated control does not receive an evidence-free clean assessment.

.Evidence
* Capture configuration, indicator state, assessment time, and unsupported-control explanation.
* Capture UTC times, assessment mode/scope, agent identities, finding correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live posture records and configuration proof.
* Missing rollout or safe reversible indicators means BLOCKED.

.Measured value
* Count assessed, unevaluated, and remediated indicators separately.
* Measure assessment delay and analyst review minutes.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the prior safe configuration.
. Wait for reassessment and record any remaining assessment lag.

=== DEF-03: Check published Foundry detection eligibility

Agent group: foundry_hosted. Security layer: defender.

Priority: P1. Required: No. Availability: Detection public preview. Published Foundry agent and telemetry required. Unavailable preview or safe fixture means BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide matched published and unpublished disposable Foundry agents.
* Approve a harmless detector-matching fixture for the selected documented detection.

.Prepare the test
. Instrument the published hosted agent explicitly.
. Record publication state and identical test inputs without assuming playground coverage.

.Positive phase
. Run the approved matching fixture through the published eligible agent.
. Find the actual detector record and correlate it with the published session.

.Negative phase
. Run the same harmless input in the unpublished playground control.
. Inspect eligibility and telemetry; do not assign published-agent detection coverage to the control.

.Expected result
* Supported detection evidence is attributable to the published eligible agent.
* An unpublished or playground-only agent is outside the documented Foundry detection scope.
* A model refusal is not a Defender detection.

.Evidence
* Capture publication state, instrumentation, detector records, responses, and control coverage state.
* Capture UTC times, detector mode/scope, identities, session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live product evidence and verified publication boundaries.
* No matching safe fixture or detector result means no detection acceptance.

.Measured value
* Count published agents with verified detection coverage.
* Measure detection delay and unpublished coverage gaps.

.Restore the test state
. Restore test versions and detection settings.
. Remove test publications and synthetic sessions where supported.

Sources: CD2, CD3, CI3.

=== DEF-04: Audit a supported Foundry runtime detection

Agent group: foundry_hosted. Security layer: defender.

Priority: P1. Required: No. Availability: Native real-time protection preview. Requires a customer-approved harmless matching fixture; otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide a published eligible agent, Security Administrator, and explicit SDK telemetry.
* Select a documented detection checkpoint and a safe matching fixture.

.Prepare the test
. Set the scoped runtime rule to audit.
. Use an isolated synthetic tool destination; prohibit external data transfer.
. Do not assume untrusted-instruction.txt matches a Microsoft detector.

.Positive phase
. Run a benign public request through the scoped agent.
. Verify normal completion and absence of an unexpected block.

.Negative phase
. Run the approved matching fixture at the selected checkpoint.
. Inspect BehaviorInfo and any audit-mode alert; verify whether the synthetic operation continued.

.Expected result
* Benign activity remains allowed.
* A matching audit decision records behavior without enforcing a block.
* Actual continuation is checked independently from the model's response.

.Evidence
* Capture rule, fixture approval/hash, behavior record, alert if present, and destination operation logs.
* Capture UTC times, audit mode/scope, identity, checkpoint/session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live decision and side-effect evidence.
* A refusal or local guardrail result cannot prove Defender audit detection.

.Measured value
* Measure detection delay and analyst review minutes.
* Count detected operations that still completed in audit mode.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the prior runtime rule and agent version.
. Remove synthetic destination content and test-only access.

Sources: CD2, CD3.

=== DEF-05: Block a supported Foundry runtime threat

Agent group: foundry_hosted. Security layer: defender.

Priority: P1. Required: No. Availability: Native real-time protection preview. Require approved matching fixture and explicit blocking rule; otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Complete the matching audit demonstration in DEF-04.
* Provide a disposable target operation with inspectable before/after state.

.Prepare the test
. Create a current explicit block rule for the test agent and selected checkpoint.
. Snapshot the destination state and baseline rule.

.Positive phase
. Run the benign control request under the blocking rule.
. Confirm the permitted synthetic operation completes without an unexpected block.

.Negative phase
. Run the approved matching fixture at the same checkpoint.
. Inspect the block decision and BehaviorInfo; verify the protected operation did not complete.

.Expected result
* Benign control activity remains allowed.
* The matched checkpoint is blocked and its protected side effect is prevented.
* Block mode can produce BehaviorInfo without a near-real-time alert.

.Evidence
* Capture block rule, fixture hash, behavior record, client result, and destination state.
* Capture UTC times, block mode/scope, identity, checkpoint/session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live policy decisions and side-effect evidence.
* Do not require an alert when the documented block path emits behavior evidence instead.

.Measured value
* Measure block-decision delay and prevented-operation count.
* Count benign operations preserved under the rule.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the original rule state and destination content.
. Remove test-only policies after confirming restoration.

Sources: CD3, CD4.

=== DEF-06: Test Studio external protection and classic-agent exclusion

Agent group: copilot_studio. Security layer: defender.

Priority: P1. Required: No. Availability: External runtime protection preview. Requires generative orchestration, provider connection, and safe matching fixture; otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide Power Platform administrator and Security Administrator.
* Prepare isolated generative and classic agents with harmless equivalent tools.

.Prepare the test
. Connect the supported external protection provider for the generative agent.
. Scope an explicit blocking rule to its approved tool checkpoint.
. Record classic-agent exclusion; never promise the same external inspection.

.Positive phase
. Run a harmless permitted tool request through the generative agent.
. Verify provider-enabled execution and the resulting synthetic tool output.

.Negative phase
. Run the approved matching fixture through the generative agent and verify blocked tool execution.
. Run the harmless control in the classic agent; inspect its excluded protection status separately.

.Expected result
* Supported generative tool execution is inspected under the configured path.
* Matched generative execution is blocked before its protected side effect.
* Classic agents skip external threat detection.

.Evidence
* Capture orchestration type, provider connection, rule, decisions, and both tool logs.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, identities, session/tool correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live external-protection evidence.
* Classic-agent behavior and model refusal cannot establish provider protection.

.Measured value
* Count supported and excluded Studio agents.
* Measure provider setup minutes and prevented tool operations.

.Restore the test state
. Restore provider connections and runtime rules.
. Remove disposable agents and synthetic tool outputs.

Sources: CD1, CD3, CD5.

=== DEF-07: Separate protected Work IQ tools from direct tools

Agent group: sdk_enabled. Security layer: defender.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: Work IQ MCP and runtime protection preview. Requires Microsoft 365 Copilot licensing and matching safe fixture; otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide a supported SDK integration and approved Work IQ tool.
* Provide an equivalent direct synthetic tool with isolated logs.

.Prepare the test
. Scope an explicit block rule to the documented protected tool path.
. Record which operations traverse Work IQ and which bypass that integration.

.Positive phase
. Invoke the approved Work IQ tool with a benign request.
. Verify completion and correlate its protected-path telemetry.

.Negative phase
. Submit the approved matching fixture through the protected path and verify its blocked side effect.
. Submit the harmless equivalent through the direct tool; document uncovered execution without claiming Microsoft blocking.

.Expected result
* Supported Work IQ operations receive the configured runtime policy.
* Direct unsupported tools do not gain inline protection merely through SDK registration.
* Local guardrails remain a separate control.

.Evidence
* Capture tool routing, rule, decisions, fixture approval, and both destination logs.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, identity, tool/session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live protected-path evidence and verified direct-tool boundaries.
* Missing safe detector fixture means BLOCKED.

.Measured value
* Count protected and uncovered tool paths.
* Measure integration minutes and prevented protected operations.

.Restore the test state
. Restore routing and prior policy settings.
. Revoke test tool access and remove synthetic outputs.

Sources: CM4, CD3, CI3.

=== DEF-08: Verify current blocking rules after the licensing transition

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: defender.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: Optional migration test. Requires retained legacy rule evidence, current preview access, and safe matching fixture; otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide an approved nonproduction legacy Agent 365 rule and current licensing.
* Identify whether the documented legacy Copilot Studio Cloud Apps exception applies.

.Prepare the test
. Export legacy rule metadata without assuming it enforces after July 1.
. Create a current scoped block rule using the supported policies experience.

.Positive phase
. Run a benign control under the new rule.
. Confirm the intended operation remains allowed.

.Negative phase
. Run the approved matching fixture under the current explicit block rule.
. Verify actual prevention; compare the rule ID with legacy metadata rather than trusting the old policy name.

.Expected result
* Current configuration supports the tested benign operation.
* The recreated rule supplies attributable blocking evidence.
* Legacy presence alone does not prove current enforcement.

.Evidence
* Capture legacy and current rule IDs, exception review, decision, and protected side-effect state.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, identity, session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require current live decisions.
* Do not generalize the transition behavior to the documented legacy Studio exception.

.Measured value
* Count reviewed, recreated, and unverified legacy rules.
* Measure migration minutes and blocked operations.

.Restore the test state
. Restore approved policy configuration without reintroducing an ineffective legacy dependency.
. Remove only disposable migration rules after owner approval.

Sources: CD4, CD3.

=== PUR-01: Require explicit file sharing with an Agent 365 instance

Agent group: sdk_enabled. Security layer: purview.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: Requires eligible instance, supported file path, and any applicable Frontier approval. Missing approval or access means BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide a disposable Agent 365 instance and SharePoint test site.
* Provide file owner and compliance reviewer.

.Prepare the test
. Create separate public and restricted synthetic files.
. Explicitly share the public file with the instance; do not share the restricted file.

.Positive phase
. Ask the instance to read the shared public file.
. Verify its response contains POC-APPROVAL-6142 and the file access record.

.Negative phase
. Ask the same instance to read restricted-record.txt by its exact test URL.
. Inspect access denial and confirm POC-RESTRICTED-4826 is absent from the delivered response.

.Expected result
* Explicitly shared content can be accessed through the supported path.
* Unshared content is denied; user access alone does not establish instance access.

.Evidence
* Capture explicit ACLs, instance identity, requests, responses, and access decisions.
* Capture UTC times, sharing mode/scope, identity/session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live storage and agent evidence.
* A model refusal without resource denial is insufficient.

.Measured value
* Count explicitly shared resources and denied unshared reads.
* Measure access-review minutes.

.Restore the test state
. Restore prior file sharing and remove synthetic copies.
. Remove only temporary instance permissions.

Sources: CU1, CE4.

=== PUR-02: Require VIEW and EXTRACT for encrypted source content

Agent group: sdk_enabled. Security layer: purview.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: Requires eligible Agent 365 instance, encrypted sensitivity labels, and explicit rights configuration. Otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide Information Protection administrator and a disposable instance.
* Use a supported Office document containing the restricted synthetic marker.

.Prepare the test
. Create an encrypted test label with explicit instance VIEW and EXTRACT rights.
. Create a matched control document without the required explicit instance rights.

.Positive phase
. Ask the instance to summarize the explicitly authorized encrypted document.
. Verify successful access and the label's effective rights.

.Negative phase
. Request the control document with missing EXTRACT or missing explicit instance authorization.
. Inspect rights denial and confirm the restricted marker is not returned.

.Expected result
* Explicit sharing plus VIEW and EXTRACT permits the documented access.
* Broad organizational or authenticated-user rights do not substitute for explicit instance rights.

.Evidence
* Capture label encryption settings, grants, access decisions, and response content.
* Capture UTC times, label mode/scope, instance identity, request correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live rights enforcement and delivered-content evidence.
* Do not infer rights from the human sponsor's access.

.Measured value
* Count rights combinations verified and denied extraction attempts.
* Measure encrypted-content access-review minutes.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the original label policy and rights.
. Delete test documents and remove disposable label assignments when permitted.

Sources: CU1.

=== PUR-03: Verify Agent 365 generated files do not inherit labels automatically

Agent group: sdk_enabled. Security layer: purview.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: Requires an eligible Agent 365 instance with supported file generation. Missing instance or output capability means BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide a synthetic labeled source and a dedicated output library.
* Record existing destination default and automatic labeling policies.

.Prepare the test
. Isolate the test destination from unrelated auto-labeling where authorized.
. Explicitly share the source with the instance and grant required rights.

.Positive phase
. Generate a new file from the authorized labeled source.
. Inspect the source label and independently inspect the generated file's label metadata.

.Negative phase
. Attempt to rely on source-label inheritance without applying a destination control.
. Inspect the new output again; identify missing protection or separately applied destination labels.

.Expected result
* The instance can generate authorized synthetic content.
* Source sensitivity labels do not automatically transfer to Agent 365 generated files.
* Any output label must be attributed to its actual destination or explicit labeling control.

.Evidence
* Capture source label, effective rights, output metadata, and destination policy settings.
* Capture UTC times, labeling mode/scope, identity, generation correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live file metadata.
* Do not report a destination label as inherited source protection.

.Measured value
* Count generated files needing explicit protection.
* Measure label-verification and remediation minutes.

.Restore the test state
. Restore destination labeling policies before reuse.
. Remove synthetic outputs and restore source sharing.

Sources: CU1, CU2.

=== PUR-04: Classify the synthetic restricted marker

Agent group: copilot_studio. Security layer: purview.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires Purview permissions to create and test a custom sensitive information type. Missing entitlement means BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide Information Protection administrator and synthetic fixture files.

. Create a disposable custom sensitive information type matching POC-RESTRICTED-4826 exactly.
. Use the documented Purview test facility; record pattern and confidence configuration.

.Positive phase
. Submit restricted-record.txt to the live Purview sensitive-information test.
. Record its match count and classified marker without using real sensitive data.

.Negative phase
. Submit public-procedure.txt and untrusted-instruction.txt to the same test.
. Verify neither file matches the restricted marker classifier.

.Expected result
* The restricted fixture matches the configured synthetic classifier.
* Control fixtures do not match.
* Classification testing alone does not prove agent grounding or DLP enforcement.

.Evidence
* Capture classifier definition, fixture hashes, live test inputs, and match results.
* Capture UTC times, classification mode/scope, operator identity, test correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require actual Purview classification results.
* Local regex matching cannot substitute for the product classifier.

.Measured value
* Count expected and unexpected classification matches.
* Measure classifier setup and validation minutes.

.Restore the test state
. Remove the unused disposable classifier after dependent tests.
. Restore any test policy references before deletion.

Sources: CU1, CU2.

=== PUR-05: Check Studio Word and PowerPoint label inheritance

Agent group: copilot_studio. Security layer: purview.

Priority: P1. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires the documented Studio output capability, supported labeled sources, and Microsoft channel. Otherwise BLOCKED..

.Negative phase

* Do not extend this behavior to Agent 365 instance-generated files.

.Evidence

.Pass criteria

.Measured value

.Restore the test state

=== PUR-06: Restrict Studio SharePoint grounding with label-based DLP

Agent group: copilot_studio. Security layer: purview.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires supported SharePoint grounding, Microsoft channel, and scoped Purview DLP enforcement. Missing support means BLOCKED..

* Unrestricted SharePoint grounding remains usable.

=== PUR-11: Validate retention on supported Studio interactions

Agent group: copilot_studio. Security layer: purview.

Priority: P1. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires supported retention and a supported user-deletion action. Missing capability or unavailable processing verification means BLOCKED..

* Provide retention administrator and an isolated supported Studio interaction location.
* Approve a retention window and wait period before destructive test actions.

. Select Microsoft Copilot Experiences in the retention policy; include only the approved test user.
. Record preservation behavior, effective policy, and any overlapping holds.

. Create a supported synthetic interaction and confirm policy application.
. Use eDiscovery on the test user's mailbox to verify the retained interaction.

.Negative phase
. Delete the user-visible synthetic interaction through its supported interface; stop if that action is unavailable.

* The selected supported location receives the retention policy.

* Capture UTC times, retention mode/scope, user/agent identity, correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live preservation evidence after processing.
* Pending processing remains NOT_RUN or BLOCKED with a reason, never assumed success.

.Measured value
* Measure policy propagation and retrieval minutes.
* Count preserved supported interactions and unsupported locations.

.Restore the test state
. Restore prior retention settings only with records-owner approval.
. Document retained test items until their authorized disposition date.
