Sources: CP2, CP3, CP4.

=== MDE-03: Block the official Claude Code runtime demonstration

Agent group: shadow_local. Security layer: defender_endpoint.

Priority: P1. Required: No. Availability: Windows native runtime protection preview. Requires supported Claude Code and official demo prerequisites; otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide an isolated onboarded Windows device and supported Claude Code version.
* Use CP4's official benign demonstration and documented setup.

.Prepare the test
. Set scoped native runtime protection to block.
. Start a new Claude Code session after settings propagate.
. Record the demonstration's expected protected action without adding a harmful payload.

.Positive phase
. Run a harmless public-procedure task.
. Verify normal output and continued application usability.

.Negative phase
. Execute the official matching demonstration.
. Inspect the Defender block message, protection history, and absence of the blocked demonstration action.

.Expected result
* The benign task remains allowed.
* Native runtime inspection blocks the matched threat checkpoint.
* Threat blocking does not uninstall or universally disable Claude Code.

.Evidence
* Capture versions, block settings, demo revision, protection history, device evidence, and action outcome.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, user/device identity, session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live runtime enforcement and actual side-effect evidence.
* Toast absence alone is not failure when notification settings suppress it.

.Measured value
* Count prevented demonstration actions and permitted benign tasks.
* Measure enforcement and investigation minutes.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the previous runtime policy and restart the test session.
. Remove demo artifacts and confirm benign execution still works.

Sources: CP2, CP3, CP4.

=== MDE-04: Separate native runtime protection from Frontier whole-app blocking

Agent group: shadow_local. Security layer: defender_endpoint.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: Optional Frontier UI boundary plus runtime preview evidence. Missing either authorized surface means BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide approved Frontier access and supported local Codex CLI or Claude Code inventory.
* Retain correlated runtime evidence from MDE-02 or MDE-03.

.Prepare the test
. Record exact tool type; distinguish CLI, desktop, and VS Code extension records.
. Do not apply extension policies to native CLI records.

.Positive phase
. Open the discovered native local-agent record in the Frontier experience.
. Compare its discovery evidence with the separate MDE runtime policy and actual runtime decision.

.Negative phase
. Inspect the available whole-app administrative actions for Codex CLI or Claude Code.
. Confirm the supported UI lacks that blocking capability; reject an inference that runtime blocking implies whole-app disablement.

.Expected result
* Native CLI discovery and native runtime threat protection remain separate capabilities.
* The documented native Codex and Claude Code admin surfaces do not provide whole-app blocking.
* Supported VS Code extension controls are a different scope.

.Evidence
* Capture exact agent type, UI actions, version, and correlated runtime-control evidence.
* Capture UTC times, UI/runtime mode and scope, identities, record correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live capability evidence.
* Do not report unavailable whole-app controls as tested enforcement.

.Measured value
* Count correctly classified enforcement surfaces.
* Measure capability-review minutes and unsupported-action assumptions removed.

.Restore the test state
. Restore view filters and temporary Frontier assignments.
. Leave endpoint policies at their previously approved baseline.

Sources: CP1, CP2, CP3.

=== MDE-05: Validate OpenClaw supported network inspection

Agent group: shadow_local. Security layer: defender_endpoint.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: Endpoint runtime preview. Requires supported inspectable network flow and approved harmless matching fixture; otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide isolated Windows device, supported OpenClaw version, and controlled synthetic endpoint.
* Approve a safe fixture matched to the network detector; CP4 native-hook demo is not assumed compatible.

.Prepare the test
. Enable the documented network inspection and scoped block settings.
. Record transport, TLS inspection eligibility, and lack of certificate pinning or HTTP/3 on the tested path.

.Positive phase
. Send a benign synthetic request over the supported inspected flow.
. Verify service completion and network inspection context.

.Negative phase
. Send the approved matching fixture over the same supported flow.
. Inspect the block and destination logs; separately record pinned or HTTP/3 paths as unsupported coverage.

.Expected result
* Supported benign traffic remains usable.
* A matched inspected request is blocked before its protected service action.
* Certificate-pinned and HTTP/3 traffic cannot receive this coverage claim.

.Evidence
* Capture network prerequisites, fixture approval/hash, runtime decision, and destination request logs.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, device/user identity, connection/session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live network enforcement and destination evidence.
* No documented or customer-approved matching fixture means BLOCKED without a detection result.

.Measured value
* Count inspected and unsupported transport paths.
* Measure block latency and prevented service operations.

.Restore the test state
. Restore previous inspection and runtime policy settings.
. Remove synthetic provider sessions and disposable endpoint data.

Sources: CP2, CP3, CP4.

=== MDE-06: Measure OpenClaw whole-app blocking collateral scope

Agent group: shadow_local. Security layer: defender_endpoint.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: Frontier and Intune restricted test. Disposable tenant only; all managed Windows devices may be affected. Otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Use a disposable tenant containing only approved test Windows devices.
* Obtain explicit approval for shared Node.js blocking and possible tenant-wide propagation.

.Prepare the test
. Snapshot Intune policies, device enrollment, and existing shared-runtime controls.
. Prepare OpenClaw and a harmless unrelated Node.js control process on test devices.

.Positive phase
. Run a harmless OpenClaw task and the unrelated Node.js control.
. Record successful execution on every enrolled test device.

.Negative phase
. Apply the documented Shadow AI Block policy and inspect its actual Intune assignment scope.
. After propagation, retry both applications on all test devices and record shared-runtime collateral blocking.

.Expected result
* Whole-app control can block OpenClaw execution.
* Shared Node.js blocking can also affect unrelated Node.js applications.
* This is not selective prompt-content blocking; propagation may take 15 minutes to eight hours.

.Evidence
* Capture approved tenant isolation, policy assignments, device status, and both application outcomes.
* Capture UTC times, block mode/scope, device/user identities, execution correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live Intune and process evidence across the actual assignment scope.
* Any production enrollment or unapproved shared-runtime impact means BLOCKED before policy application.

.Measured value
* Measure propagation and recovery minutes.
* Count affected devices, intended blocks, and collateral application blocks.

.Restore the test state
. Restore prior shared-runtime and Intune policies.
. Verify both applications recover on every test device before removing the disposable tenant.

Sources: CP1, CP2.

=== MDCA-01: Block an unsanctioned AI domain for a device group

Agent group: shadow_local. Security layer: defender_cloud_apps.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires Cloud Apps and Endpoint integration, supported Network Protection, and limited device-group enforcement..

.Prerequisites
* Provide Cloud Apps administrator and two disposable onboarded devices.
* Select an approved test AI service domain without production dependencies.

.Prepare the test
. Include only the test device group; retain one excluded control device.
. Snapshot app sanction state, network protection, and generated domain indicators.

.Positive phase
. Open the service from both devices before blocking.
. Record successful domain access using harmless public requests.

.Negative phase
. Mark the test service unsanctioned for the approved scope and allow documented propagation.
. Retry from both devices; verify included-device denial and excluded-device access.

.Expected result
* Domain enforcement blocks the included device after propagation, which can take up to three hours.
* The excluded device remains unaffected.
* This proves domain access control, not inspection of prompt content.

.Evidence
* Capture sanction state, scope, indicators, device policy, and both access outcomes.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, device/user identity, request correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live domain and endpoint evidence.
* Do not label a domain block as a prompt-injection detection.

.Measured value
* Measure policy propagation and recovery minutes.
* Count affected versus excluded devices and blocked domains.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the prior sanction state and indicator policy.
. Verify domain access returns only where it existed before testing.

Sources: CA1, CA2.

=== MDCA-02: Review OAuth app governance without inventing agent telemetry

Agent group: partner_integrated. Security layer: defender_cloud_apps.

Priority: P1. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires licensed app governance, supported OAuth application, and authorized test consent. Missing support means BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide app governance reader, consent administrator, and an integrated test OAuth application.
* Use a synthetic provider resource with reversible permission grants.

.Prepare the test
. Record the OAuth app, service principal, approved grants, and partner agent mapping.
. Enable the supported governance connection without altering unrelated policies.

.Positive phase
. Perform an allowed synthetic read through the consented application.
. Inspect its app-governance permissions and available activity evidence.

.Negative phase
. Revoke only the disposable read grant and start a fresh provider authorization attempt.
. Verify access denial and unchanged resource state; compare governance update timing.

.Expected result
* Governance provides supported OAuth permission and activity visibility.
* Grant removal constrains the selected resource path after propagation.
* OAuth app evidence is not automatically a complete agent execution trace.

.Evidence
* Capture grants, governance records, revocation, fresh authorization decision, and resource outcomes.
* Capture UTC times, governance mode/scope, app/user identity, request correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live identity, governance, and resource evidence.
* Distinguish consent enforcement from app-governance detection.

.Measured value
* Count reviewed grants and mapped OAuth applications.
* Measure permission-removal propagation and investigation minutes.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the original approved grants and provider connection.
. Remove synthetic resources and temporary consent permissions.

Sources: CA3, CI2, CD4.

=== DFC-01: Separate cloud AI infrastructure from agent-level coverage

Agent group: foundry_hosted. Security layer: defender_cloud.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires Defender CSPM or applicable AI service plan and read access to separate Agent 365 entitlement state..

.Prerequisites
* Provide cloud security reader and a disposable Foundry account/project.
* Record the current Agent 365 and Defender plan assignments without removing production licenses.

.Prepare the test
. Enable the approved cloud plan for the disposable resource.
. Create a resource-only control without a published agent; keep agent identity evidence separate.

.Positive phase
. Locate the Foundry account/project in cloud AI posture inventory.
. Correlate resource IDs and applicable infrastructure recommendations.

.Negative phase
. Inspect the resource-only control for an actual published agent identity and agent telemetry.
. Verify infrastructure discovery is not counted as agent-level protection or multicloud content inspection.

.Expected result
* Cloud plans discover supported accounts, projects, and model infrastructure.
* Agent-level entitlement and integration remain separate after the July 1 transition.
* A model resource is not an agent identity.

.Evidence
* Capture plan assignments, resource inventory, recommendations, and separate agent entitlement/identity review.
* Capture UTC times, plan mode/scope, resource and operator identities, correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live inventory evidence.
* Do not infer agent preview coverage from account-level discovery.

.Measured value
* Count infrastructure resources and verified agent identities separately.
* Measure inventory reconciliation minutes and coverage gaps.

.Restore the test state
. Restore original cloud plan settings for the test scope.
. Remove disposable resources without altering shared security connectors.

Sources: CC1, CC2, CD4.

=== DFC-02: Verify model detection with prompt evidence disabled

Agent group: foundry_hosted. Security layer: defender_cloud.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: Requires supported model threat protection and an approved harmless matching fixture. Without an actual safe trigger, record BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide cloud security administrator and a disposable supported inference endpoint.
* Approve synthetic prompt evidence collection for one controlled phase.

.Prepare the test
. Enable the supported AI services threat plan.
. Record prompt-evidence settings independently from agent-level protection.

.Positive phase
. Enable prompt evidence and submit the approved harmless matching fixture.
. Inspect the model detection and permitted synthetic prompt details.

.Negative phase
. Disable prompt evidence and repeat the approved matching fixture in a fresh request.
. Verify detection remains available while prompt content is masked or omitted.

.Expected result
* Supported model threat detection produces attributable records.
* Disabling suspicious prompt evidence changes content visibility, not the documented detection capability.
* This is model-service coverage, not proof of agent-level instrumentation.

.Evidence
* Capture plan and evidence settings, fixture approval, detections, and redacted detail comparison.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, endpoint/caller identity, request correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require actual live model detections.
* No matching detection means no claim that content-setting behavior was verified.

.Measured value
* Count detections retained without prompt-body disclosure.
* Measure investigation minutes and exposed evidence-field count.

.Restore the test state
. Restore previous prompt-evidence and plan settings.
. Remove synthetic endpoint sessions according to the approved retention process.

Sources: CC2, CD4, CR1.

=== GSA-01: Apply routed Studio outbound access policy

Agent group: copilot_studio. Security layer: global_secure_access.

Priority: P1. Required: No. Availability: Restricted environment integration. Requires current supported rollout, Entra Internet Access, and approved forwarding; otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide Power Platform and Global Secure Access administrators.
* Use a disposable tenant, Studio environment, and controlled public synthetic HTTP endpoints.
* Approve baseline security-profile effects at tenant scope before configuring the gateway.

.Prepare the test
. Enable documented environment traffic forwarding for the supported HTTP, custom connector, or MCP path.
. Scope a test web policy to allow one synthetic endpoint and deny another.

.Positive phase
. Invoke the allowed endpoint through the selected routed Studio action.
. Verify response completion and correlated gateway traffic.

.Negative phase
. Invoke the denied endpoint through the same routed action type.
. Inspect the gateway policy decision and verify no completed destination operation.

.Expected result
* Routed allowed traffic completes.
* The scoped web policy denies matched routed traffic.
* Unrouted connectors and arbitrary agent traffic do not inherit this coverage.

.Evidence
* Capture forwarding, routing path, policy, gateway logs, and destination request logs.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, user/agent identity, connection correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live gateway and destination evidence.
* A connector error without a policy decision is insufficient.

.Measured value
* Count controlled and unrouted outbound paths.
* Measure forwarding setup and policy propagation minutes.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the previous environment forwarding and gateway policy.
. Remove synthetic endpoints and temporary connector permissions.

Sources: CN1.

=== GSA-02: Block a matching routed JSON prompt

Agent group: shadow_local. Security layer: global_secure_access.

Priority: P1. Required: No. Availability: Requires available prompt-injection protection, routed TLS-inspected traffic, supported schema, and safe matching fixture. Otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide Global Secure Access administrator and an assigned test endpoint/user.
* Approve a harmless detector-matching fixture for the selected supported AI service.

.Prepare the test
. Configure documented routing, TLS inspection, conversation schema, and a scoped blocking policy.
. Keep prompts within the documented text limit; do not assume generic untrusted instructions trigger detection.

.Positive phase
. Send a benign JSON text prompt through the routed supported service.
. Verify service response and the matched gateway inspection context.

.Negative phase
. Send the approved matching JSON prompt through the same route.
. Inspect the gateway block and destination evidence that the protected model request did not complete.

.Expected result
* Supported benign requests are allowed.
* A matching inspected request is blocked before the target model operation.
* A model-only refusal is not a gateway block.

.Evidence
* Capture routing/TLS/schema configuration, fixture approval, policy decision, and destination outcome.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, user/device identity, request correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live gateway enforcement and verified routing.
* Missing matching fixture or destination evidence remains BLOCKED or incomplete.

.Measured value
* Measure gateway-decision delay and prevented model requests.
* Count routed services with verified inspection.

.Restore the test state
. Restore prior routing, TLS inspection, and policy settings.
. Remove synthetic service conversations and test assignments.

Sources: CN2.

=== GSA-03: Measure prompt-inspection schema and size boundaries

Agent group: shadow_local. Security layer: global_secure_access.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: Requires supported gateway inspection and approved boundary fixtures. Missing inspection metadata means BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide a controlled supported service and Global Secure Access administrator.
* Use harmless text and file payloads only; do not encode harmful instructions.

.Prepare the test
. Record the matched JSON conversation schema and effective routing.
. Prepare a short JSON control, a file-only payload, and text exceeding 64,000 characters.

.Positive phase
. Send the short JSON control through the supported inspected path.
. Inspect available gateway fields showing the matched request and schema.

.Negative phase
. Submit the approved file-only and oversized synthetic controls.
. Inspect available processing evidence; record files as unsupported and text beyond the limit as outside inspected coverage.

.Expected result
* The supported JSON text path is eligible for inspection.
* Files are not covered by the documented prompt-inspection promise.
* Text beyond 64,000 characters is truncated; do not claim end-to-end inspection.

.Evidence
* Capture payload type/length, routing/schema settings, gateway records, and resulting service actions.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, user/device identity, request correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live processing evidence with explicit coverage limits.
* An allowed response alone cannot prove which characters were inspected.

.Measured value
* Count supported and excluded payload types.
* Measure inspection eligibility coverage and boundary-review minutes.

.Restore the test state
. Restore prior schema and routing settings.
. Delete synthetic uploads and oversized conversations where supported.

Sources: CN2.

=== SENT-01: Correlate Agent 365 telemetry in the Sentinel data lake

Agent group: sdk_enabled. Security layer: sentinel.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires Sentinel data lake, connector permissions, Agent 365 telemetry, and approved consumption. Otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide Sentinel administrator and an instrumented synthetic SDK agent.
* Confirm the Agent 365 solution and connector versions are supported.

.Prepare the test
. Enable the Agent 365 telemetry connector.
. Inspect the actual UnifiedAgentObservability dataset metadata and available schema in the tenant.

.Positive phase
. Run one uniquely correlated synthetic agent session.
. Find its ingested telemetry using the live lake interface and discovered schema.

.Negative phase
. Search an intentionally nonexistent session marker in the same bounded interval.
. Verify unrelated records are not counted as matching telemetry or as a Log Analytics table.

.Expected result
* Supported telemetry reaches the Sentinel data lake.
* The absent control marker produces no attributable target session.
* UnifiedAgentObservability is a dataset label, not an invented Log Analytics KQL schema.

.Evidence
* Capture connector health/version, observed schema, source trace, search definition, and actual results.
* Capture UTC times, connector mode/scope, identities, session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live lake evidence and source correlation.
* Use only the tenant's observed schema; do not substitute CloudAppEvents.

.Measured value
* Measure telemetry delivery and search minutes.
* Count source sessions correlated into the lake.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the prior connector state if test-only.
. Remove temporary access and retain evidence under the agreed data policy.

Sources: CS1, CI4.

=== SENT-02: Import agent identities through the separate preview connector

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: sentinel.

Priority: P2. Required: No. Availability: Microsoft Agent Identities connector preview. Requires authorized preview access and supported solution version; otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide Sentinel and Entra administrators with approved connector permissions.
* Select disposable agent identity and blueprint objects.

.Prepare the test
. Configure Microsoft Agent Identities separately from the Agent 365 telemetry connector.
. Inspect the current connector's four documented identity dataset labels and tenant schema.

.Positive phase
. Import the selected identity and blueprint assets.
. Match dataset identifiers to their live Entra source objects.

.Negative phase
. Search for a nonexistent blueprint ID within the same import scope.
. Verify imported identity assets are not presented as session transcripts or XDR incidents.

.Expected result
* The identity connector supplies supported asset datasets.
* A nonexistent identity has no attributable imported asset.
* Identity import does not prove runtime telemetry collection.

.Evidence
* Capture connector/version, dataset metadata, source objects, import results, and negative search.
* Capture UTC times, connector mode/scope, operator/object identities, correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require actual imported assets and source-object comparison.
* Missing preview or incompatible solution version remains BLOCKED.

.Measured value
* Count imported identities and blueprints matched to Entra.
* Measure synchronization delay and asset-reconciliation minutes.

.Restore the test state
. Restore the previous preview connector configuration.
. Remove temporary grants and disposable identity assets when approved.

Sources: CS2, CE4.

=== SENT-03: Synchronize a Defender XDR incident separately

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: sentinel.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires Defender XDR connector permissions and an approved actual synthetic incident. No suitable incident means BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide Sentinel administrator and Defender incident analyst.
* Use an existing approved test incident or an official supported benign simulation.

.Prepare the test
. Enable XDR incident and alert synchronization for the test workspace.
. Record selected raw event settings separately; prevent duplicate legacy incident connectors where documented.

.Positive phase
. Locate the actual Defender test incident and its alerts.
. Verify its synchronized Sentinel incident identifiers and entities.

.Negative phase
. Inspect an unrelated lake telemetry session without an XDR incident.
. Verify it is not counted as an incident or proof that every hunting table was ingested.

.Expected result
* The selected XDR incident is synchronized through its own connector.
* Raw-event selections and lake telemetry do not automatically create equivalent incidents.

.Evidence
* Capture connector selections, source incident, destination incident, and telemetry-only control.
* Capture UTC times, connector mode/scope, analyst/entity identities, incident correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live incident and control evidence.
* Do not invent alerts to satisfy the test.

.Measured value
* Measure incident synchronization and triage minutes.
* Count matched alerts/entities and duplicate incidents.

.Restore the test state
. Restore prior connector selections and test incident status.
. Remove only temporary analyst permissions.

Sources: CS3, CS1.

=== SENT-04: Verify independent Sentinel connector behavior

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: sentinel.

Priority: P1. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires an isolated test workspace with Agent 365 lake and XDR connectors. Preview identities are not required..

.Prerequisites
* Provide Sentinel administrator and explicit approval for a test-only connector interruption.
* Keep production workspaces and connectors outside the test scope.

.Prepare the test
. Snapshot both connector states and collection windows.
. Prepare a synthetic agent session and an approved XDR incident update.

.Positive phase
. Generate both signals while both connectors are enabled.
. Verify separate lake telemetry and XDR incident delivery.

.Negative phase
. Disable only the test Agent 365 telemetry connector and generate new uniquely marked signals.
. Verify bounded lake delivery stops while XDR updates continue; distinguish queued records by generation time.

.Expected result
* Each enabled connector supplies its own data path.
* Changing the lake connector does not disable XDR synchronization.
* Old or queued data does not prove continued new ingestion.

.Evidence
* Capture before/after connector states, source times, ingestion searches, and XDR updates.
* Capture UTC times, connector mode/scope, identities, independent correlation IDs, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live evidence from both independent paths.
* Shared production connector changes are prohibited; unavailable isolation means BLOCKED.

.Measured value
* Measure connector interruption and recovery minutes.
* Count independently verified data paths and missed test records.

.Restore the test state
. Restore both connectors to their original states.
. Verify fresh telemetry resumes and document any unbackfilled interval.

Sources: CS1, CS2, CS3.

=== COP-01: Verify Security Copilot incident-summary fidelity

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: security_copilot.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires enabled Security Copilot, authorized Defender data, and available capacity. Phased entitlement absence means BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide authorized security analyst and an actual approved PoC incident.
* Record capacity entitlement and permissions; do not assume E5/E7 enablement is complete.

.Prepare the test
. Export the incident's actual alerts, entities, and timeline as the comparison source.
. Use the supported incident-summary capability, not an unavailable preview chat surface.

.Positive phase
. Request a summary of the actual selected incident.
. Compare every named entity, alert, and time with the source records.

.Negative phase
. Ask whether an absent synthetic tool action occurred in that incident.
. Verify the answer preserves missing evidence rather than inventing a tool execution.

.Expected result
* The summary reflects available authorized records.
* Unsupported actions remain unknown or unsupported by evidence.
* Security Copilot is not an independent agent sensor.

.Evidence
* Capture source records, prompts, responses, analyst fact checks, and capacity usage.
* Capture UTC times, access mode/scope, analyst identity, incident/session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live Copilot responses and checked source evidence.
* Any unsupported factual assertion must be corrected before acceptance.

.Measured value
* Measure analyst verification and summary-production minutes.
* Count supported claims, unsupported claims, and consumed SCUs.

.Restore the test state
. Restore temporary analyst permissions and incident state.
. Handle saved Copilot sessions under the approved retention process.

Sources: CK1, CK2.

=== COP-02: Validate generated hunting queries against real schemas

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: security_copilot.

Priority: P1. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires supported Copilot hunting assistance, authorized data, and capacity. Otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide an analyst with query permissions and actual test telemetry.
* Use documented available Defender tables or the tenant's observed schema.

.Prepare the test
. Record available tables and a known synthetic event ID.
. Set a bounded time range and approve read-only query execution.

.Positive phase
. Ask Copilot for a query locating the known event.
. Review fields and filters; execute the approved query and verify the expected event.

.Negative phase
. Ask for evidence from an unavailable synthetic table or undocumented lake schema.
. Reject invented tables and fields; verify Copilot identifies missing schema or requires analyst correction.

.Expected result
* The reviewed valid query runs against actual available data.
* Missing schema does not become fabricated query evidence.
* Generated KQL is assistance, not proof of detection.

.Evidence
* Capture schema inventory, generated and corrected queries, errors, results, and capacity usage.
* Capture UTC times, query mode/scope, analyst identity, session/event correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require actual live generation and execution evidence.
* Never accept a query solely because its syntax looks plausible.

.Measured value
* Measure query drafting, correction, and execution minutes.
* Count invalid fields, verified results, and consumed SCUs.

.Restore the test state
. Remove temporary saved queries and test-only permissions.
. Retain sanitized query evidence without storing customer payloads.

Sources: CK2, CS1, CD2.

=== COP-03: Preserve missing-data and authorization boundaries in Copilot

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: security_copilot.

Priority: P1. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires Security Copilot access and two approved analyst permission scopes. Otherwise BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide authorized analyst and restricted analyst test accounts.
* Use an actual synthetic incident accessible only to the authorized analyst.

.Prepare the test
. Record underlying product permissions and Copilot access for both accounts.
. Keep prompts limited to the synthetic incident ID and approved metadata.

.Positive phase
. Request the incident facts as the authorized analyst.
. Verify each returned fact against the accessible source records.

.Negative phase
. Request the same protected incident details as the restricted analyst.
. Inspect the response and source access result; verify protected details are not disclosed or invented.

.Expected result
* Authorized source data supports the permitted answer.
* Restricted source access remains restricted or explicitly unavailable.
* Copilot access does not grant independent access to all Defender or Purview data.

.Evidence
* Capture role assignments, underlying access checks, both Copilot responses, and source comparisons.
* Capture UTC times, access mode/scope, analyst identity, session/incident correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live responses and underlying authorization evidence.
* Sanitized output must preserve enough detail to verify non-disclosure.

.Measured value
* Count protected facts withheld and unsupported claims.
* Measure access-review and evidence-verification minutes.

.Restore the test state
. Restore previous analyst roles and Copilot assignments.
. Remove test-only sessions according to retention requirements.

Sources: CK1, CK2, CM2.

=== RSP-01: Contain each verified agent execution path

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: response.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires approved test-only response authority across M365, Entra, and the selected runtime..

.Prerequisites
* Provide incident lead, identity administrator, and runtime owner.
* Select a disposable agent with mapped channel, direct endpoint, and credential paths.

.Prepare the test
. Record original availability, identity state, compute state, and active credential types.
. Choose containment actions for each path; exclude production dependencies.

.Positive phase
. Run a benign transaction on every authorized mapped path.
. Verify expected synthetic destination state and end-to-end correlation.

.Negative phase
. Apply approved channel block, identity disablement, and runtime stop where supported.
. Retry every path, including issued-token and API-key controls; inspect remaining execution and resource side effects.

.Expected result
* Baseline authorized paths operate before containment.
* Each selected containment action affects only its documented scope.
* Residual token or key access requires separate resource or runtime containment, not an unsupported success claim.

.Evidence
* Capture action approvals, before/after states, sign-in decisions, endpoint logs, and destination state.
* Capture UTC times, action mode/scope, operator/caller identities, incident/session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live evidence for every agreed execution path.
* Uncontained mapped paths remain explicit failures or blocked work, not silently excluded.

.Measured value
* Measure detection-to-action and action-to-containment minutes.
* Count contained paths and residual access paths.

.Restore the test state
. Preserve evidence before restoring original approved settings.
. Revoke disposable credentials and remove test-only resources after owner approval.

Sources: CM3, CE2, CE4, CD3.

=== RSP-02: Restore service without restoring prohibited access

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: response.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires completed authorized containment and captured configuration baseline..

.Prerequisites
* Provide incident lead and owners of all changed controls.
* Keep the restricted synthetic record and its original deny policy available.

.Prepare the test
. Compare current settings with the approved pre-test baseline.
. Identify changes that must remain, including original least-privilege restrictions.

.Positive phase
. Restore approved compute, identity, channel, and policy settings in the documented order.
. Run the original authorized public operation and verify end-to-end service recovery.

.Negative phase
. Retry the originally prohibited restricted-record operation as u-deny.
. Verify its original denial still holds and no broad permission grant was introduced during recovery.

.Expected result
* Authorized service resumes under the approved baseline.
* Previously prohibited access remains prohibited.
* Rollback does not mean disabling every security control.

.Evidence
* Capture baseline/current configuration diff, approvals, allowed transaction, and prohibited-access decision.
* Capture UTC times, restored mode/scope, identities, incident/session correlation, and artifact hashes.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require live recovery and negative-access evidence.
* All test changes need an owner-confirmed disposition.

.Measured value
* Measure restoration minutes and remaining configuration differences.
* Count recovered paths and preserved deny boundaries.

.Restore the test state
. Remove temporary response roles and test assignments.
. Archive sanitized evidence; remove synthetic resources only after restoration verification.

Sources: CM3, CE2, CE4, CU1.

=== RSP-03: Reconstruct an incident without confusing configuration and execution

Agent group: cross_platform. Security layer: response.

Priority: P0. Required: Yes. Availability: Requires real synthetic live-product records from at least two enabled control layers. Missing correlation means BLOCKED..

.Prerequisites
* Provide incident analyst and access to the selected source records.
* Use synthetic events already generated by approved live cases; do not invent detections.

.Prepare the test
. Select one permitted operation and one actually denied operation.
. Record source retention windows and approved evidence storage location outside the source repository.

.Positive phase
. Correlate the permitted operation across identity, runtime, and destination records where available.
. Build a timestamped timeline showing the actual completed side effect.

.Negative phase
. Correlate the denied operation with its policy decision and unchanged destination state.
. Remove one correlation field from an analysis copy; verify the analyst marks the resulting link unproven.

.Expected result
* The timeline separates configuration, decision, execution, and side effect.
* Missing correlation remains an explicit uncertainty.
* Registry synchronization, model refusal, and local guardrails do not become Microsoft enforcement evidence.

.Evidence
* Capture original source records, policy snapshots, destination state, timeline, and uncertainty annotation.
* Capture UTC times, policy mode/scope, identity/session correlation, and hashes for both phase artifacts.

.Pass criteria
* Both phases require hashed live-product artifacts and independent source correlation.
* Keep unexecuted required cases NOT_RUN; evidence hashes verify integrity, not the truth of a product claim.

.Measured value
* Measure timeline reconstruction and analyst validation minutes.
* Count correlated events, unproven links, and missing evidence fields.

.Restore the test state
. Restore temporary analyst access and analysis filters.
. Retain original evidence under the approved policy; remove disposable analysis copies when no longer needed.

Sources: CD2, CD3, CI4, CS1, CS3, CR1.
